# Kernel implementation and evidence

## Authority, boundary and current claims

The [publication checkpoint](#publication-checkpoint) was published on 2026-10-19; the user then asked to continue, and that round is recorded in the [v0.1.0 checkpoint](#v010-continuation-checkpoint) (first-userspace slice [A18](decisions.md), roadmap A19, branch-and-PR flow A20); the next request ([A21](decisions.md), finalize and QEMU-test the port and provide a bare-metal suite) is partially delivered and recorded in the [v0.2.0 checkpoint](#v020-checkpoint). These are bounded task checkpoints, not a reduced definition of the whole port: Linux6.18.57 commit `2ff600d29ee478d0a8d46ef8a58afb5e038fe692`, every architecture/driver/protocol/filesystem/facility and all qualification obligations remain mandatory and unfinished. Existing planning inventories retain their structural meaning.

The first implementation cell is x86_64, little endian, 4 KiB pages, four-level paging, one executing bootstrap CPU, QEMU q35/TCG, UEFI OVMF and external Limine 12.9.3. Since v0.1.0 one static `/init` also runs in ring 3 under a declared configuration, and since v0.2.0 it can fork children on a single-CPU FIFO scheduler with a tmpfs rootfs and the syscalls exercised by the nine oracle groups ([v0.2.0 checkpoint](#v020-checkpoint)); no durable filesystem, networking, SMP or other-architecture claim follows. Physical execution, independent architecture/unsafe/security review and all reference performance gates remain mandatory and open. No host installation, boot-entry change, reboot, firmware change or raw-device operation is authorized.

**Current physical authority:** diagnostic-001/002 remain permanently withdrawn after the confirmed emitted error-code ABI defect. Actual007 supplies its missing-path failing-before witness; corrected008 and the independently reviewed minimal repair underpin new diagnostic-003. Director accepted the actual archive/metadata and software-consumer records and conditionally cleared **only its exact original lineage** under [unchanged separate-media/recovery requirements](diagnostic-handoff.md). Old artifacts/HOLD records are untouched; no physical evidence, later-source clearance or full Linux qualification is claimed.

## v0.2.0 checkpoint

**2026-10-21, `main` 0eb3b1d (v0.2.0).** Observed by the integrator and by one reproducibility run on the canonical checkout `/home/bigsmarty/Projects/linux-rs`; all `target/` paths are retained local ignored evidence, not Git content. This records what [A21](decisions.md) (2026-10-20, "finalize the port as far as possible, test it in QEMU, and provide a bare-metal test suite") has delivered so far, as pull requests #12-#27. It is a bounded software checkpoint: **A21 is partially delivered, not completed** (the port is not finished), and it is **not** M1 exit, Linux compatibility, physical qualification or performance evidence. The [v0.1.0 checkpoint](#v010-continuation-checkpoint) and the [publication checkpoint](#publication-checkpoint) stay below as history. The `v0.2.0` tag does not exist until the integrator creates it at the merge of this record (`git tag -l` showed only `v0.1.0` when it was written); the executable sources are identical to 0eb3b1d, where the evidence below ran.

### Final evidence (main 0eb3b1d)

**Oracle groups.** Each group is a `user/init` mode whose output was recorded on the pinned Linux 6.18.57 and is required to equal the kernel's output one line for one line, in order (`xtask/oracle/expected.tsv`; [oracle suite](#oracle-syscall-test-suite)). On `main` each runs as a `kernel-test` `oracle-<group>` case and as a group of the kernel-run suite. The first column is the number of expected lines (checks, the summary row and info rows) that matched; the second is the `init: tests <group> passed=<n> failed=0` count printed by the kernel (read from `target/kernel-tooling/main-v020/`). The match is limited to the cases these `/init` test programs run on the pinned Linux; it is not a statement about Linux behaviour in general, and calls outside the groups (for example `kill(0)`/`kill(-1)`, `ITIMER_VIRTUAL`/`ITIMER_PROF`, several `mmap` cases) stop the kernel loudly instead.

| Group | Lines matched / expected / observed | `passed=` | Last stage that changed it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ids` | 158 | 157 | #19 |
| `time` | 119 | 118 | #20 |
| `mm` | 122 | 121 | #21, #22 |
| `fs` | 286 | 284 | #22 |
| `signal` | 202 | 201 | #23 |
| `fork` | 165 | 164 | #27 (new; 122 expected lines recorded first, 165 after review round 1) |
| `proc` | 136 | 135 | #27 (needs `fork` and cross-task waits; did not run before) |
| `jobctl` | 71 | 70 | #27 (new) |
| `pipe` | 39 | 38 | #27 (new) |

In the `baremetal-suite` `kernel-test` case every group printed `result=pass`, `matched == expected == observed` and `mismatches=0` (reproducibility record, fresh clone and `main` checkout alike).

**Gate chain** (the fresh clone below, `bash target/gates.sh <clone> fresh-v020 <clone>/target/gates-fresh-v020`, run unmodified; twelve steps, all `exit=0`):

### The bare-metal suite for the user

`target/kernel-tooling/baremetal-suite-002/` (integrator's run on `main` 0eb3b1d, `xtask baremetal-suite --output ...`; manifest `target/kernel-tooling/baremetal-suite-002/manifest.tsv`) holds the tree for **one separate USB stick** in `usb/`; **build id `0eb3b1d7e311`**, suite kernel sha256 `8f46497c...ae9` (full hash in the table above). `sha256sum -c usb/SHA256SUMS` matched all four files when this record was written. The kernel boots under Limine, runs its boot self-tests, prints a hardware inventory (CPUID, memory map, ACPI/MADT, RTC/TSC, framebuffers, PCI ids by read-only configuration mechanism 1), runs the nine oracle groups each as a fresh `/init` on a fresh rootfs compared line by line with the pinned Linux's output, replays its log in pages (about six seconds per page) and ends on one summary screen carrying the build id and a log digest. It has no disk, USB-storage, network or sound driver and calls no firmware runtime service, so it writes no storage or firmware state. It does reprogram timer and interrupt hardware (it resets and restarts the HPET main counter and clears its enable and legacy bits, programs the LAPIC, IOAPIC, PIT and 8259, reads the RTC, and writes the PCI 0xCF8 address register); the complete list is [what the suite never does](bare-metal-suite.md#what-it-never-does). The generated `usb/README.txt` also says "it writes nothing anywhere" in one place; read that as the same no-storage, no-firmware-state statement.

How the user runs it (the complete, safety-annotated procedure is `usb/README.txt` and [the procedure](bare-metal-suite.md#kernel-run-suite-a21); the user performs every device step, no agent will): choose a spare USB stick whose contents are not needed, confirm with `lsblk -o NAME,SIZE,MODEL,TRAN,MOUNTPOINTS` that it is `TRAN=usb` before any `sudo`, format it FAT32, copy `EFI`, `limine.conf`, `linux-rs-kernel` and `initramfs.cpio`, verify with `SHA256SUMS`, leave the normal boot entry unchanged, boot the stick once through the firmware's one-time boot menu, wait about 3 to 10 minutes, photograph every replay page and the summary in order, hard-reset, and send the photographs back. A screen that stays unchanged for ten minutes is a result too and is to be photographed.

QEMU boot of that exact tree (`tree-boot/summary-screen.png`, std VGA, q35 TCG `qemu64` 512M, OVMF, no serial): the author of this record opened the PNG and read a build-id `0eb3b1d7e311` header, `RESULT: ALL PASS (9 groups, 23 self-tests)`, per-group matched/expected counts equal to the table above, `SUITE complete ok=true groups=9/9 proofs=23/23`, log digest `70bb7525b674b69d`, 68,672 captured log bytes and 28 s uptime. The manifest still says `visual_review pending: the final screen is not parsed; a person must read it`; that field is not changed here, and an agent reading a QEMU screenshot is not physical evidence.

### Review process and its limits

The flow was feature branch, pull request, review, fix rounds, then the integrator's canonical gate run, then merge ([A20](decisions.md)). Authors and reviewers were Claude subagents; the integrator was the orchestrating session. For #20-#27 each pull-request body records two independent reviewers with different lenses (Linux exactness; unsafe/concurrency/security/resources, or physical safety/correctness for the suite) whose findings were fixed and then checked by an independent verifier (for example #20: five should-fix items; #21: eight; #22: one blocking and eighteen should-fix for the lane plus six for the file-mapping integration; #25: two; #27: three in WP5, none blocking in WP6-7, one in WP8). Some rounds are recorded as adversarial verification (see the signal-wiring review rounds), and mutation runs (a deliberately broken source line must fail a named case) were used where a check had to detect an omission (the fork group-end mutations are in the lane records; the `invlpg` mutation runs were lost, see below). GitHub review objects (`COMMENTED`) exist for #12-#19; for #20-#27 the review outcome is in the pull-request bodies and lane records, and no GitHub review object was found for them when this record was written. **Per-site review status of the assembly and unsafe sites added since v0.1.0** (`AGENTS.md` asks for an architecture reviewer, an unsafe reviewer and compiler/disassembly evidence for each site): the scheduler lane records "no independent architecture or unsafe review has happened" for `switch_to_asm`, `ret_from_fork_asm` and `restore_regs_and_iret`, with disassembly taken from the exact ELFs of `kernel-test` run `sched-001` (`switch_to_asm` 15 instructions, `ret_from_fork_asm` 7, `restore_regs_and_iret` 17); the fork lane records the CR3 switch in `context_switch`, the slot table's `unsafe` and `Tables::fork_user`/`destroy_user` as not independently reviewed, with disassembly not re-taken. Whether the later agent review rounds covered these sites, and whether the final merged ELFs (`89f83880...`, `eb222bfb...`, `8f46497c...`) were disassembled for them, is **not verified** by this record; no disassembly of the merged ELFs was taken for it. Author and reviewer agents share one model family, so "independent" here means separate agents with different lenses, not a different kind of reviewer. **No human has reviewed any of this**: every review is an agent review, no independent human review of unsafe, architecture or security boundaries exists, and the "no independent review" sentences inside several lane records were written before their review rounds and are kept as the history of those records. Reviews also do not replace the missing items under [not done](#not-done-in-v020).

### Known evidence losses

* **PR #13 author runs.** The author's `kernel-test` and gate runs for the kernel-services stage (`fgate1`, `fgate2`) and its `invlpg` mutation runs (`mut-noflush`, `mut-line414`, `mut-line461`) lived in the lane worktree's ignored `target/` and were deleted when that worktree was removed (2026-10-20). The [foundation record](#kernel-services-foundation) still describes them as observed; the directories no longer exist, so those figures cannot be re-verified from the retained evidence. The integrator's canonical runs `canon-pr13` and `canon-pr13b` (`target/gates-canon-pr13b/summary.txt`: all ten lines `exit=0`; `kernel-test` 18/18 in `target/kernel-tooling/canon-pr13b`) are retained and are the evidence for that stage.
* **PR #27 final per-case QEMU directories.** When the process-lane worktree was removed after merge, an archive-helper name clash let the removal delete the per-case QEMU directories of the last gate run `wp8-g2` (serial logs and built ELFs). Retained: `target/evidence-archive/proc-fork/closure/gates-wp8-g2/` (`summary.txt` and the logs, all twelve steps `exit=0`; `kernel-test.log` with 43/43 and 4/4), the stack records `closure/stack-records/*.tsv` and the earlier full runs' summaries (`pf-g2`, `pf-g4`, `pj-g1`, `pj-g2`, `wp8-g1`), plus the mutation logs. The helper was fixed (clash-safe, non-zero exit on leftovers). The regenerated per-case evidence is the post-merge run `main-v020` and the fresh-clone run above, which show the same 43/43 and 4/4 results on the merged tree.
* The stack-watermark and fork-group figures in the [process lane closure](#process-lane-closure) were read from the `wp8-g2`, `pf-g4` and `wp8-t2` runs; the table there is not re-derivable from `target/` any more except through the retained `stack-records`.

### Routing at v0.2.0

Twenty-six routed rows are `in_progress` and 2,597 are `not_started` of 2,623 (`xtask status` in the fresh clone; see [routed status](#routed-implementation-status)); the five-column schema and the two status values are unchanged. Rows route actual slices, not complete upstream prefixes. Crates added to the vendored set since v0.1.0: `buddy_system_allocator` 0.13.0 (23 vendored packages verified).

### Not done in v0.2.0

- **Physical execution of anything.** No physical evidence exists. The bare-metal suite above is **prepared, not cleared**: nothing new is physically cleared by this record, and nothing will be until the user has run it and a record of that run (the user's photographs, the build id and log digest, and what they show) says so. Diagnostic-003 remains the only conditionally cleared lineage ([handoff](diagnostic-handoff.md)); the suite kernel is a new lineage with no handoff clearance beyond this "prepared, not cleared" note (the A21 note in the handoff records the same).
- **Running the suite is the user's own informed decision, and the ring-3 gate recorded earlier is still open.** The [first-userspace record](#first-userspace-slice-kernel-side) says that the missing CPU-vulnerability mitigations (no KPTI: ring 3 runs with the whole kernel mapped in one CR3, so Meltdown-class CPUs leak), SMP, KASLR, stack protection, NMI/`#MC` recovery and independent architecture/security review "gate any physical run of ring 3", and that the diagnostic-003 authority covers none of it. None of these is closed; KASLR, stack protector and NMI/`#MC` recovery are absent. The suite runs ring-3 code, but only its own harness-built `/init` and the oracle test programs, with no untrusted input; that limits the exposure and is not a mitigation. The kernel also reprograms timer and interrupt hardware ([what the suite never does](bare-metal-suite.md#what-it-never-does)); it writes no storage or firmware state.
- **Physical blind spots: a first run may legitimately fail or print `WARN` lines.** Unverified on the Ryzen 9 9900X / ASUS TUF B650 board and not visible in QEMU: IOAPIC pin routing of IRQ0, quick-PIT accuracy, HPET behaviour after reset, TSC-deadline use on Zen 5, xAPIC page access, and NMI/SMI effects on calibration (a failing tick is reported only as `WARN timer-init`; see the limits of [interrupt controllers and timekeeping](#interrupt-controllers-and-timekeeping)); the alarm clocks (`CLOCK_REALTIME_ALARM`/`CLOCK_BOOTTIME_ALARM`) may differ from Linux on bare metal (a blind spot, not observed; see [time syscalls](#time-syscalls)); and compat-mode `SYSCALL`, the `#DB` IST/DR7 scrub and SMEP/SMAP violations were never executed (first-userspace record). A failure or `WARN` on the first physical run is a possible, expected outcome to photograph, not proof that the suite or the board is faulty.
- **One CPU, no SMP.** No per-CPU state, no CPU hotplug, no RCU, one FIFO run queue instead of EEVDF, no preemption of kernel code (`CONFIG_PREEMPT_VOLUNTARY` declared).
- **No storage, network, USB or input drivers**; no serial, disk, NIC, sound or keyboard path on the workstation, which is why the suite returns results as photographs of the framebuffer; no durable filesystem (tmpfs from the initramfs only).
- **No threads, copy-on-write, `vfork` or namespaces.** `fork` copies the address space eagerly; `CLONE_VM`, `CLONE_THREAD`, `CLONE_SIGHAND`, `CLONE_FILES`, `CLONE_FS`, `vfork` and namespace flags validate in Linux's errno order and then stop loudly.
- **Limits that Linux does not have:** 64 queued real-time signals per queue (`RT_QUEUE_CAPACITY`); a pool of 127 children beside init (kernel stacks; `EAGAIN` at the limit, table row T11); no `ETXTBSY` for `/init` after `exec`; the `execve` argument block must fit 16 KiB (table row T7); writable `MAP_SHARED` file mappings and several other cases stop loudly (see the lane records); `ppoll`, `select`, `pselect6`, `epoll`, ptrace, a real tty and pidfd-based waits are absent.
- **CPU-vulnerability mitigations are absent**; xstate beyond FXSR, vDSO, `CONFIG_RSEQ` and compat (32-bit) syscalls remain declared-configuration limits.
- **Whole syscall and subsystem families are absent** (each lane's own "Not done"/shortcut list is authoritative; the headings are the [identity](#identity-and-process-attribute-syscalls), [time](#time-syscalls), [memory-mapping wiring](#memory-mapping-wiring), [VFS spine](#vfs-spine) and [signal](#signal-model) records):
  - *Credentials:* fixed root. `setuid` and the other credential setters and `capset` are not implemented and stop the kernel; capabilities derive from `euid == 0` (table row T4); no LSM.
  - *Files:* `mkdir`/`rmdir`/`rename`/`link`/`symlink`/`chmod`/`chown`/`utime*`, `mount`/`umount`/`chroot`, `fsync`/`sync`, `flock`, `splice`/`sendfile`, xattrs and ACLs, procfs/sysfs/devtmpfs, FIFOs and sockets. "No durable filesystem" above does not convey this: even the tmpfs has no creation or rename syscalls.
  - *Time:* `timer_*`, `adjtimex`/`clock_adjtime`, `settimeofday`, `alarm`, `ITIMER_VIRTUAL`/`ITIMER_PROF` and CPU-clock `nanosleep` with a future expiry stop loudly; `stime` is always 0; process CPU time starts at 0 at `/init`; `sysinfo` load averages are over-reported after sleeps.
  - *Memory:* `mremap`, `mlock*`, `mincore`, shmem/hugetlb, `/proc/self/maps`, RSS counters and `setrlimit` enforcement; no SMP TLB shootdown.
  - *Signals:* `kill(0)`/`kill(-1)`, group stop for threads, core dumps, `signalfd`, `pidfd_send_signal` and the obsolete `sigaction` forms; interrupts stay masked while signals are delivered.
- **Performance is unqualified**: no measurement against the pinned Linux was taken; stack depth figures are maxima over the exercised paths, not a bound.
- **No human review.** Fork failure paths: the stack-pool-exhaustion unwind was run (`forkbomb` drives it twice, after the address space, descriptors, pins and `fs_struct` were built, and checks tables, files and RAM afterwards); the frame and heap `ENOMEM` unwinds at each earlier step were read, not run (no allocation-failure injection into `fork`).
- **An open user decision** (the process-lane design record `target/designs/process-lane.md` is local and untracked; the question is also in the [process lane closure](#process-lane-closure) and the [pull request #27](https://github.com/Big-Smarty/linux-rs/pull/27) body): any `Outcome::Unimplemented` stops the whole kernel, so a child that reaches a system call Linux implements and this kernel does not halts every process. It was kept because a success-shaped stub is forbidden. Terminating only the offending task with a loud console line and a fatal-signal-style status would be a behaviour change that needs the user's decision before any untrusted binary runs; the oracle `/init` issues only implemented calls. Nothing has been changed in answer to it.
- The rest of M2-M8 (and every architecture, driver family, filesystem, protocol and facility not routed above) is open; this checkpoint is not whole-kernel completion.

## Publication checkpoint

**2026-10-19, bounded wrap-up:** the authorized memory003 + corrected boot-image wave is landed, including the necessary scalar Rust memory-provider repair found during artifact review. No future SMP/platform/object/rootfs feature is part of this publication. Director verified successor `kernel-implementation-astra` actually runs `openai-codex/gpt-6-astra:max`; predecessor evidence/failures were inherited rather than restarted or silently rewritten. This is implementation plus exercised software evidence, **not full M1, Linux completion, physical or performance qualification**.

### Landed consumers and limits

- One indexed buddy backend now serves orders0–10; order0 caller compatibility remains without retaining incidental cyclic address order. Whole-block `Frame` ownership, checked byte alignment, zero-before-issue, explicit reserved-order reclaim and table rollback share the existing allocator. The old cyclic backend/verdict is deleted. Hierarchical free-word indexing and eligible-alignment search replace RAM-wide full-prefix rescanning; this is performance engineering, **not measured Linux parity**.
- `kernel/src/image/{bytes,cpio,elf,mod,tests}.rs` provide checked borrowed raw newc/crc and ELF inspection. Shared byte decoding also serves real ACPI parsing. Limine module metadata/payload extents are validated/reserved; each payload borrow ends before owned CR3, and the actual consumer verifies every retained payload page lacks an alias afterward. Two real hash-bound archives and one genuinely empty module are consumed. Compression recognition is not decompression; structural ELF decoding is not native exec policy, task mapping, VFS unpack, init/rootfs or userspace execution. Generic extended ELF program numbering and Linux incomplete-unpack side effects remain open.
- New/changed source for this bounded wave: `kernel/src/{bitmap,boot,buddy,firmware,lib,main,memory,runtime}.rs`, the five image files above, and `xtask/src/kernel.rs`. Root/member Cargo manifests, `package.rs`/metadata, CPU, console, paging, sync and baremetal source are unchanged by this wave. Exact008 CPU SHA below remains the repaired BSP/CPL0/scalar/no-unwind contract, not a live SMP authorization.
- Actual009 matched11/11 and its PNG was accepted, **but** fresh review found newly linked compiler-builtins handwritten x86 `memcpy` assembly, avoidable under the language/assembly contract. It remains provider-unqualified. The replacement in `runtime.rs` copies `MaybeUninit<u8/u64>` state without `assume_init`, preserving uninitialized padding: bounded byte peel, unaligned source word reads, aligned volatile destination word stores, exact tails. Valid nonoverlapping ranges may share an allocation; source shared readers are allowed, competing source writes/conflicting destination accesses are not. Zero count does not dereference null. No memmove/overlap promise, speculative runtime stubs or scalar-at-least-Linux claim is added.
- Real runtime checks cover272memset and4224memcpy alignment/length/return/sentinel combinations, null-zero, initialized fields with genuinely uninitialized padding, and both directions between disjoint ranges in one buffer. Director caught and removed a redundant identical-alias comparison: the surviving live source-only shared reader is compared to an independent index pattern, plus the full80-byte oracle.010 and011 each actually matched11/11, but later source corrections required fresh012 rather than rebinding old evidence.

| Additional reference initramfs semantics | Source/provenance reading only, no new observer/fixture/build/image/boot. Earlier real BIOS/UEFI/TCG and separate KVM reference cells retain their own narrowly described outcomes, including the strict warning-prefix failure. |

Publication goes through the separately authorized publisher after explicit final freeze; Git/SSH/remote success is the publisher's observed result, not inferred here. **Full Linux6.18.57 remains unfinished:** all remaining M0 gates/M1–M8, all architectures/drivers/filesystems/protocols/configurations, complete MM/SMP/timers/scheduling/processes/userspace, storage/network/security, root/replacement packaging, independent qualification, physical recovery evidence and matched at-least-Linux performance remain required and open. Current ledger has10`in_progress`/2613`not_started`, never `done` or a compatibility percentage;97,294 inventory entries and the five-column schema are unchanged.

### Audit fixes (2026-10-19)

An independent read of `kernel/` (no critical/high finding) led to these source changes on `fix/kernel-audit`: the framebuffer console now comes up before `cpu::validate` and `BootInfo::take` (`BootInfo::take_framebuffer`, same kind5/kind7 extent checks, so early `FAIL` records reach the serial-less physical screen); the raw command line is a bounded copy (`BootInfo::command_line`, 2048 bytes with NUL as Linux x86 `COMMAND_LINE_SIZE`, longer or unterminated lines fail with `CommandLine` rather than truncate) and the `test=` selectors are parsed only under `qemu-self-test`; the pixel store uses exposed provenance; `cpu::validate` rejects CR4.PGE/PCIDE; a boot module failing inspection logs `WARN Initramfs unpacking failed: module=N ...` and is skipped (as `init/initramfs.c`) while span/byte-bound errors stay fatal; one duplicate buddy request validation was removed. Not changed: ACPI/page-merge fail-closed handoff assumptions and the `rep movsq` note.

Checks (worktree `kernel-audit`): `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo +nightly clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked --offline -- -D warnings` and `cargo +nightly test --workspace --locked --offline` (10+59+45+2 passed, new command-line unit test included); both target-specific strict Clippy commands (`kaudit-clippy-{self,default}-001`); fresh `kernel-test` run `kaudit-001` matched 11/11 cases, run from a clone under `/tmp/ka` because the QMP socket path exceeds 100 bytes from the worktree path (copy retained under `target/kernel-tooling/kaudit-001-clone-copy`; the earlier `kaudit-001` here is the aborted socket-path attempt). ELF SHA-256: default `65fbe2eeba245099baeb75eea8f42f96c724cc4ccd3f346623751ef9a6e71e26`, `qemu-self-test` `e6b026e1e24c1fb84d30a8885d8bc1fb84cba1d30d485606d8bec200ca7398b0`. The default-boot screenshot shows the banner and every `CHECK` record through `CHECKPOINT bootstrap-complete` (human review still pending). The module-skip path was exercised once by hand (gzip-headed module 0 replaced in a copy of the success image: WARN record, module 1 inspected, exit 33); it is not a harness case. These bytes are not the diagnostic-003 lineage and carry no physical clearance.

### First userspace program

`user/init` (package `linux-rs-init`, binary `init`) is a static, libc-free x86_64 Linux executable meant to be the kernel's first `/init` and, unchanged, a differential oracle on real Linux. Source: `user/init/src/main.rs`; crate and rejection records in [dependencies](dependencies.md#first-userspace-program-userinit-one-admitted-crate-two-rejections).

- **Output:** `init: hello from userspace` (the checked `write(1, ...)` itself), one summary line `init: argc=<n> argv0=<s> pid=<n> rsp_mod16=<n> auxv=<KEY:value,...> execfn=<s> platform=<s> envp=<a,b>` (hex values; `*` for AT_SYSINFO_EHDR, AT_RANDOM, AT_EXECFN, AT_PLATFORM; `envp` lists its entries only when pid is 1 and they are exactly `HOME=/`,`TERM=linux`, otherwise just their count, so host runs and PID-namespace pid-1 runs never print the environment), then `init: ok` and `exit_group(0)`. The NULL-write EFAULT check needs stdout to be a file, pipe or tty: `/dev/null` ignores the buffer, so the check fails there. A failing check prints `init: FAIL <check> got=<value>` and exits 1; a panic prints `init: FAIL panic`. `argv[1] == fault` prints `init: faulting on purpose` and reads address 0 (SIGSEGV); `mmap` prints `init: calling mmap` and calls `mmap` (a syscall the first kernel slice does not implement); `rseq` expects `-ENOSYS` (true only in the candidate's declared `CONFIG_RSEQ=n`; the reference kernel has `RSEQ=y`); `sse` checks the initial FCW 0x37f, MXCSR 0x1f80 and zero XMM5/XMM15, then adds 1+1 on the x87 stack and squares 3.0 with SSE2 (the only FP/SIMD instructions in the binary, written with `asm!` because the target is soft-float) and prints `init: sse ok`. Unknown arguments are ignored.
- **Checks:** initial `rsp % 16 == 0`; `argc >= 1` and `argv[argc]` is the terminator; AT_PAGESZ 4096; AT_PHDR equals `__ehdr_start + e_phoff`; AT_PHENT 56; AT_PHNUM equals `e_phnum`; AT_ENTRY equals `_start`; AT_SECURE 0; AT_RANDOM non-null with 16 readable bytes; AT_EXECFN and AT_PLATFORM readable NUL-terminated; `write(1,buf,0) == 0`; `write(1,NULL,1)` and `write(1,0x8,5)` give EFAULT; `write(0x7fffffff,..)` and `write(-1,..)` give EBADF (never-open descriptors); syscall 100000 gives ENOSYS. Only as pid 1: argv0 is `/init` and envp is exactly `HOME=/`, `TERM=linux` (`init/main.c` `argv_init`/`envp_init`; `rdinit=`/`init=`, unknown `key=value` words and unknown bare or post-`--` words change them). Its limits: a malformed initial stack faults instead of being reported, and kernel-provided pointers are read directly.
- **Build** (no host build: the bin needs `--features freestanding`, as the kernel does):
  `cargo +nightly rustc -Zbuild-std=core,compiler_builtins -Zbuild-std-features=compiler-builtins-mem -p linux-rs-init --bin init --target x86_64-unknown-none --release --locked --features freestanding --target-dir <dir> -- -Crelocation-model=static -Cstrip=debuginfo -Clink-arg=--no-pie -Clink-arg=--no-undefined`.
  Result: ET_EXEC at 0x200000, no PT_INTERP/PT_DYNAMIC, empty `nm -u`, no `rep` string instruction, and no xmm/x87 instruction outside the `sse`-mode `fpu_checks`. `x86_64-unknown-none` is soft-float, so the kernel need not enable user FPU state. `memset`/`memcpy` link from the tiny `linux-rs-runtime` crate (`rt/`, moved out of the kernel binary; both the kernel and the init depend on it, the init does not depend on `kernel`) rather than `compiler_builtins`' REP assembly. A scratch build forcing a 4 KiB copy confirmed the linker picks them, and both kernel profiles' link maps show `memcpy`/`memset` from that crate. The init ELF has no `limine` section or symbol. The crate is empty off `x86_64-unknown-none`, and its self-test is behind feature `self-test`, enabled by the kernel's `qemu-self-test`; the self-test was not run in QEMU for this change.
- **Evidence:** `target/linux-reference/init-reference-003/` (README with all commands, hashes, the exact auxv order and verbatim summary lines): the host run exits 0 with `init: ok`; the pinned 6.18.57 reference `bzImage` in QEMU TCG with a one-file newc initramfs printed `init: ok` then `Kernel panic - not syncing: Attempted to kill init! exitcode=0x00000000`; with `-- fault` it printed `init[1]: segfault at 0 ...` then `exitcode=0x0000000b`; with `vdso=0` the same as normal minus AT_SYSINFO_EHDR. `init-reference-001` (printed the host environment, carried Limine statics) and `-002` (probed fd 5) are superseded. One run per mode, TCG only; the candidate kernel's run of this program is not yet recorded.
- **Assembly proof, `_start`** (`user/init/src/main.rs`, `naked_asm!`): at process entry the kernel provides only `rsp` (-> argc; `rsp % 16 == 0` per the SysV psABI), with no return address or calling-convention registers; ordinary Rust cannot read `rsp` before a prologue and no intrinsic exists, so a naked function is the smallest boundary. It zeroes `rbp` (outermost frame), copies `rsp` to `rdi` (the argument of the `extern "C"` Rust main, so alignment is reported unmodified), aligns `rsp` down by 16 (clobbers flags) so a misaligned stack is reported by a check and not by misaligned Rust code, and `call`s `start_rust` (one return address pushed on the aligned stack, no other memory access). `start_rust` never returns (`exit_group`); `ud2` traps if it did; there is no unwinding (`panic = abort`, `extern "C"`). User mode, no privilege or interrupt requirement. The `syscall` instructions are the `syscalls` crate's (see its record). Disassembly review: `init.disasm` in the evidence directory; no independent assembly review has been done.

### Generated initramfs fixtures and the built init

Worktree `harness`, branch `feat/generated-initramfs`; mechanism in [kernel tooling](kernel-tooling.md#generated-initramfs-fixtures), crate records in [dependencies](dependencies.md#host-tool-dependencies-cpio-and-elf-for-the-generated-test-fixtures). `kernel-test` no longer reads the two ignored `target/linux-reference` CPIO files: it builds `user/init`, wraps the ELF in a `cpio::write_cpio` newc archive and computes the expected `CHECK image inspected` record by construction (`records=4 files=3 regular=1 symlinks=1 trailers=1 skipped=0 elf=1 loads=3 interpreters=0`, `reserved=53248 bytes=52220` for the 52,220-byte archive). The twelfth case `bad-module` boots a gzip-magic garbage module 0 and the archive as module 1.

Exercised (all returned 0): `cargo +1.88.0 clippy -p xtask -p baremetal --all-targets --locked --offline -- -D warnings`; `cargo +1.88.0 test -p xtask -p baremetal --locked --offline` (10 + 65 tests); `cargo +nightly clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked --offline -- -D warnings`; `cargo +nightly test --workspace --locked --offline` (10 + 59 + 65 + 2 doc tests); `cargo fmt --all --check`; `check` (21 vendored packages verified) and `status`; and `env PATH="/bin:$PATH" cargo +1.88.0 run -p xtask --locked --offline -- kernel-test --output target/kernel-tooling/gen-001 --timeout-seconds 60`, then `gen-002` on the final code (**12/12 matched** each; the default framebuffer PNG of `gen-001` was viewed: the kernel line shows `module=0 ... RESERVED=53248 BYTES=52220 RECORDS=4 FILES=3 ... ELF=1 LOADS=3`, module epoch `MODULES=1`, `CHECKPOINT BOOTSTRAP-COMPLETE`). The serial of `bad-module` printed exactly `WARN Initramfs unpacking failed: module=0 error=Archive { offset: 0, error: CompressionPending(Gzip) } skipped=true` and the archive record as `module=1`; `empty-module` showed `module=1 reserved=4096 bytes=0` and `modules=2`. `kernel-package --build-output target/kernel-tooling/gen-002/freestanding-build --output target/kernel-tooling/pkg-gen-001` produced the package (the consumer derives its case catalog from the harness, now twelve cases).

**Fresh clone** (the headline result): `git clone --branch feat/generated-initramfs` of this repository into `target/fresh-gen`, an empty `CARGO_HOME` directory, `CARGO_NET_OFFLINE=true`, **no fixture file copied** (`target/linux-reference` does not exist there), then `env PATH="/bin:$PATH" cargo +1.88.0 run -p xtask --locked --offline -- kernel-test --output target/kernel-tooling/fresh-001 --timeout-seconds 60` returned 0 with **12/12 matched** (code commit `252850d`); repeated on the final head `6563ed1` in a new clone `target/fresh-gen2` with a new empty `CARGO_HOME` as `fresh-002`: again 12/12 with the same three ELF hashes as `gen-002`, empty `CARGO_HOME` left holding only Cargo's own cache directories. Its default-boot capture is `pending` human review like every capture.

**Path independence.** All build profiles pass `--remap-path-prefix` for the checkout (`/src`), the run directory (`/out`) and the sysroot (`/toolchain`) through `CARGO_ENCODED_RUSTFLAGS`, so the flags reach `core`, `compiler_builtins`, the vendored crates and the project crates alike (flags after `cargo rustc --` would reach only the final crate). The worktree run `gen-001` (checkout `.../target/worktrees/harness`) and the fresh clone `fresh-001` (checkout `.../target/fresh-gen`, different run name and `CARGO_HOME`) produced **byte-identical** `init-build/init.elf` (`c4a9bccb...6126f`), `freestanding-build/linux-rs-kernel.elf` (`04666ccd...3ccb2`), `qemu-self-test-build/linux-rs-kernel.elf` (`e439aea2...17da8`) and fixtures (`initramfs.cpio` `859498a7...d8832`, corrupt module `fc79c4d4...2e3b0`); `gen-002` reproduced the same hashes in the worktree. No `/home/` string remains in any of the three ELFs. This answers the `arch-crates` branch's recorded fresh-clone finding (see "CPU-layer evidence" below) (same segments, different whole-file hash because of embedded paths) for these inputs only. **Not run:** a control build without the remap (so the flags' necessity is inferred from the earlier differing hashes, not re-measured), a different toolchain location, a different user or a different host. Same source, toolchain and vendored bytes are prerequisites; this is not an independent reproducibility attestation and does not make later bytes part of the diagnostic003 physical lineage (they are not: the ELF changed with the new flags and source).

**Not covered:** no kernel execution of the init (no `CHECK userspace exec`, no init output or Linux "Attempted to kill init" line is expected or accepted yet); the `bad-module` warning text is the kernel's current `Debug` formatting and must be updated with it; `cpio` truncates sizes above `u32::MAX` (the generator rejects them); no new independent review of the new code.

## Engineering decisions made for this request

These are implementation sequencing choices under the request, not additional detailed user approvals:

- Use the maintained `x86_64-unknown-none` target, ELF/System V without red zone or FP/SIMD, rather than host Linux `core`. Kernel package `kernel`, binary `linux-rs-kernel`; host workspace default members remain the existing tools.
- Use the genuine Limine base revision 5 protocol first. Installed external `BOOTX64.EFI` plus OVMF permits real loader/firmware handoff without a project C stub or a new crate. Base revision 5 guarantees exited EFI boot services, defined entry state and mapped ACPI data. The kernel verifies the revision and required responses; it never calls boot services.
- A Linux bzImage/EFI-native stub preserves direct systemd-boot and broader Linux loader compatibility, but entails additional image/real-mode/mixed-mode contracts. PVH is a genuine Linux-supported 32-bit protected-mode handoff and useful direct-QEMU alternative, but requires an initial 32-to-64-bit transition and is not a physical UEFI deployment path. Neither is silently considered implemented by using Limine. Both remain required scope, together with BIOS and remaining baseline paths.
- Use explicit BSP-only early ownership, fallible RAM allocation, owned page tables and guard stacks before enabling interrupts. No generic global allocator, sleeping/reclaim/I/O, DMA, AP activation or application-facing artificial resource limit is introduced. Reserved and loader-reclaimable ranges remain reserved until their consumers and firmware lifetimes can be discharged.
- Panic aborts with bounded emergency serial output and stops; it does not reboot. QEMU's explicitly configured debug-exit device supplies a test result, not a physical shutdown implementation.

## Five required design questions

### Loader, firmware and trusted entry

1. **Can I use a preexisting crate?** `limine`, `uefi`, `acpi` and `x86_64` were candidates; this historical cell added no third-party crate. `x86_64` 0.15.5 and `raw-cpuid` 11.6.0 were admitted later for the [CPU layer](#cpu-layer-on-audited-crates). Limine is an external bootloader/firmware dependency, not linked kernel implementation. Its protocol header is 0BSD with notices preserved in the translated layout file. Compiler `core` and actual linked runtime implementations are separately audited, not claimed absent.
2. **Can I bundle functionality together?** One boot-record boundary owns immutable loader responses and checked physical/virtual translation. It is shared by firmware parsing, physical allocation and mapping construction; none builds a second memory map or allocator. Only format-independent checked byte/range operations are shared. EFI service lifetimes and ACPI checks remain distinct.
3. **Can I simplify things?** One real UEFI/Limine x86_64 debug cell and synchronous initialization reduce bring-up variables without claiming Linux boot, physical firmware or architecture parity. Four-level paging is requested and checked rather than guessed. Unknown/invalid records fail explicitly; no table is treated as a successful unsupported service.
4. **Is this secure?** Firmware, loader responses, memory-map lengths and ACPI tables cross trust boundaries. Checked counts/arithmetic/alignment, overlap rejection, range-class checks and checksums precede interpretation. The initial loader itself is trusted to supply live mappings; a malicious privileged loader is outside protection. Runtime/ACPI/reserved/MMIO memory never becomes free RAM. No firmware calls occur after exit. Secure Boot, DMA/IOMMU confinement, CPU mitigations, entropy and physical recovery are not established by this debug cell.
5. **How can I improve this?** Preserve source records in place while reserved, avoid unnecessary copies, compare boot/memory checkpoints and later Linux-matched timing/footprint on actual hardware. A native Rust EFI stub and Linux/PVH paths expand compatibility; they are obligations, not already demonstrated improvements.

### Physical-page ownership and mappings

1. **Can I use a preexisting crate?** Generic buddy/slab/heap crates do not supply Linux zones, reclaim, accounting or early reservation semantics. Use `core` checked arithmetic and a kernel-owned early bitmap with storage sized from actual usable RAM. It is not the completed Linux allocator. No allocator crate or heap is admitted.
2. **Can I bundle functionality together?** A single allocator issues non-copyable frame owners to memory tests and page-table construction. Kernel, stack, metadata, firmware and loader ownership are kept distinct. Page tables use the same physical allocator and retain ownership until retirement; freeing a frame requires its unique token.
3. **Can I simplify things?** Initial non-sleeping BSP-only allocation can fail without retry/reclaim/reserves; actual memory bounds replace fixed-success test arenas. This sequencing does not erase Linux NUMA/zones/SLUB/reclaim/OOM/GFP/accounting requirements. No userspace allocation ABI is advertised.
4. **Is this secure?** Every allocated frame originates wholly inside a validated usable range; metadata reserves itself; page-zero, holes, image, stack, firmware and loader memory are excluded. Overflow/exhaustion/invalid alignment leave accounting unchanged. Owned page references cannot outlive release. Page tables keep writable data non-executable and kernel text non-writable, with no writable direct-map alias of image text. TLB state changes precede mapping retirement. DMA and additional CPUs remain inactive; BSP-only assumptions must be replaced before enabling either.
5. **How can I improve this?** Host property/conservation tests and real frame-content/reuse checks precede batching. Later hand off exactly once to mature Linux-compatible page allocation, retaining allocation/metadata counts. Measure latency, footprint and fragmentation against the pinned reference before optimization claims.

### Exceptions, interrupts, clocks and diagnostics

1. **Can I use a preexisting crate?** Core atomics and the supported CPUID intrinsic suffice for scalar state. Architecture crates would still require entry/interrupt/stack/unsafe review; this historical cell admitted none. The later [CPU layer](#cpu-layer-on-audited-crates) admits `x86_64`/`raw-cpuid` for descriptor formats and register access.
2. **Can I bundle functionality together?** One normalized exception-frame boundary saves/restores complete scalar state. One diagnostic path is allocation-free and bounded. Timer IRQ state uses atomics, never the allocator or serial formatting inside the handler. Distinct CPU error-code conventions and NMI/double-fault stacks remain explicit.
3. **Can I simplify things?** Use one explicitly selected legacy PC clockevent/controller pair for the first q35 cell; APIC/IOAPIC, high-resolution timekeeping, SMP, NMI nesting, suspend and all other clocks/controllers remain open. Actual hardware interrupt counts and exception return assertions, not a printed banner, gate the experiment.
4. **Is this secure?** The IDT is installed before enabling maskable interrupts. Unavoidable entry assembly proves stack alignment, register preservation, DF clearing, no unwinding and `iretq` layout. Emergency stacks isolate double fault/NMI/machine-check from normal stack failure. Expected test faults require exact vector/address/error/RIP; unexpected traps report failure and stop. IRQ handlers do not allocate, sleep or take a lock; timer progress has a host-enforced timeout.
5. **How can I improve this?** Inspect final disassembly for all entry/return sites, helpers and unexpected SIMD/CRT symbols. Add real controller/time-source calibration and physical drift/latency evidence without changing Linux semantics. A VM tick count is not monotonic-clock or performance qualification.

## Ordered execution and task-specific definition of done

The task remains open until the following named obligations have implementation and evidence. The initial experiment can report only its actually exercised rows; M1 and full Linux cannot close from it.

| ID | Dependency and implementation contract | Required observation / failure behavior |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ENTRY | Freestanding target, ELF linker bounds/W^X sections, panic-abort; loader protocol and CPU capability checks before ordinary kernel execution | Real ELF artifact; defined stack and CPU state; missing response/unsupported state rejected; target specification, symbols and disassembly retained |
| HANDOFF | ENTRY; immutable validated map, actual HHDM, executable address and EFI64 handoff, ACPI RSDP/root table parsing with reserved lifetime | Sorted non-overlap/overflow/range/checksum checks; malformed table/map negative tests; no boot-service calls; preserve reserved ranges |
| RAM | HANDOFF; real variable-sized metadata and unique physical-page allocation | Alignment/bounds/conservation, zero/reuse/content isolation, multiple frames, exhaustion then recovery; malformed map and overflow reject without fabricated allocation |
| TABLES | RAM; kernel-owned four-level tables, kernel sections, non-executable direct map, guard stacks, CR3 transition | Read-back/walker permissions, no text writable alias, guard/nonpresent and readonly/NX CPU faults; no freed page-table reference |
| TRAPS | ENTRY/TABLES; GDT/TSS/IDT, dedicated emergency stacks, complete scalar save/return | Deliberate breakpoint return, exact expected page-fault diagnosis/fixup, unexpected fault terminal case, frame/stack assertions and disassembly |
| TICK | TRAPS; configured legacy PC controller/clockevent, interrupt masking and EOI | Multiple delivered interrupts, atomic monotonic counts, disable prevents progress, preserved interrupted scalar state; missing tick times out nonzero |
| FAILURE | All above; bounded diagnostic stop and explicit test mode | Controlled panic/unexpected-trap modes terminate with distinct nonzero QEMU result; timeout never equals success |
| BUILD | Exact source/compiler/target/linker/runtime/firmware identity | No undefined symbols, CRT/libc/unwinder/SIMD/standalone assembly; map helper code to reviewed Rust sources; reproducible clean builds and retained hashes |
| REVIEW | Independent architecture, memory, unsafe and security review | Reviewers and findings retained; no self-certification |
| PHYSICAL | Same artifact lineage, native platform protocol/console/controller support and user-approved recovery | User-operated separate removable/candidate selection, captured physical evidence and known-good recovery; no host mutation by agents |
| PERF | Pinned Linux reference cell plus matched qualified physical cell | Boot/memory/timer/idle metrics, repeats/confidence/security equivalence; unmeasured is unqualified |
| FULL | Dependency-ordered M2–M8 and every pinned obligation | Every architecture/driver/protocol/filesystem/facility/ABI/configuration plus review/physical/performance closure; no scope deletion |

## Pinned source and specification map

Read before implementation: Linux commit above, `Documentation/arch/x86/boot.rst` (GPL-2.0), `arch/x86/platform/pvh/head.S` (GPL-2.0; Copyright C 2016 Oracle and/or its affiliates), `arch/x86/kernel/idt.c` (GPL-2.0-only), `mm/memblock.c` (GPL-2.0-or-later; Peter Bergner/IBM, June 2001). These establish sequencing/behavior reference; no C/standalone assembly is imported or linked. This bootstrap expresses architectural mechanisms informed by those references, not a claim that those complete files have been ported. The independently fetched annotated tag/commit/tree match the pin. `target/implementation-prereqs/upstream-verification.txt` records a locally valid signature by fingerprint `647F28654894E3BD457199BE38DBBDC86092693E`, authenticated against kernel.org HTTPS/WKD fingerprint publication. GPG reports `TRUST_UNDEFINED`; neither Web-of-Trust certification nor ultimate trust is claimed.

Limine protocol `3a0526b700e356f0eac1b71a77697b3fd1c707a3`, `PROTOCOL.md` and `include/limine.h`, supplies external loader ABI definitions. Its 0BSD notices remain in `kernel/src/boot.rs`; original validation code is GPL-2.0-only, so the combined file accurately declares `GPL-2.0-only AND 0BSD`, not a new 0BSD grant for original project code. The protocol revision was resolved by `git ls-remote`, which establishes identity rather than signature authentication. CPU authority inspected for this initial cell: [Intel SDM volume 3A, order 253668-060US, September 2016](https://www.intel.com/content/dam/www/public/us/en/documents/manuals/64-ia-32-architectures-software-developer-vol-3a-part-1-manual.pdf), sections 4.1.4, 6.14.2–6.14.5 and 11.12.1–11.12.2: unconditional long-mode SS:RSP saves/returns, stack alignment/IST and CPUID/PAT selection. This edition supports these initial mechanisms, not a claim of contemporary CPU-erratum/mitigation closure or complete manual review.

External boot artifacts are not Rust kernel code. Installed `limine 12.9.3-2` is a package-signature-validated external BSD-2-Clause bootloader with bundled third-party notices in `/usr/share/doc/limine/{COPYING,3RDPARTY.md}`. Its versioned source tag resolves to `e3aa138d383ca4fe061f05931b5a54497cd502f2`; `common/protos/limine.c` declares maximum base revision6. Installed `BOOTX64.EFI` SHA-256 is `94308b4e9ee1783a65cfd60570240a80595baf125c5b18e729d3dd977194ad51`. Installed `edk2-ovmf 202608-1` is external firmware with BSD-2-Clause-Patent/Apache-2.0/MIT package provenance and notices at `/usr/share/licenses/edk2-ovmf/{License.txt,OvmfPkg.License.txt}`. CODE SHA-256 is `2febd26c0b4cf95a636a941afa37d64a552723443ed9ac72f763f6840da98cb4`; pristine VARS SHA-256 is `5d2ac383371b408398accee7ec27c8c09ea5b74a0de0ceea6513388b15be5d1e`. The provenance worker retained exact versions, hashes and notice hashes in `target/implementation-prereqs/tooling-manifest.txt`; package integrity was checked, not source-to-binary reproducibility. No firmware update or global trust-store operation occurred. Redistribution must preserve every applicable third-party notice; local firmware use does not approve linking its implementation into the kernel.

## Routed implementation status

The five-column [ledger](../planning/workstreams.tsv) still accepts only `not_started` and `in_progress`. Twenty-six routes below are active and 2,597 remain not started: the publication checkpoint's ten, plus `ws-kernel-fork-c` for the [process creation and exit lane](#process-creation-and-exit), `ws-kernel-pid-c` for the [task model](#task-model-and-process-syscalls) (`ws-kernel-exit-c` gains its wait/reparent half), `ws-kernel-sched` for the [scheduler core](#scheduler-core), plus `ws-kernel-sys-c` for the [identity and process-attribute syscalls](#identity-and-process-attribute-syscalls), `ws-kernel-signal-c` for the [signal model](#signal-model) and its delivery wiring, `ws-kernel-time` for the [time syscalls](#time-syscalls), plus `ws-arch-x86-entry`, `ws-kernel-exit-c`, `ws-drivers-tty` and `ws-mm-mmap-c` for the [first-userspace slice](#first-userspace-slice-kernel-side), and `ws-mm-vma-c`, `ws-mm-mprotect-c` and `ws-mm-memory-c` for the [memory-mapping model](#memory-mapping-model) and `ws-mm-madvise-c` for the [wired memory syscalls](#memory-mapping-wiring), plus `ws-mm-shmem-c` and `ws-drivers-char` for the [VFS spine](#vfs-spine) (`ws-fs`, `ws-init` and `ws-drivers-tty` gain its files). New selected mechanisms start `ws-mm-page-alloc-c`, `ws-init` and `ws-fs`; existing bitmap/ownership routes continue. Constants/layout references are recorded in provenance, not promoted into claims of complete header/API implementation. No count is a compatibility percentage.

| Existing workstream / upstream ownership prefix | Actual Rust implementation | Exercised cell / limits |

Full entry/APIC/SMP/syscalls/platforms remain open. The first-userspace slice adds `traps.c` signal/message mapping and `get_sigframe_size` for the FXSAVE-only FPU. Ring-3 entry, the `SYSCALL` entry, SMEP/SMAP, one full-`pt_regs` entry stub for every exception and interrupt vector, and the exit-to-user path (timers, signal delivery, the `.Lerror_bad_iret` equivalent, `FXSAVE`/`FXRSTOR`/`lar`) are in `cpu.rs`/`userspace.rs`; ring-3 faults are forced signals that user handlers can catch ([signal wiring](#signal-wiring)). |
| `ws-arch-x86-entry` / `arch/x86/entry` | [Syscall dispatch by pinned number table](../kernel/src/syscall/mod.rs) | `int nr` truncation, the 6.18.57 table (the `syscalls` crate plus 17 numbers Linux answers `-ENOSYS`), `write`/`getpid`/`exit`/`exit_group`; everything else in the table stops loudly. The `SYSCALL` entry (`pt_regs`, `iretq` return, `STAR`/`LSTAR`/`CSTAR`/`SFMASK`) is in `cpu.rs`. |
| `ws-arch-x86-mm` / `arch/x86/mm` | [Owned four-level tables](../kernel/src/paging.rs) | Current consumer has W^X/four guards/no image or module payload alias, complete table-allocation failure rollback. Also user mappings below `TASK_SIZE_MAX` (U/S on every level, NX unless executable), mapped, unmapped, protected and freed both before and after CR3 publication (`invlpg` + atomic PTE updates + rollback; the [update rules](#kernel-services-foundation)), and the software user-page walk (`UserSpace` borrows `&mut Tables`). The [memory-mapping model](#memory-mapping-model) ports `arch/x86/mm/fault.c` (`do_user_addr_fault` decision, `access_error`) and `arch/x86/mm/mmap.c` (`mmap_base`, `mmap_address_hint_valid`), now [wired](#memory-mapping-wiring): a ring-3 page fault and a kernel access to a non-resident user page are resolved by the model on the real tables (`Tables::mm_*`: shared zero frame, private anonymous frames, `PROT_NONE` keeping the frame, `invlpg`), a refused ring-3 fault is today's segfault report and init death, and the vsyscall page, OOM, bad PTEs and file faults are loud stops. No PCID/global, SMP TLB shootdown, page-table freeing, reclaim or other page modes. |

Static PIE, `PT_INTERP`, `SIGBUS` pages and an existing script interpreter are explicit unsupported cases. |

No decompression (a compressed module is skipped with Linux's failure text), `init=`/`rdinit=`, command-line environment, initrd reclaim or `do_mounts` beyond the failure texts. |

Not scheduler/RCU/printk/watchdog or Linux userspace compatibility. Not scheduler/RCU/printk/watchdog or Linux userspace compatibility. |

No EEVDF, classes, nice, affinity, load balancing, cgroups, PSI, `sched_setscheduler` or any `/proc/sched` output; no SMP. Not independently reviewed ([record](#scheduler-core)). |

Not Linux qspinlock/lockdep/preemption/RT/SMP/fairness closure. |

The oracle `ids` group matches 158/158 (QEMU TCG `qemu64`); setters, credentials and any second task stop the kernel ([record](#identity-and-process-attribute-syscalls)). |

Job control is wired ([pipes, `poll` and job control](#pipes-poll-and-job-control): group stop and continue, `wants_signal`, the orphaned-group rule, `kill` to groups and everyone through the task list); no ptrace/core dumps, no XSAVE/ia32/x32 frames, one thread per group. |

Alarm clocks without a wakeup, CPU-clock sleeps, `settimeofday`, `adjtimex`, POSIX/interval timers and signal-interrupted sleeps are not implemented ([record](#time-syscalls)). Added by the [signal wiring](#signal-wiring): `ITIMER_REAL` (`setitimer`, `getitimer`, `alarm`: [`signal/itimer.rs`](../kernel/src/signal/itimer.rs)), the interruptible deadline wait ([`signal/wait.rs`](../kernel/src/signal/wait.rs)) and the interval timer as a [clockevent](../kernel/src/timer.rs) wake-up source; `ITIMER_VIRTUAL`/`ITIMER_PROF` stop loudly. |
| `ws-lib` / `lib` | [Bounded native bitmap](../kernel/src/bitmap.rs), checked [byte decoding](../kernel/src/image/bytes.rs) | The bitmap now supports the one indexed buddy backend; obsolete cyclic-selection tests/implementation are removed. Not complete Linux library/atomic bitmap or other-width/endian execution. |
| `ws-mm` / `mm` | [Frame/reservation ownership](../kernel/src/memory.rs), [fallible heap policy](../kernel/src/heap.rs), [`#[global_allocator]` over buddy frames](../kernel/src/kalloc.rs) | Actual RAM-sized metadata, raw initialization before typed references, whole-block noncopy owners, zero-before-issue, quiescent reserved-order reclaim and `split_page`; a fallible-only kernel heap (vendored `buddy_system_allocator`, power-of-two rounding, chunks never returned, task context only, `alloc` built `no_global_oom_handling`) is a project mechanism, not a SLUB/SLAB port. One CPU; no slab caches/zones/NUMA/reclaim/GFP flags. |
| `ws-mm-mmap-c` / `mm/mmap.c` | [`brk`/`mmap`/`munmap` syscalls](../kernel/src/syscall/memory.rs), [ELF loader through the model](../kernel/src/exec/load.rs), [access_ok and copies](../kernel/src/usercopy.rs), [`mmap`/`munmap`/`brk`/`do_mmap`/`__get_unmapped_area`/`find_vma`/`expand_stack` model](../kernel/src/mm/ops.rs) | The [memory-mapping model](#memory-mapping-model) ports `brk`, `do_mmap`, `ksys_mmap_pgoff` (typed `NeedsFile`/`Unsupported` outcomes), `__get_unmapped_area`, `find_vma`, `may_expand_vm`, `expand_downwards` callers and `vm_brk_flags` as host-tested pure code and is [wired](#memory-mapping-wiring): the `brk`, `mmap` (anonymous; fds 0-2 are the console, whose tty has no `->mmap`: `-ENODEV`; other descriptors `-EBADF`; shmem, hugetlb and files with `->mmap` stop loudly), `munmap` syscalls and the ELF loader call it, and the plan's own checks are deleted (one mechanism). `mm/util.c` (`vm_mmap_pgoff`, `__vm_enough_memory`, modes 0/1) and `mm/mmap_lock.c` (`lock_mm_and_find_vma`, without locks) are used as selected helpers; their rows stay `not_started`. |
| `ws-mm-vma-c` / `mm/vma.c` | [VMA set, merge/split/unmap, `anon_vma` identity, gap search, `mmap_region`/`do_brk_flags`](../kernel/src/mm/space.rs) | Sorted-`Vec` VMA set with `vma_merge_new_range`/`vma_merge_existing_range`/`vma_modify`/`__split_vma`/`vms_gather_munmap_vmas`, `find_mergeable_anon_vma`, `unmapped_area{,_topdown}`, `expand_downwards`, `acct_stack_growth`, `insert_vm_struct`; wired through the syscalls and the loader. No maple tree, locks/RCU, rmap, `mremap`, `vma_expand` for `mremap`, sealing, userfaultfd or file hooks. |
| `ws-mm-mprotect-c` / `mm/mprotect.c` | [`do_mprotect_pkey`, `mprotect_fixup`](../kernel/src/mm/ops.rs) | Check order, hole semantics, partial effect, `VM_MAY*` limits, accounting; no protection keys, MDWE, `pkey_*`, NUMA-hinting or LSM hook. Wired: the `mprotect` syscall edits the real tables (`PROT_NONE` keeps the frame). |
| `ws-mm-memory-c` / `mm/memory.c` | [`handle_mm_fault` anonymous path](../kernel/src/mm/fault.rs) | Demand-zero (zero page on read, private frame on write), write-protect reuse and copy from the zero page; file faults return a typed `NeedsFile`. No swap, THP, NUMA hinting, `fork`/COW sharing, GUP, `remap_pfn` or accounting counters. Wired: ring-3 faults and kernel accesses to non-resident user pages. |
| `ws-mm-shmem-c` / `mm/shmem.c` | [tmpfs inode arena, directories, sparse page store, space accounting](../kernel/src/vfs/tmpfs.rs) | `shmem_get_inode`, `shmem_mknod`/`mkdir`/`link`/`unlink`/`symlink`, `shmem_setattr`/`getattr`, `shmem_file_read_iter`, `BOGO_DIRENT_SIZE` directory sizes, `shmem_reserve_inode` and `shmem_inode_acct_blocks` limits (half of RAM, all inodes), `libfs` `simple_offset_*` directory cookies (newest first); only the rootfs instance exists. No swap, THP, `memfd`, xattrs, ACLs, quota, `shmem_zero_setup` or shared mappings. |
| `ws-mm-madvise-c` / `mm/madvise.c` | [`madvise` argument checks and VMA walk](../kernel/src/syscall/memory.rs) | `is_valid_madvise`, `madvise_should_skip` and `madvise_walk_vmas` (`-ENOMEM` for a hole) with `MADV_NORMAL`, which changes nothing observable here; every other valid behaviour stops loudly as unimplemented. No `process_madvise`, `MADV_DONTNEED`/`FREE`/`WILLNEED`/`REMOVE`/`DONTFORK`, hwpoison, THP or KSM. |
| `ws-mm-page-alloc-c` / `mm/page_alloc.c` | [Selected indexed buddy splitting/coalescing](../kernel/src/buddy.rs) | Orders0–10, natural/caller byte alignment, fragmentation/conservation and real RAM consumers. Not full Linux page allocator/GFP/watermarks/migration/per-CPU cache policy. |

The [publication checkpoint](#publication-checkpoint) bound the combined source and evidence of that round; the [v0.1.0 checkpoint](#v010-continuation-checkpoint) is now current. Historical cell9 and004–010 records remain immutable and scoped to their own inputs; ignored local evidence is **not** a checked-in distribution. No `done` state or whole-prefix completion is introduced.

## First-userspace slice (kernel side)

Decision [A18](decisions.md) selects ring-3 execution of a static `/init` from the boot archives ahead of the M2/M3 scheduler and SMP gates. This section records the kernel side. **Phase 1:** every mechanism below as host-tested pure logic in the `kernel` library, the freestanding loader that builds `/init`'s address space before CR3 publication, and the shared user-copy walk exercised against the real owned tables. **Phase 2:** the ring-3 transition and `SYSCALL` entry in `cpu.rs` (Linux GDT selectors, `STAR`/`LSTAR`/`SFMASK`, a `pt_regs` frame, `iretq` return), routing of ring-3 exceptions to `exec::report`, SMEP/SMAP, the state moved into statics, `Run /init as init process`, the `CHECK userspace exec ...`/`RESULT` contract and Linux's `NO_INIT` panic, with **manual** QEMU runs of `user/init`. **Round 3** (review findings): the harness now generates the initramfs and builds `/init`, the catalog has 18 cases (see [kernel tooling](kernel-tooling.md)), a missing `/init` ends in what the pinned kernel prints through `prepare_namespace`, configuration equivalence is a behavioural table, x87/SSE work in ring 3, and the ring-3 hardening gaps the independent reviews named are closed or listed. Still missing: any scheduler, `execve`, signals, other syscalls, SMP, CPU-vulnerability mitigations and physical hardware. This is a running first userspace in one QEMU cell, not Linux compatibility or M1/M2/M3 completion.

**Declared configuration** (one table, [`kernel/src/config.rs`](../kernel/src/config.rs): every entry cites the pinned source that defines the behaviour and lists the syscalls it turns into `-ENOSYS`): boot option `norandmaps` (no stack, brk or `arch_align_stack` randomisation, so `STACK_TOP = 0x7ffffffff000` exactly), `vdso=0` (no `AT_SYSINFO_EHDR`), `noxsave` (FXSAVE-only state, so `AT_MINSIGSTKSZ` is 0x5a0; **x87 and SSE still run**, as on a `noxsave` Linux), `nofsgsbase` (`AT_HWCAP2 = 0`, asserted at start-up because it is only true while CR4.FSGSBASE is clear); `CONFIG_RSEQ=n` (no `AT_RSEQ_*` and `rseq` 334 is `-ENOSYS`, `kernel/sys_ni.c:392`), `CONFIG_X86_USER_SHADOW_STACK=n` (`map_shadow_stack` 453), `CONFIG_UPROBES=n` (`uretprobe`/`uprobe` 335/336), `CONFIG_X86_X32_ABI=n`; `CONFIG_IA32_EMULATION=y` with the boot parameter `ia32_emulation=0` (`ia32_enabled()` false, `arch/x86/include/asm/ia32.h:84`): `AT_MINSIGSTKSZ` keeps the `sigframe_ia32` sizing of the config option (0x5a0, equal to the oracle) while, exactly as Linux does for `ia32_emulation=0`, no `int $0x80` gate is installed (`#GP`, `idt.c:233-239`), `SYSENTER` MSRs are zero and compat `SYSCALL` is the `-ENOSYS` `CSTAR` stub (`common.c:2230-2252`); a Linux booted without that parameter dispatches compat syscalls, which this kernel does not. Root credentials, no secure-exec. The pinned oracle (`target/linux-reference/init-reference-004`, local evidence, the exact harness-built init ELF) ran the reference configuration (RSEQ and UPROBES on) with randomisation and vDSO on; its `vdso=0` auxv line minus the two `AT_RSEQ_*` entries is the comparison. `AT_HWCAP` is the raw `CPUID.1:EDX`: equal to Linux's `x86_capability[CPUID_1_EDX]` on the `qemu64` model, where no capability is cleared or forced; other CPUs can differ, so only `qemu64` is compared exactly. `AT_HWCAP2` is 0 only without FSGSBASE: under `-cpu max` Linux would set `HWCAP2_FSGSBASE` and enable CR4.FSGSBASE, this kernel does neither.

### Mechanisms and their five design questions

**Exec loader** (`exec::plan`, `exec::args`, `userspace::load`; the archive replace chain is now the [VFS spine](#vfs-spine)'s unpacker). 1. *Preexisting crate?* `goblin`/`object`/`elf` parse ELF but not Linux's acceptance order, errnos or `elf_load` arithmetic, and none unpacks a cpio into a tree with Linux's replace rules; the existing borrowed [`image::elf`](../kernel/src/image/elf.rs) and [`image::cpio`](../kernel/src/image/cpio.rs) decoders are reused and `syscalls` supplies `Errno`. 2. *Bundle?* One plan type serves the loader, the page-content filler and the fault printer (`Vmas`); one archive walk serves discovery and the boot inspection. 3. *Simplify?* Static `ET_EXEC` only; static PIE and `PT_INTERP`, overlapping segments, mappings that end beyond EOF (Linux `SIGBUS`) and more than 16 `PT_LOAD` are explicit unsupported errors, never reinterpreted. 4. *Secure?* The ELF and archive are untrusted: all arithmetic wraps exactly where the C does or is checked, every `TASK_SIZE_MAX` bound of `__get_unmapped_area` and `load_elf_binary` is applied before any frame is allocated, `p_vaddr`/`p_offset` mismatches fail as `vm_mmap` does, and a 20,000-case corruption loop covers header and program-table bytes. `padzero` is applied only to writable segments, as in Linux (`clear_user` on a read-only private mapping fails and is ignored), so no kernel byte is invented or hidden. 5. *Improve?* Replace the fixed 16-segment table by a VMA tree with the first `mmap`/`brk`; add static PIE with ASLR base selection; map file pages lazily from the page cache instead of copying.

**Initial stack** (`exec::stack`). 1. *Crate?* None builds a Linux auxv. 2. *Bundle?* One builder writes the whole block into a caller-owned window (the top four stack pages, one order-2 frame), so the loader needs no second copy. 3. *Simplify?* Fixed 36-page stack (4 argument pages plus Linux's 32-page `stack_expand`); no growth on fault. 4. *Secure?* Every offset is checked against the window and fails as `-E2BIG`; the pointer arrays and strings are laid out as `create_elf_tables` does, so a hostile command line cannot move `rsp` outside the mapped pages. `AT_RANDOM` is RDRAND when CPUID reports it, otherwise a `SplitMix64` expansion of the TSC, which is predictable. 5. *Improve?* A kernel entropy pool, then growth up to `RLIMIT_STACK`.

**User address space** (`paging::Tables::map_user`, `paging::UserSpace`). 1. *Crate?* The `x86_64` crate migration is a separate branch; this reuses the existing owned table writer. 2. *Bundle?* The same `map` that built the kernel tree installs user leaves, with `USER` carried on every level of a user path and refused under a supervisor-only table; frames come from the one buddy allocator. 3. *Simplify?* No huge user pages, no PCID/global entries, frames are never freed (init's death panics the kernel). 4. *Secure?* All user table writes precede `Tables::activate`, so the existing "no software write after CR3 publication" invariant stands. Leaves are NX unless the segment is executable, writable only when requested, never at or above `TASK_SIZE_MAX`; module payload pages are copied into fresh zeroed frames while the loader mapping epoch is active and no payload borrow survives `userspace::load`. 5. *Improve?* Share the module payload copy-on-write instead of copying, once a page cache exists.

**User copy** (`usercopy`, `paging::UserSpace`). 1. *Crate?* None applicable in `no_std` kernel space. 2. *Bundle?* One pair `copy_to_user`/`copy_from_user` over one `UserPages` trait; syscalls, the stack check and a future `strncpy_from_user` use it, nothing else touches a user address. 3. *Simplify?* A software page-table walk plus the direct-map alias of the frame replaces exception-table fixups. 4. *Secure?* `access_ok` is Linux's `__access_ok` runtime branch (`ptr + len` without wrap and `<= TASK_SIZE_MAX`); each page must be present and user (and writable for writes) at every level before a byte moves; short copies return the uncopied count and `copy_from_user` zero-fills the tail. The kernel never dereferences a user virtual address, so SMAP cannot trap and no `STAC`/`CLAC` window exists; the module documents where one would go if a direct-access path is ever added. TOCTOU: one task on one CPU, user code does not run during a syscall, so nothing can unmap between walk and copy; this argument MUST be replaced (atomic walk-and-copy under an address-space lock, or fault fixups) before a second task, a second CPU or `munmap`/`mprotect`. 5. *Improve?* Fault-and-fixup copies avoid the software walk on the fast path.

**Ring-3 entry, SYSCALL, exceptions and FPU** (`cpu.rs`, `userspace.rs`). 1. *Crate?* The vendored `x86_64` 0.15.5 supplies the MSR/CR/DR/segment types (`Efer::update`, `Star::write`, `LStar`, `SFMask`, `Cr0/Cr4::update`, `Dr0..Dr7`, `DS/ES/FS/GS::set_reg`), wrapped in `unsafe fn`s with contracts because the crate marks `LStar::write`/`SFMask::write` safe although they can break the CPU; `raw-cpuid` reports SYSCALL/SEP/SMEP/SMAP/UMIP/FXSR/SSE; `core::arch`'s `_fxrstor64` is the only FP instruction. No crate provides the entry or `iretq` frame. 2. *Bundle?* One kernel stack (the `BOOT_STACK` top = `TSS.RSP0`) serves SYSCALL, exceptions and IRQs from ring 3; `kernel_main`'s frame is abandoned by `enter_user` (it never returns) and all later state is in `STATE` behind a `RawSpinLock`; any ring-3 CS (`cs & 3 == 3`, so compatibility mode too) takes the user path of the existing IDT entries. 3. *Simplify?* `syscall_entry` builds a Linux `pt_regs` and always returns by `iretq` (the SYSRET fast path needs canonical-RIP and RCX/R11 checks); the user RSP scratch is one global word (no `swapgs`, no per-CPU); every ring-3 exception is fatal (no handlers exist) so exceptions do not save a full `pt_regs`; the kernel is soft-float and there is one task, so user FP state is never touched by ring 0 and needs no context switch. 4. *Secure?* `SFMASK` is Linux's `MSR_SYSCALL_MASK`; `STAR`/`LSTAR` as Linux; `CSTAR` points at a `-ENOSYS` `sysret` stub except on Intel CPUs (Linux `wrmsrq_cstar`: ignored there, a `#VE` in a TDX guest); the three `SYSENTER` MSRs are zeroed (`idt_syscall_init`), so `SYSENTER` from ring 3 cannot enter ring 0 at a stale target; INT3 and INTO gates are DPL 3 (Linux `SYSG`), every other gate DPL 0; `#DB` has its own IST stack (Linux `IST_INDEX_DB`) and DR0-3/DR7 are scrubbed at boot (`initialize_debug_regs`); the window between `SYSCALL` and the stack switch can be entered only by IST vectors (NMI, `#MC`, `#DF`, `#DB`), which never touch the user RSP (the earlier "no interrupt can intervene" comment was wrong and is fixed); NMI, `#MC`, `#DF` and any unclassified vector taken from ring 3 are kernel failures, never init deaths; CR4.SMEP/SMAP/UMIP are enabled when CPUID reports them and `CHECK userspace exec` reports SMEP/SMAP as read back (`qemu64` has none, `-cpu max` both: the `cpu-max` case requires `true true`); FS/GS selectors and bases are zeroed like `start_thread`; x87/SSE are enabled as Linux does with `noxsave` (CR4.OSFXSR|OSXMMEXCPT, CR0.EM=TS=0, MP|NE set) and init starts from Linux's initial image (FCW 0x37f, MXCSR 0x1f80, registers zero, loaded by `FXRSTOR` so no firmware residue leaks); `#MF`/`#XM` reach init as SIGFPE. An executed audit (`CHECK userspace-audit`) walks the published tree and proves no entry of the kernel half carries the user bit and no user mapping reaches `TASK_SIZE_MAX`. 5. *Improve?* Per-CPU stack and `swapgs`, the SYSRET fast path, `CLAC` at exception entry once a direct user-access path exists, compat syscalls (`IA32_EMULATION=y`), UMIP emulation, XSAVE and lazy FP context switching with the scheduler.

**Syscall slice** (`syscall`). 1. *Crate?* `syscalls = "=0.8.1"` (no default features, `x86_64`) supplies `Sysno` and `Errno`; its x86_64 table was compared with the pinned `syscall_64.tbl` and differs only by 17 numbers Linux answers with `-ENOSYS` (kept in a sorted list with a drift test). Its raw `asm!` wrappers are not referenced: the linked `qemu-self-test` ELF contains no `syscall` instruction (`objdump -d` count 0), though `Errno`'s name table is linked in through `Debug` formatting. 2. *Bundle?* One `dispatch` and one `write` path for fds 0-2 (init's `/dev/console`). 3. *Simplify?* `write`, `getpid` (always 1), `exit`, `exit_group`; a number Linux implements but this slice does not returns `Outcome::Unimplemented` and the caller prints `FAIL syscall unimplemented nr=<n>` and stops - neither fake success nor a broad `ENOSYS`. 4. *Secure?* Number and `unsigned int` arguments are truncated as in Linux; `write` follows `vfs_write` (fd `-EBADF`, `access_ok` on the unclamped count `-EFAULT`, clamp to `MAX_RW_COUNT`) and `iterate_tty_write` (2048-byte chunks; a chunk that cannot be fully copied is dropped, returning earlier chunks or `-EFAULT`). Output processing is the default termios (`OPOST|ONLCR`: `\n` becomes `\r\n`); the oracle's serial log shows exactly that. 5. *Improve?* Table-driven argument decoding once more than a handful of syscalls exist.

**Fault and death reporting** (`exec::report`). 1. *Crate?* No. 2. *Bundle?* One `Display` per Linux line; the VMA bracket is shared by the segfault and trap lines. 3. *Simplify?* The panic backtrace and the following `Code:` line are not printed; CPU/core/socket are fixed to the BSP. 4. *Secure?* The exit code is the signal number with no core flag; kernel addresses in a user fault always show the `PROT` bit (`sanitize_error_code`); a vector that is not a plain user signal (NMI, `#DF`, `#MC`, `#NM`) returns `None` so it can never be misreported as init's death. 5. *Improve?* Real signal delivery, `Code:` bytes and `wait` status.
