| Item | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `/init` found with initramfs semantics (order, full replacement incl. an empty body, spellings, `-EISDIR` names, skipped modules, non-regular, hard link, the four fallback paths, `#!` scripts) | `vfs::tests` (unpacker replace chain, spellings, symlinked and replaced `init`), `exec::script` tests; the `no-init`, `bad-module`, `empty-module` and `success` cases | done |
| ELF acceptance and errors in Linux order, hostile inputs | `exec::plan` tests incl. the oracle's exact program table and a corruption loop | phase 1 done; `ET_DYN`/`PT_INTERP`/overlap/`SIGBUS` pages explicit unsupported |
| Stack and auxv byte-exact, `AT_MINSIGSTKSZ` derivation | `exec::stack` tests (hand-computed layout, length matrix, oracle auxv order/values) | phase 1 done |
| Address space built before CR3, usercopy on owned tables | informal QEMU: `CHECK userspace-image ok` after `Tables::activate` | phase 1 done (informal run, see below) |
| `write`/`getpid`/`exit*` semantics, ENOSYS table | `syscall` tests | phase 1 done; entry path phase 2 done |
| Death/segfault/trap text | `exec::report` tests against the oracle's literal line | text done; IDT wiring done (phase 2) |
| Ring-3 entry, `SYSCALL` entry, exceptions from ring 3, SMEP/SMAP/UMIP, FPU | catalog cases `success`, `init-segfault`, `sse`, `cpu-max` (`smep=true smap=true`), 12 manual tiny inits, the oracle's `sse` run | done in one QEMU cell |
| `Run /init as init process`, `CHECK userspace exec`, `RESULT`/`FAIL init exitcode`, `NO_INIT` and `Failed to execute /init (error N)` paths | catalog cases `success`, `no-init`, `unimplemented-syscall`, `rseq`, the framebuffer capture of the default build, manual wrong-machine/shebang/fallback runs | done; the harness generates `/init` |

### Shortcuts (each is also a `shortcut:` comment at its site)

Loader and ABI: only `--` words reach `argv` (no `init=`/`rdinit=`/command-line environment, and "Too many boot init vars" is a plain `FAIL`); `/init` entries with `..` components are ignored and a hard-linked `/init` is rejected; **a damaged archive is skipped whole, whereas Linux keeps the entries it unpacked before the error (so Linux may find an `/init` this kernel never sees)**; the file's owner and setuid bits are ignored; static `ET_EXEC` only, at most 16 `PT_LOAD`, no overlap, no mapping past EOF; a `#!` `/init` gives `-ENOEXEC`/`-ENOENT` as `load_script` and an explicit unsupported failure when its interpreter exists; a `PT_INTERP` is looked up in the archives: absent is `-ENOENT` as `open_exec`, present is the explicit "unsupported dynamic executable" stop; if `/init` fails to execute while one of Linux's four fallback inits (`/sbin/init`, `/etc/init`, `/bin/init`, `/bin/sh`) exists in the archives, the kernel stops with `FAIL fallback init present` instead of running it (with none present it prints Linux's lines and `NO_INIT`); with no `/init` the kernel prints what the pinned reference kernel prints through `prepare_namespace` minus the configuration-dependent filesystem list, not `NO_INIT`; fixed 36-page stack and no growth; no randomisation; `PF_X`/`PF_W` without `PF_R` read too; `AT_RANDOM` falls back to a predictable seed without RDRAND (note before any stack protector or static PIE); frames of a failed load are not returned.

Process model: **a syscall Linux implements but this slice does not stops the whole kernel (`FAIL syscall unimplemented nr=N`) - acceptable only for a single trusted init, since a user must never be able to stop the kernel; it must become the real syscall or an error returned to that task with a second process**; syscalls turned off by the declared configuration return `-ENOSYS`; no signals, so every exception is init's death; compat syscalls, `int $0x80` and `vsyscall` are absent; UMIP is enabled but, unlike Linux, `SGDT`/`SIDT`/`SMSW` are not emulated and kill init; the user RSP scratch word and the kernel stack are single-CPU (no `swapgs`/per-CPU); `AC` set by ring 3 is not cleared at exception entry (harmless while no kernel path touches user virtual addresses; add `CLAC` with the first direct access); no FP context switch (one task, soft-float kernel).

### Blind spots that block physical use

None of Linux's CPU-vulnerability mitigations exist: no KPTI/PTI (ring 3 runs with the whole kernel mapped in the same CR3, so Meltdown-class CPUs leak), no IBRS/eIBRS/STIBP/IBPB or retpolines, no RSB filling or call-depth tracking, no `VERW`/MDS/TAA/MMIO buffer clearing on return to user, no SRSO/BHI handling, no SSBD, no `swapgs` fences. Together with SMP, per-CPU data, KASLR, stack protection, NMI/`#MC` recovery, and independent architecture/security review, these gate any physical run of ring 3; the physical diagnostic authority in [diagnostic handoff](diagnostic-handoff.md) covers none of it.

### Linux sources read (6.18.57, commit `2ff600d29ee478d0a8d46ef8a58afb5e038fe692`)

`fs/binfmt_elf.c`, `fs/exec.c`, `mm/vma_exec.c`, `mm/mmap.c`, `mm/util.c`, `init/main.c`, `init/initramfs.c`, `lib/cmdline.c`, `kernel/params.c`, `lib/ctype.c`, `kernel/exit.c`, `kernel/panic.c`, `arch/x86/entry/syscall_64.c`, `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl`, `arch/x86/include/asm/{elf,sigframe,uaccess_64,page_64_types}.h`, `arch/x86/include/uapi/asm/sigcontext.h`, `arch/x86/kernel/{signal,traps,process}.c`, `arch/x86/kernel/fpu/signal.c`, `arch/x86/mm/fault.c`, `fs/read_write.c`, `drivers/tty/{tty_io,n_tty}.c`, `mm/memory.c` (`print_vma_addr`). Per-file notices and transformation records are in the new source headers.

### Evidence actually produced

**Final round-3 tree** (branch head after merging `origin/main` 448407a, PR #9's harness): `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo +nightly clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked --offline -- -D warnings`; `cargo +nightly test --workspace --locked --offline` (kernel 121, xtask 70, baremetal 10, two doc tests); `cargo +1.88.0 clippy -p xtask -p baremetal --all-targets --locked --offline -- -D warnings` and `cargo +1.88.0 test -p xtask -p baremetal --locked --offline`; both target-specific strict Clippy commands (`qemu-self-test` and `freestanding`, fresh target directories `p3-clip-self-2`/`p3-clip-def-2`, with the cargo-home config of `kernel-build --output target/kernel-tooling/p3-build-001`); `xtask check` and `status` (14 `in_progress`, 2,609 `not_started`); and the full maintained catalog `env PATH="/bin:$PATH" cargo +1.88.0 run -p xtask --locked --offline -- kernel-test --output target/kernel-tooling/p3-test-003 --timeout-seconds 60`, which matched **18/18** (`p3-test-001` and `p3-test-002`, earlier runs of the same inputs, also 18/18). The success-type cases `success`, `empty-module`, `bad-module`, `sse`, `rseq` and `cpu-max` ran the generated `/init` (sha256 `0daf382f...d8fb`) to `RESULT success`; `init-segfault`, `no-init` and `unimplemented-syscall` printed the Linux texts listed in [kernel tooling](kernel-tooling.md); the default-boot framebuffer capture of `p3-test-003` shows the `no-root` lines (human review of the PNG is still the harness's own caveat).

ELF review of both `p3-test-003` profiles (self-test sha256 `9ae56c8d...8d6a`, default `18204fca...4d19`): `nm -u` empty; exactly one FP/SIMD instruction in each, `fxrstor64` of `INIT_FXSTATE` in `init_user_fpu`, and no xmm/ymm/zmm/mm/x87 operand, `fninit`, `xsave` or `ldmxcsr` anywhere else; two compiler-generated `rep movsq` as before; `wrmsr` 13 (8 `enable_syscalls`, 3 `enter_user`, 1 `init_user_fpu`, 1 `start_timer`), one `mov dr6`, `iretq` 14, `sysretd` 1, no `swapgs`/`syscall`/`sysenter`/`sysretq`.

**Oracle** `target/linux-reference/init-reference-004` (README there; `oracle-noroot.log` retains the no-`/init` boot of the pinned kernel that the `no-init` case's text is taken from): pinned 6.18.57 under QEMU TCG `-cpu qemu64` booted the exact harness-built init and archive in `normal`, `vdso=0`, `-- fault`, `-- sse` and `vdso=0 -- sse`; the harness's expected summary line, the candidate's `success` line and its `sse` line each equal the `vdso=0` oracle line minus the two `AT_RSEQ_*` entries (script-checked). Linux passes the `sse` mode, so the initial FP state and the x87/SSE2 results are Linux's, not only this kernel's.

**Manual QEMU runs** (not harness evidence, one run each, logs and inputs under `target/manual-p3/` and the earlier `manual-p2/`, kernel `final-kernel.sha256`): twelve hand-made single-segment inits on the final self-test ELF (`exit(3)` -> `0x00000300`; `ud2` -> `trap invalid opcode`, 4; `#DE` -> 8; `int3` -> `trap int3`, 5; `hlt` and `int $0x80` -> `general protection fault`, 11; write to the text page -> segfault `error 7`; jump to 0 -> `error 14`; `getpid`+`exit` -> `0x00000100`; `sysenter` -> `#UD` under QEMU; non-canonical `rsp` -> `#GP` under QEMU, `#SS` on hardware; unimplemented `read` -> `FAIL syscall unimplemented nr=0`), a `#!/bin/nothing` `/init` (`Failed to execute /init (error -2)` then `NO_INIT`), a dynamic `/init` whose `PT_INTERP` is absent (`error -2`) or present (`FAIL userspace error=Load(Unsupported(Interpreter))`), a wrong-machine `/init` with `/bin/sh` also in the archive (`FAIL fallback init present path=/bin/sh unsupported`), and an archive without `/init`. Earlier phase-2 runs with a previous init build covered `-cpu max` SMEP/SMAP and the wrong-machine and mode-0644 `/init` cases (now also catalog or unit cases).

Blind spots: no SMP, no physical hardware, no CPU-vulnerability mitigations (see above), `syscall32_ignore` and the `#DB` IST/DR7 scrub never executed against a real compat-mode `SYSCALL` or breakpoint, SMEP/SMAP violation behaviour not exercised, `AT_HWCAP` compared exactly only for `qemu64`, TCG only, no performance data, and the independent reviews of PR #8 are source/disassembly reviews, not qualification.

## Oracle syscall test suite

Worktree `oracle-tests`, branch `test/oracle-syscall-suite`. The syscall slices that follow (memory, identity, time, signals, files, processes) need exact targets, so the tests were written **first** and proven on the pinned Linux 6.18.57 (`target/linux-reference/artifacts/bzImage`, reference config), which is the oracle. Nothing here runs on this kernel yet: a mode stops at `FAIL syscall unimplemented` until its syscalls land, and the implementing slices add the `kernel-test` cases (the catalog is untouched; the existing 18 cases still match, `r2-gate`).

**Interface.** `user/init` takes the group as `argv[1]` (`-- mm`, `-- ids`, `-- time`, `-- signal`, `-- fs`, `-- proc` on the kernel command line, like `fault`/`sse`/`rseq`/`mmap`, whose output is unchanged). Source: `user/init/src/tests/{mod,mm,ids,time,signal,fs,proc}.rs`; libc-free on the already-admitted `syscalls` crate, no allocation, no new dependency. Each check prints one line, `init: test <group>.<name> ok` or `init: test <group>.<name> FAIL got=<v> want=<v>`; a failing check does not stop the group; the group ends with `init: tests <group> passed=N failed=M`, then `init: ok` (or exit status 1 if anything failed). `fs` also prints one `init: info fs.getdents-d_off [...]` line. Result lines are written with one `write(2)`; kernel `printk` lines can still interleave with them on the console (observed once in `init-reference-005/oracle-proc-kvm.log`, not in 006), so a consumer keeps only lines starting `init: test `, `init: tests ` or `init: info `.

**Expected output.** `xtask/oracle/expected.tsv` (`group kind name line`; exposed as `ORACLE_EXPECTED` next to the case catalog in `xtask/src/kernel.rs`, with a unit test that the summary counts equal the check lines and names are unique). Taken from the TCG run of `init-reference-006`; the kvm and the repeated TCG runs produced byte-identical test lines for every group except, in the first capture, the one printk interleaving above.

**Fixture.** `xtask/src/fixture.rs::TEST_FILES` adds `fixture/` (dir 0755), `hello.txt` (0644, `hello, oracle\n`), `empty`, `noexec` (0755 text, `ENOEXEC`), `sub/nested.txt`, `link` -> `hello.txt` to the generated initramfs. The expected `CHECK image inspected` tail is computed from the table (`records=11 files=10 regular=5 symlinks=2`); the 18 cases matched with it.

### Checks per group (oracle: all pass, TCG `qemu64`, KVM `host`, repeated TCG)

| Group | Checks | Covers |
|---|---:|---|
| `mm` | 121 | `brk` (start = page-aligned `_end`, grow/shrink/unaligned/regrow-zero, below-start, wall of an existing vma, huge), anonymous `mmap` (top-down placement at exactly `0x7ffff7fff000 - len`, adjacency, hint honoured/rounded down/occupied/low, `MAP_32BIT`, `MAP_FIXED` replace + zero, past/straddling `TASK_SIZE`, `MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE` EEXIST cases, `PROT_NONE`/read-only, flags, EINVAL/ENOMEM/EBADF matrix), `munmap` (head/tail/split/hole-spanning, EINVAL cases), `mprotect` (split, EINVAL/ENOMEM, partial effect before ENOMEM), zero fill and read-back, kernel-side access via pipe EFAULT probes |
| `ids` | 157 | uid/gid/pid/tid/ppid/pgid/sid, `getres*`, `getgroups`, `set_tid_address`, `set_robust_list`, `uname` (all six fields, NUL padding), `arch_prctl` (FS/GS set/get round trips, non-canonical EPERM, EFAULT, invalid codes, `ARCH_GET_CPUID`), `prlimit64`/`getrlimit` for all 16 resources + set/EPERM/EINVAL/ESRCH/EFAULT, `umask`, `getcwd`, `sched_{get,set}affinity`, `sched_get_priority_*`, `sysinfo`, `prctl` name/dumpable/timerslack, `personality`, `getpriority` |
| `time` | 118 | `clock_gettime` for ids 0-11 + invalid, orderings, `clock_getres` exact (1 ns hrtimer clocks, 1 ms coarse), `clock_settime` EINVAL, `gettimeofday`, `time`, `times`, `nanosleep` and `clock_nanosleep` (relative, absolute future/past, EINVAL/EFAULT/EOPNOTSUPP matrix), a successful `clock_settime` followed by `time`/`REALTIME_COARSE` and the answered-at-once CPU-clock sleeps (added after review, `init-reference-007`) |
| `signal` | 201 | `rt_sigaction` (defaults, round trips, mask stripping, EINVAL/EFAULT, install-before-EFAULT), `rt_sigprocmask`/`rt_sigpending`, `kill`/`tkill`/`tgkill`/`rt_sigqueueinfo`/`rt_tgsigqueueinfo` and their errors, `siginfo` fields, pending/coalescing/real-time queueing/ordering, `SA_NODEFER`/`SA_RESETHAND`/`sa_mask`, frame (return address = restorer, `rsp % 16 == 8`), `rt_sigreturn` restoring all 15 registers + flags and honouring a patched context/mask, hardware faults (`SIGSEGV` MAPERR/ACCERR/NX/non-canonical with `si_addr`, error code, trapno, cr2; `SIGFPE`, `SIGILL`, `SIGTRAP`), `sigaltstack` (query/set/errors/`SA_ONSTACK`/`SS_AUTODISARM`), `rt_sigtimedwait`, `rt_sigsuspend`, `pause`, `nanosleep`/`read` interruption and `SA_RESTART`, pid 1 ignoring unhandled signals |
| `fs` | 284 | `/dev/console` on fds 0-2 (char 5:1, flags, tty ioctls including the 36 `TCGETS` bytes of the ttyS0 console), `open`/`openat`/`read`/`pread64`/`readv`/`preadv`/`lseek`/`close`, `stat`/`lstat`/`fstat`/`newfstatat`/`statx`/`access`/`faccessat2` with every deterministic field, `getdents64` (order, `d_type`, `d_ino`, cookie seek, errors), error matrix (ENOENT, ENOTDIR, EISDIR, EBADF, EFAULT, ENAMETOOLONG, ELOOP, EMFILE, EEXIST), `dup`/`dup2`/`dup3`, `fcntl`, `chdir`/`fchdir`, `readlink`, create/write/`ftruncate`/`unlink` on the rootfs, file-backed `mmap`, `mknod` + `/dev/null`, `/dev/zero`, `/dev/full` |
| `proc` | 135 | `fork` (pid, ppid, tid, pgid in the child), `wait4` status encoding (exit codes incl. wrap, killed by signal, stopped `0x137f`, continued `0xffff`), WNOHANG, rusage, EFAULT still reaps, `waitid` (siginfo fields, WNOWAIT, errors), orphan adoption by pid 1, `SIGCHLD` (queued, handler info, `SIG_IGN`/`SA_NOCLDWAIT` auto-reap), `setsid`/`setpgid`, `execve` of `/init execd` (argv/envp, fd inheritance and `O_CLOEXEC`, signal dispositions, mask, umask, cwd; stdout captured through a pipe and compared whole) + failure errnos, `pipe2` (capacity 65536, `F_SETPIPE_SZ`, EPIPE/SIGPIPE, `FIONREAD`, `poll`) |

### What the oracle showed (all recorded as exact checks)

- **Initramfs boot.** The rootfs is tmpfs: root mode `01777`, `nlink = 2 + subdirectories`, directory size `40 + 20 * entries`, `getdents64` lists the newest entry first. `default_rootfs` (`init/noinitramfs.c`) leaves `/dev` (empty but for `/dev/console`, char 5:1, mode 0600) and `/root` (0700); **no devtmpfs is mounted**, so `/dev/null`, `/dev/zero` and the rest do not exist until `mknod`. Descriptors 0-2 are `/dev/console` opened by the kernel: `F_GETFL` is `O_RDWR` without `O_LARGEFILE`. Archive mtime 0 is applied; atime/ctime are the unpack time (not checked).
- **Memory.** Mmap top with `vdso=0 norandmaps` is `0x7ffff7fff000`; x86 rounds a non-fixed hint *down*; `do_mmap` does consult `mmap_min_addr` (`round_hint_to_min` raises a low non-fixed hint, `security_mmap_addr` checks fixed addresses with a CAP_SYS_RAWIO exemption), but the reference config's `CONFIG_DEFAULT_MMAP_MIN_ADDR=4096` makes `mmap(0x1000)` succeed without exercising either, so those two checks pin the value 4096 only; `mmap` ignores unknown protection bits, `mprotect` does not; `mprotect` over a hole changes the leading part, then reports ENOMEM; `brk` refuses to come within one page of the next vma.
- **Signals.** Handlers of pending, unmasked signals run newest-frame first (all frames are pushed before the first handler runs); `SA_RESETHAND` resets only the handler; a faulting `oact`/`oldset` pointer is reported after the new action/mask is installed; `SS_ONSTACK` is accepted as input; `uc_stack` records the interrupted context; `int3` and a general-protection fault are `SI_KERNEL` (0x80) with no address; PROT_NONE and untouched read-only pages fault with error code 4 and 6, a present read-only page with 7; pid 1 silently ignores `SIGKILL`, `SIGSTOP`, `SIGTERM` it has no handler for.
- **Time.** `clock_getres` reports 1 ns only after the hrtimer high-resolution switch (about 1.4 s into boot under TCG), 1 ms before; the test waits (bounded at 10 s). `clock_nanosleep` on the raw/coarse clocks is EOPNOTSUPP, on `CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID` EOPNOTSUPP. The `REALTIME_ALARM`/`BOOTTIME_ALARM` clocks work (QEMU has an RTC).
- **Wait.** `__WCLONE` (`0x80000000`) is a valid `wait4` option, so the EINVAL check uses `0x10`; a `wait4` whose status pointer faults has already reaped the child; `wait4(INT_MIN)` is ESRCH; `waitid` with nothing ready under WNOHANG returns 0 with `si_signo` 0.

### Environment-dependent items (and how they are handled)

Held on both accelerators: everything above. Configuration the checks assume (declare it in the candidate): `mmap_min_addr` 4096; the init runs as root with the default capability set; `RLIMIT_CORE` 0 (a core-dumping signal reports status 6/11 without the core bit); NX enabled (instruction-fetch fault error codes `0x14`/`0x15`); `HZ=1000`; one CPU; an RTC. Handled rather than removed: `uc_flags` is compared without `UC_FP_XSTATE` (xsave is CPU-model dependent; the candidate declares `noxsave`); `RLIMIT_NPROC`/`RLIMIT_SIGPENDING` (derived from RAM) are checked only as `cur == max > 0`; `sysinfo` RAM only in range; `sched_getaffinity` assumes `-smp 1`; the 1 ms coarse tick assumes the reference `HZ=1000`; the alarm clocks assume an RTC; the `mmap_min_addr` rounding of a hint below the minimum is **not exercised** (it needs a sysctl write, i.e. `/proc`); `getdents64` `d_off` values are printed as an `info` line, not asserted (the file lists them, but a harness case must match only `check` and `summary` rows exactly). Removed after the oracle disagreed with the first draft: `clock_nanosleep(CLOCK_PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID)` (sleeps forever, the clock does not advance while sleeping), devtmpfs-node checks (replaced by `mknod`), atime. Not covered: `vfork`, threads/`clone`, `SA_ONSTACK` fault delivery on a bad stack, `ptrace`, `int $0x80` (reference `IA32_EMULATION=y`, candidate `ia32_emulation=0`), `RSEQ` (candidate `CONFIG_RSEQ=n`), ELF interpreter paths, writable shared mappings, `/proc` and `/sys` (not mounted).

### Answers to the five design questions

*Preexisting crate?* The admitted `syscalls` crate supplies raw calls and `Sysno`/`Errno`; nothing else is needed. *Bundle?* One framework (`tests/mod.rs`: `T`, `sys`, `Probe`) shared by six groups; eight small `naked_asm!` stubs in `signal.rs` (restorer, entry probe, register probe, five fault probes; the `proc` group reuses the one restorer), each with the AGENTS.md per-site proof and a disassembly excerpt of the built init in `init-reference-006/stubs.objdump.txt` (compiler evidence for the x86-64 `x86_64-unknown-none` release build only). *Simplify?* Counters over generated name tables; no allocation, no formatting machinery beyond `core::fmt`. *Secure?* The tests deliberately pass hostile pointers/lengths; the module header states the one unsafe boundary (`sys`), and a kernel bug is expected to show as a failed check or a stop, never silently. *Improve?* Each implementing slice adds `kernel-test` cases that diff against `ORACLE_EXPECTED`; the `info`/environment items above are the first candidates for pinning once the matching kernel mechanism exists.

### Evidence and commands

`target/linux-reference/init-reference-006/` (local evidence, not committed; `init-reference-005` is the first capture of the suite before the review fixes, kept with an erratum): `init`/`initramfs.cpio` = the artifacts of the branch's gates `kernel-test` run (`<worktree>/target/kernel-tooling/<run>/init-build/init.elf`; hashes in `SHA256SUMS`), `oracle-<mode>-{tcg,tcg-repeat,kvm}.{cmd,log}` per group, `oracle-{base,fault,sse}-tcg.*` showing the existing modes unchanged, `stubs.objdump.txt`, `SHA256SUMS`, `README.md`. Command (TCG; the KVM runs use `-accel kvm -cpu host`):

```
timeout 90 qemu-system-x86_64 -machine q35 -accel tcg -cpu qemu64 -m 512M -smp 1 -kernel target/linux-reference/artifacts/bzImage -initrd initramfs.cpio -append 'console=ttyS0 panic=-1 vdso=0 norandmaps -- mm' -nographic -no-reboot
```

Results: 121/121, 157/157, 108/108, 201/201, 283/283, 135/135 (1,005 checks) on TCG, the repeat and KVM (the `time` group has since grown to 118 checks, re-recorded in `init-reference-007`; the `fs` group has since gained `console-tcgets-termios`: 284/284 on TCG with the pinned reference kernel and the harness-built `/init` of `v5`, the 284 expected lines compared line for line, see [VFS spine, review round 1](#review-round-1)); `base`, `fault` and `sse` print what `init-reference-004` recorded. Blind spots: one reference build and one host; TCG/KVM only (no physical machine); single CPU; the oracle is a reference-configured Linux, so every value is "Linux 6.18.57 under that configuration", not a statement about other configurations.

## Signal model

Roadmap `WP-M3.4` (signals and FPU state), first slice: Linux 6.18.57 signal semantics for **x86_64, one single-threaded process, FXSR-only FPU state** (the declared `noxsave` configuration), as pure host-tested library code in [`kernel/src/signal/`](../kernel/src/signal/mod.rs). Branch `feat/signal-model`. It was first merged unwired; **it is wired by the [signal wiring](#signal-wiring) slice** (`ws-kernel-signal-c` is routed `in_progress`, see the [routing table](#routed-implementation-status); `ws-arch-x86-kernel` was already active). The checklist below is the spec that slice implemented.

### What exists

| Module | Linux source (6.18.57) | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `signal` (mod) | `include/linux/signal.h`, `signal_types.h`, `asm-generic/signal-defs.h`, `asm/ptrace.h`, `asm/segment.h` | `SigSet`, `SigAction` (32-byte kernel `struct sigaction`, `UAPI_SA_FLAGS`), `AltStack`, `Regs` (the `pt_regs` field order of `cpu::PtRegs`), the `Cpu` trait (FXSAVE image, `mxcsr_mask`, FXRSTOR, FPU reset, the `lar` test of `force_valid_ss`) |
| `signal::info` | `asm-generic/siginfo.h`, `kernel/signal.c:3500-3578`, `arch/x86/kernel/traps.c`, `arch/x86/mm/fault.c`, `fpu/core.c` | exact 128-byte `siginfo_t` (`SigInfo::to_bytes`), `siginfo_layout`/`known_siginfo_layout`, the `siginfo` plus `trap_nr`/`error_code`/`cr2` of each user exception (`exception`, `page_fault`, `fpu_exception_code`) |
| `signal::state` | `kernel/signal.c` send side | `State` = `sighand` + `signal_struct` + task fields: actions, blocked mask, saved mask, private and shared pending sets with `siginfo` queues, altstack, `SIGNAL_UNKILLABLE`, group exit; `prepare_signal`, `legacy_queue`, `complete_signal`, `__send_signal_locked`, `force_sig_info_to_task`, `dequeue_synchronous_signal`, `dequeue_signal`, `next_signal` |
| `signal::syscalls` | `kernel/signal.c` syscalls | `rt_sigaction`, `rt_sigprocmask`, `rt_sigpending`, `sigaltstack` (+ `sigaltstack_size_valid`), `kill`, `tkill`, `tgkill`, `rt_sigqueueinfo`, `rt_tgsigqueueinfo`, each with Linux's check order and `-errno` |
| `signal::frame` | `arch/x86/kernel/signal_64.c`, `signal.c`, `fpu/signal.c`, `asm/sigframe.h` | the `rt_sigframe` (440 bytes: `pretcode`, `ucontext` 304, `siginfo` 128), `get_sigframe`, FXSAVE image with `_fpx_sw_bytes`, `x64_setup_rt_frame`, `rt_sigreturn` with `restore_sigcontext`, `restore_altstack`, `force_valid_ss`, `fpu__restore_sig` |
| `signal::deliver` | `kernel/signal.c:2885-3174`, `arch/x86/kernel/signal.c:151-253` | `get_signal` (death mark, synchronous first, `SIG_IGN`, handler + `SA_RESETHAND`, default ignore / `SIGNAL_UNKILLABLE` / stop / fatal), `handle_signal`, the `ERESTART*` rules as functions of a `Regs`, `signal_delivered`, `do_signal_or_restart`, `handle_pending_signals` (the exit-to-user loop) |

Frame facts reproduced: `rsp` at handler entry is `round_down(buf_fx - 440, 16) - 8` with `buf_fx = round_down(sp - 128 - 512, 64)` (so `(rsp + 8) % 16 == 0` and the FXSAVE image sits below the frame); `uc_flags = UC_SIGCONTEXT_SS | UC_STRICT_RESTORE_SS` (no `UC_FP_XSTATE` without XSAVE); `uc_stack` stores the *stored* altstack flags, not a computed `SS_ONSTACK`; `fpstate` points at the 64-byte aligned 512-byte image whose bytes 464..512 are `_fpx_sw_bytes { magic1 = 0x46505853, extended_size = 516, xfeatures = 0, xstate_size = 512 }` (xfeatures 0 because `fpu_user_cfg.default_features` is never set when XSAVE is off, `fpu/xstate.c:808-813`; **confirmed on the pinned kernel** by the frame dump in `target/linux-reference/sigframe-probe-001/`, see below); bytes Linux never stores (4 padding bytes of `stack_t`, `sigcontext.reserved1`, `siginfo` without `SA_SIGINFO`) are left as they were; registers on entry `rdi=sig rsi=&info rdx=&uc rax=0 rip=handler rsp=frame cs=0x33`, `ss` kept only if `lar` accepts it, `rflags &= ~(DF|RF|TF)`; `SA_RESTORER` is mandatory (`-EFAULT` becomes forced SIGSEGV; `force_fatal_sig` when the failing signal is SIGSEGV); the handler runs with `blocked | sa_mask | sig` (no `sig` with `SA_NODEFER`); `SS_AUTODISARM` resets the altstack for the handler and `rt_sigreturn` re-arms it. `rt_sigreturn` order: range check, read mask and flags, **install mask**, restore altstack (all errors but `EFAULT` squashed, so it is a no-op while on the stack), registers (`cs|3`, `ss|3`, `FIX_EFLAGS` only, `orig_ax = -1`, `force_valid_ss` unless `UC_STRICT_RESTORE_SS`), FPU (null resets, unaligned or reserved-MXCSR image fails); any failure leaves the earlier steps applied, forces SIGSEGV and returns 0.

The `AT_MINSIGSTKSZ` question: `sigaltstack` compares only against `MINSIGSTKSZ` (2048); `sigaltstack_size_valid` is `true` unless the FPU state is dynamically sized or `strict_sas_size` is on (neither under `noxsave`), so 0x5a0 is **not** a `sigaltstack` limit here. `frame::MAX_FRAME_SIZE` derives 0x5a0 (`sigframe_ia32` 732 + 15 + 624 + 63, rounded to 16; the oracle prints `signal: max sigframe size: 1440`) and the strict rule `size > 0x5a0 - 624 + 512` is implemented behind a constant.

### Interface for the wiring step

Done by the [signal wiring](#signal-wiring) slice (one deliberate difference: `Cpu`'s `lar` is the one new `asm!` site; `Task::Pages` is a by-value view instead of `&mut impl UserPages`). The original checklist:

1. Make `cpu::PtRegs` and `signal::Regs` one type (the field lists are identical; today it would be a field-wise copy).
2. Implement `signal::Cpu` in the binary: `fxsave`/`fxrstor` as the 64-bit forms (`_fxsave64`/`_fxrstor64`, i.e. `fxsaveq`/`fxrstorq`, as Linux), `mxcsr_mask` from the FXSAVE area's `MXCSR_MASK` (or the architectural default `0xffbf` when it is zero), `fpu_reset` as the existing `init_user_fpu`, `ss_is_valid_user_data` as `lar` with Linux's masking: `ar & (AR_DPL_MASK | AR_S | AR_P | AR_TYPE_MASK)` must be DPL 3, S, P and type `RWDATA` or `RWDATA_EXPDOWN`, i.e. raw descriptor types 2, 3, 6, 7 (`AR_TYPE_MASK` is bits 9-11, so the accessed bit is ignored); a failing `lar` counts as 0.
3. Own one `signal::State` in the process (`State::new_init(rlimit)` for pid 1), pass `&mut impl UserPages` (the one `usercopy` trait, as `syscall::Context` already provides) and `regs.sp` where documented.
4. Dispatch `rt_sigaction`, `rt_sigprocmask`, `rt_sigpending`, `sigaltstack`, `kill`, `tkill`, `tgkill`, `rt_sigqueueinfo`, `rt_tgsigqueueinfo` to the `sys_*` functions (arguments are the raw registers; `sys_kill` returns `None` for `pid == 0` or `-1`, which must stop loudly as an unimplemented syscall) and `rt_sigreturn` to `frame::rt_sigreturn`; replace the ring-3 exception path by `State::force_fault(&info::exception(..)/page_fault(..))`.
5. On every return to ring 3 call `deliver::handle_pending_signals` (it loops like `exit_to_user_mode_loop`) and act on `Outcome::Exit` (`do_exit(exit_code)`: init's death is the existing `InitDeath` panic with `exit_code`), `Stop` (not modelled: treat as unimplemented), `Resume`/`Handled`. A handler entry or `rt_sigreturn` changes `rip`/`rsp`/`rflags`/`cs`/`ss` to arbitrary values, so such a return must use `iretq`, never `sysret`. Store the syscall result in `regs.ax` *before* calling, so `ERESTART*` results are visible, and set `regs.orig_ax = nr` on syscall entry and `-1` on exceptions (`syscall_get_nr` compares the low 32 bits with -1). `frame::rt_sigreturn` must also, as `restore_sigcontext` does first of all, make any pending `restart_syscall` return `-EINTR` (`restart_block.fn = do_no_restart_syscall`); this model has no restart block, so the first user of `restart_syscall` (`nanosleep`, `poll`, `futex`) owns that line and must add it there.

### Mechanisms and their five design questions

**Send side and delivery** (`state`, `deliver`). 1. *Preexisting crate?* No: the crate report finds none with kernel signal-queue semantics; `bitflags` would only wrap constants that must stay plain `u64` masks, because `rt_sigaction` truncates with `UAPI_SA_FLAGS` and reports raw bits. 2. *Bundle?* One `State` holds what Linux spreads over `sighand`, `signal_struct` and `task_struct`; one `send_signal` path serves kill, tkill, `rt_sigqueueinfo`, forced faults and future kernel senders; delivery is one `get_signal`. 3. *Simplify?* One thread: private and shared sets are both kept (the observable differences are `tkill` versus `kill` and the private-first order), `wants_signal` is "not blocked", no wakeups, job control, ptrace, posix timers, signalfd, cgroup freezer or `real_blocked`; queued `siginfo` lives in two 64-entry arrays (no heap needed). 4. *Secure?* SIGKILL/SIGSTOP cannot be blocked, caught or ignored (`sig_kernel_only`), nor delivered to the global init; every signal number is range-checked as an `int` before use; `rt_sigqueueinfo` cannot forge `SI_USER` or `SI_TKILL` for another pid and refuses non-zero expansion bytes for unknown layouts (`E2BIG`); queue storage is bounded and a full queue loses the `siginfo`, never memory safety (`#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`). 5. *Improve?* The pending sets are single `u64`s so `next_signal`, `sigpending` and flushes are branch-light, and coalesced standard signals allocate nothing. The heap-backed queue and measured send-to-handler latency are future work.

**Syscall decisions** (`syscalls`). 1. *Crate?* `syscalls::Errno` only (already admitted). 2. *Bundle?* All use the one `usercopy` pair (`get_user`/`put_user` are two-line wrappers, not a second mechanism). 3. *Simplify?* Single-process pid table (shortcuts below). 4. *Secure?* Pointers are never dereferenced; the order of failures follows Linux so a hostile argument cannot reach a later step (`EFAULT` of `act` precedes the signal checks, `EPERM` of `sigaltstack` precedes mode validation, `rt_sigqueueinfo` copies and validates before any permission or pid decision). 5. *Improve?* Argument decoding is table-free.

**Frame construction and `rt_sigreturn`** (`frame`). 1. *Crate?* `zerocopy` was recommended by the crate report for `repr(C)` ABI structs, but admitting it needs `syn` 2 vendored plus a build-script allow-list entry in the auditor, which is larger than this change; the structures are written and read field by field with explicit little-endian helpers (`le`, `to_le_bytes`), which also reproduces Linux's *partial* stores (padding and `reserved1` untouched) that a whole-struct `IntoBytes` write would not. Revisit when the first sizeable ABI struct set (`stat`, `clone_args`, `epoll_event`) arrives and `zerocopy` is admitted for them. 2. *Bundle?* One layout table (constants checked against `repr(C)` mirrors in the tests); FPU and segment facts behind the single `Cpu` trait so the proofs for FXSAVE/FXRSTOR/`lar` live in the binary. 3. *Simplify?* Native x86_64 frame only; no XSAVE/PKRU/AVX layout (`UC_FP_XSTATE` absent), no ia32/x32 frames, no shadow stack, no FRED, no TF single-step bookkeeping. 4. *Secure?* `rt_sigreturn` restores only `FIX_EFLAGS` bits (IF, IOPL, NT, VM, VIF/VIP never come from user memory), forces RPL 3 on `cs`/`ss`, replaces an unusable `ss` unless the frame says `UC_STRICT_RESTORE_SS`, rejects a misaligned FXSAVE pointer and reserved MXCSR bits before the FPU is touched (resetting the user FPU state on any failure), and `orig_ax` is forced to -1 so a forged frame cannot trigger a syscall restart. The 128-byte red zone and the altstack overflow check keep the frame off live data. 5. *Improve?* The FXSAVE image is built in a stack buffer and written once; no second copy of the frame.

### Shortcuts (each is also a `shortcut:` comment at its site)

* Process table: a positive pid other than this process, and a process group other than init's, answer `ESRCH`; `kill(0, ..)` and `kill(-1, ..)` return `None` (unimplemented) because their answer depends on kernel threads that do not exist here. Wrong the moment a second process exists.
* Queue capacity: 31 reserved slots for classic signals (at most one per signal per set, so a forced fault's `siginfo` is never lost) plus 64 real-time entries per set, i.e. an RT pending limit of `min(RLIMIT_SIGPENDING, 64 per queue)`; a full RT quota behaves like a failed `sigqueue_alloc` (`EAGAIN` for user-queued RT signals, silent `siginfo` loss otherwise). Replace by a `try_reserve`-grown `Vec`.
* `wants_signal` assumes the task is running or about to dequeue; stop and sleep states, wakeups and `TIF_SIGPENDING` are the scheduler step's. `get_signal` returns `Stop(sig)` for default stop actions without modelling group stop; core dumps are not produced (`Fatal { core: true }` is a request; `RLIMIT_CORE` is 0 for init, so the exit code is the number).
* Not implemented (Linux has them): `pidfd_send_signal`, `signalfd`, the obsolete `sigaction`/`sigprocmask` forms. They are not stubs: none is dispatched. (`rt_sigsuspend`, `rt_sigtimedwait`, `pause` and `restart_syscall` were added by the [signal wiring](#signal-wiring) slice.)
* `State::new` is the state of a new process; `State::flush_on_exec` is `execve`'s (handlers back to default but `SIG_IGN` kept, flags/restorer/mask cleared, altstack address and size zeroed, blocked mask and pending signals kept). Fork inheritance is not written yet.
* Single-step (`TIF_SINGLESTEP`), uprobes/rseq signal hooks (`CONFIG_RSEQ=n`), ptrace stops, `SA_EXPOSE_TAGBITS` tag hiding (identity on x86) and `real_blocked` are absent.
* A fault partway through the frame leaves exactly the stores that happened (same field order as Linux), but the page-fault *retry* of `copy_fpstate_to_sigframe` (fault the page in, retry) does not exist: `UserPages` either has the page or does not.

| Queueing: coalescing, RT FIFO, rlimit and overflow, synchronous-first, private-before-shared, stop/cont flush, forced signals | `signal::state` tests | host-tested |
| Frame bytes, stack choice, altstack, nesting, alignment sweep, `SA_*` effects, restart rules, `rt_sigreturn` validation, newest-frame-first loop | `signal::tests` (expected bytes from `repr(C)` mirrors and `offset_of!`; literal `FIX_EFLAGS = 0x50DD5`) and, byte for byte against the real kernel, `model_reproduces_the_pinned_linux_frame_*` (frame and FXSAVE area of two frames dumped by Linux 6.18.57, normal stack and altstack, plus `rt_sigreturn` of the real frame) | host-tested; the frame builder equals Linux's bytes for these two cases |
| Exceptions to `siginfo`/`trapno`/`err`/`cr2` | `signal::info` tests, mapping read from `traps.c`/`fault.c` and compared with the oracle's `int3`, `#GP`, `#DE`, `#UD`, `#PF` rows (codes, addresses, `err = 0x14` for an instruction fetch) | host-tested |
| Differential run of the whole `signal` group against this model's consumer, ring-3 execution, `iretq` return path, real FXSAVE/FXRSTOR | see [signal wiring](#signal-wiring) | done for the rows listed there; SMP, ia32/x32, XSAVE frames and physical remain **not done** (XSAVE/AVX layouts need their own configuration, no second machine) |

### Oracle cross-check

`target/linux-reference/init-reference-005` (pinned 6.18.57, TCG and KVM) passes all 201 `signal` checks. The model was written from the source and then compared by hand with the names and values of the checks that concern pure semantics: initial altstack `{0, SS_DISABLE, 0}`, `1024 -> ENOMEM` / `2048 ok`, `SS_ONSTACK` accepted and not stored, flags 4/8 `EINVAL`, in-handler query `(base, SS_ONSTACK, size)`, `uc_stack` flags 0, AUTODISARM `(0, SS_DISABLE, 0)` in the handler and re-armed after, `sigpending-short-size`, the `kill`/`tkill`/`tgkill` error rows, `sigqueueinfo` `EPERM`/`ESRCH`/`EFAULT`/`EINVAL`/signal 0, `int3` `(SIGTRAP, SI_KERNEL, trapno 3, err 0)`, `#GP` `(SI_KERNEL, addr 0, 13, 0)`, `#DE` `(code 1, addr = ip, 0, 0)`, `#UD` `(code 2, addr = ip, 6)`, page-fault `err 0x14`, and "unmasked handlers run newest frame first" (`pending_signals_stack_frames_newest_first`). It is a manual comparison, not an automated differential. None of the 201 checks constrains `uc_flags`, the `fpstate` bytes or the frame position (TCG and KVM agree on all of them), so the FP part of the frame is pinned by a separate probe, below. `x86/fpu: x87 FPU will use FXSAVE` and `signal: max sigframe size: 1440` in the TCG log confirm the FXSR-only assumptions.

### Frame probe on the pinned kernel

`target/linux-reference/sigframe-probe-001/` (README, `probe.rs`, `probe.cmd`, `probe-tcg.log`, `init`, `initramfs.cpio`, `SHA256SUMS`; local evidence, not in git): a throwaway libc-free static `/init` whose `SA_SIGINFO` handler dumps the raw 440-byte `rt_sigframe` and the 512-byte FXSAVE area, once on the normal stack (SIGHUP blocked, `sa_mask` = SIGUSR2) and once on an installed altstack, on the pinned `bzImage` under QEMU TCG `-cpu qemu64` (no XSAVE). It settles: `uc_flags = 6`; `_fpx_sw_bytes = { 0x46505853, 516, xfeatures 0, 512 }`; `fpstate = round_down(sp - 640, 64)`; frame `= round_down(fpstate - 440, 16) - 8`; `uc_stack` stores the stored flags; `oldmask = uc_sigmask` = the pre-delivery mask; `trapno = err = cr2 = 0` for a `kill`-generated signal. Both dumps are embedded in `kernel/src/signal/linux_frames.rs` and `signal::tests::model_reproduces_the_pinned_linux_frame_*` rebuilds each frame with the model from the dump's own interrupted registers and requires all 440 + 512 bytes to match, then `rt_sigreturn`s the real frame back to the original registers. Blind spots: TCG only, one kernel-generated (`kill`) path, no Linux-side `rt_sigreturn` evidence, no XSAVE/AVX, and stale-stack bytes (`stack_t` padding, `reserved1`) that were zero in the fresh stack, so those two "never stored" claims rest on the source.

### Evidence actually produced

Gate run `signal-gate-006` on the branch rebased onto `main` e20bcfd (`target/gates.sh`, logs in `target/gates-signal6/`, every step `exit=0`): `cargo fmt --all --check`; host 1.88 Clippy and tests of `xtask`/`baremetal`; `cargo +nightly clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`; `cargo +nightly test --workspace` (kernel library 261 tests, 62 of them `signal::*`); `xtask kernel-test` 25/25 (the binary does not call the module); target Clippy of both kernel profiles; `xtask check` and `status` (18 `in_progress` after the `ws-kernel-signal-c` route). An earlier run of the same tree (`signal-gate-005`) had `kernel-test` killed by SIGTERM (exit 143) while stray background waiters from a previous run were being cancelled; it was repeated unchanged as 006 rather than counted. Because the module is not reachable from the freestanding binary, none of this exercises the signal code in ring 3, on hardware or against Linux automatically; the Linux comparison is the byte-exact replay of the two probe frames.

### Linux sources read (6.18.57, commit `2ff600d29ee478d0a8d46ef8a58afb5e038fe692`)

`kernel/signal.c`, `kernel/exit.c` (`do_group_exit`), `include/linux/{signal,sched/signal,signal_types,errno}.h`, `include/uapi/asm-generic/{signal,signal-defs,siginfo,ucontext}.h`, `arch/x86/kernel/{signal,signal_64,traps}.c`, `arch/x86/kernel/fpu/{signal,core,init,xstate}.c`, `arch/x86/mm/fault.c`, `arch/x86/include/asm/{sigframe,sighandling,signal,ptrace,syscall,segment,traps,desc_defs}.h`, `arch/x86/include/uapi/asm/{sigcontext,signal,ucontext,debugreg}.h`.

## Time syscalls

Worktree `time`, branch `feat/syscalls-time` (rebased onto `main` e2f8618). The `time` oracle group (118 checks) now passes on this kernel with output identical to the pinned Linux's expected lines. Routed as `ws-kernel-time` (`kernel/time`: `posix-timers.c`, `posix-cpu-timers.c`, `posix-clock.c`, `alarmtimer.c`, `hrtimer.c` nanosleep, `time.c`, `timekeeping.c` `do_settimeofday64`) with `kernel/sys.c` `times` and `kernel/sched/cputime.c` accounting as selected helpers; those prefixes' other files stay `not_started`.

### What is reachable

| Syscall | Behaviour (Linux 6.18.57 check order) |
| --- | --- |
| `clock_gettime` | `clockid_to_kclock` first (`-EINVAL` before any pointer is touched, also for `CLOCK_SGI_CYCLE` and every id above 11: the reference config has `POSIX_AUX_CLOCKS=n`), then the clock, then the 16-byte `put_timespec64` (`-EFAULT`). REALTIME, MONOTONIC, MONOTONIC_RAW, BOOTTIME, TAI (`tai_offset` 0), the two COARSE clocks (monotonic floored to a tick; realtime = that + the realtime offset, but never below the value the last `clock_settime` stored, as `xtime` is that value until the next tick), PROCESS/THREAD_CPUTIME_ID (`sum_exec_runtime`), the alarm clocks (`-EINVAL` unless an RTC exists, see below) and negative ids: pid-encoded CPU clocks (`pid_for_clock`: pid 0 or this task's id, `which` 0/1/2 = PROF/VIRT/SCHED = utime+stime / utime / runtime, otherwise `-EINVAL`) and `CLOCKFD` ids (`-EINVAL`: no posix-clock device can exist, `get_clock_desc` fails for every fd). |
| `clock_getres` | Same id rules; a null result pointer is valid. `hrtimer_resolution` is 1 ns for REALTIME/MONOTONIC/MONOTONIC_RAW/BOOTTIME/TAI/alarm and the SCHED CPU clocks (high-resolution mode is active from boot here; Linux switches some time after boot, the oracle waits for it), one tick (1 ms, `HZ=1000`) for COARSE and the PROF/VIRT CPU clocks. |
| `clock_settime` | Only `CLOCK_REALTIME`, CPU-clock ids and `CLOCKFD` ids have a setter; everything else `-EINVAL` before the pointer is read. Then `-EFAULT`; REALTIME: `timespec64_valid_settod` (`-EINVAL`), `CAP_SYS_TIME` (`-EPERM`), `do_settimeofday64` (a time earlier than `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` is `-EINVAL`: `wall_to_monotonic > ts_delta`). The timekeeper now holds a settable realtime offset (`Clock::real_offset_ns`, `-wall_to_monotonic`) and the set value (`Clock::settod_real_ns`, the coarse floor); CPU clocks: `-EINVAL` for a bad target, else `-EPERM` ("you can never reset a CPU clock"); `CLOCKFD`: `-EINVAL`. |
| `nanosleep`, `clock_nanosleep` | `get_timespec64` (`-EFAULT`), `timespec64_valid` (`-EINVAL`), `TIMER_ABSTIME` (only bit 0; other flag bits are ignored for hrtimer clocks, `-EINVAL` for the alarm clocks). `clock_nanosleep` checks the id (`-EINVAL`) and `kc->nsleep` (`-EOPNOTSUPP` for THREAD_CPUTIME, MONOTONIC_RAW, COARSE, `CLOCKFD`) before the pointer. Relative sleeps add to monotonic time with `KTIME_MAX` saturation; absolute sleeps translate REALTIME/TAI/BOOTTIME/alarm times to monotonic with the clock's offset and return at once for the past. The wait is `timer::wait_until_ns` (halt between interrupts, never returns early). `rmtp` is written only on interruption. CPU-clock sleeps (`CLOCK_PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID`, pid-encoded clocks) return 0 at once for a zero expiry or an absolute one not above the clock (`posix_cpu_timer_set` arms nothing, resp. fires immediately); only a genuine wait stops loudly. |
| `gettimeofday` | Two `put_user`s (seconds, then microseconds), then the 8-byte `sys_tz` (always zero: no `settimeofday`). |
| `time` | Whole seconds of the tick-published realtime (`ktime_get_real_seconds`; right after `clock_settime` that is the set second, not the floored tick view), stored through `tloc` (`-EFAULT`) and returned. |
| `times` | `struct tms` of four `i64` in `USER_HZ`=100 ticks (`nsec_to_clock_t`): `utime`/`stime` through `cputime_adjust`, children zero; the return value is `jiffies_64_to_clock_t(INITIAL_JIFFIES + uptime ticks)` with Linux's `INITIAL_JIFFIES` (`-300*HZ` as 32 bits), i.e. about 429,466,730 right after boot, not zero. |

### Design

- **Library/binary split.** `kernel/src/syscall/time.rs` is `forbid(unsafe_code)`: clock-id decoding (`clockid_to_kclock`), validation, deadline arithmetic and the interrupted-sleep protocol, over a new `Clocks` trait (supertrait of `Context`; `testing::Task` implements it with scriptable fakes, 15 host tests). `kernel/src/cputime.rs` (pure, atomics only) is the accounting and Linux's `cputime_adjust` (3 host tests). The binary supplies the TSC clocks (`timer::clock_state`, `set_real_offset_ns`), the halting wait and a `static CPUTIME`. `Process` only gains the restart block (the tick interrupt writes the accounting while a syscall holds the `PROCESS` lock, so it cannot live behind that lock).
- **Accounting (`CONFIG_TICK_CPU_ACCOUNTING=y`, as the reference).** `sum_exec_runtime` is exact: time since init started minus time spent in sleeps (a task blocked in `nanosleep` is off the CPU; the halted CPU is idle). `utime`/`stime` advance by `TICK_NSEC` per tick, classified by the interrupted CS (`account_process_tick`); a tick that finds the task asleep is idle. `times()` scales them to the runtime (`thread_group_cputime_adjusted`, monotonic across calls); the PROF/VIRT CPU clocks read the raw tick counts, the SCHED clocks (PROCESS/THREAD_CPUTIME_ID) the exact runtime. To make ticks exist while init runs, `timer::resume_tick()` restarts the periodic tick that the boot proofs masked (Linux keeps the tick while a task runs; only idle stops it).
- **Alarm clocks.** `alarmtimer_get_rtcdev()` is true when the CMOS RTC answered at boot (`timer::rtc_present`; QEMU's does). Without suspend an alarm timer fires exactly when the hrtimer on the same base would, so `clock_nanosleep` on them uses the same wait after Linux's checks (`-EOPNOTSUPP` no RTC, `-EINVAL` flags, `-EPERM` `CAP_WAKE_ALARM`); the RTC wakeup is never armed.
- **Interrupted sleeps.** `Clocks::sleep_until` returns `Some(remaining)` when a signal interrupts; the group then writes `rmtp` (relative only, `-EFAULT` wins) and returns `-ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK` (516), or `-ERESTARTNOHAND` (514) for absolute sleeps, exactly as `do_nanosleep`/`hrtimer_nanosleep`, host-tested with a fake that interrupts. The restart block is recorded here, not left to the signal lane: `Clocks::set_restart_block` stores `do_no_restart_syscall` plus `rmtp` when a sleep starts and, on a relative interruption, the restart function (`hrtimer_nanosleep_restart`/`alarm_timer_nsleep_restart`), the timer base's clock id (a relative `CLOCK_REALTIME` sleep is on `CLOCK_MONOTONIC`, `hrtimer.c:1638`; alarm sleeps store the alarm type) and the absolute `expires` (`hrtimer_get_expires_tv64`: now on that base + request + the 50 us `timer_slack_ns`, `init_task.c:146`; alarm sleeps have no slack), in `Process::restart`. **The signal lane is wired** ([Signal wiring](#signal-wiring-integration-with-the-merged-lanes)): `sleep_until` is `signal::wait::sleep_until` and returns `Some(expires - now)` as soon as a signal is pending (the interval timer is a wake-up source of the halt), `restart_syscall` (219, `syscall::time::restart_syscall`) resumes `Process::restart` with `deadline = expires` on the block's clock and keeps the block installed when interrupted again, `rt_sigreturn` resets it to `do_no_restart_syscall` (`restore_sigcontext`), and `signal::deliver` turns 514/516 into `EINTR` (handler ran) or a restart. The `signal` group's `nanosleep-*` and `SA_RESTART` checks pass in `oracle-signal`.
- **Clock writer rule.** `clock_publish` is a seqcount with one writer; `clock_settime` runs with IF clear (the `SYSCALL` entry masks it), so a tick on the same CPU cannot spin on an odd sequence (`timer::set_realtime` asserts `!interrupts_enabled()` in debug builds).
- **Lock order.** `sleep_until` runs under the `PROCESS` lock and `timer::wait_until_ns` takes the leaf `TIMER_HW` (`cpu::timer_transaction`); the tick handlers only `try_lock` it. The new edge is therefore `PROCESS` -> `TIMER_HW`, a leaf (no other lock is taken while it is held), recorded beside `PROCESS`'s order in `userspace.rs`. `CPUTIME` and the clock statics are lock-free atomics.

### Linux differences and limits (named, not hidden)

- `stime` stays 0 in practice: syscalls run with interrupts off, so a tick never lands in the kernel on a running task; it is taken after `sysret` and accounted as user. `cputime_adjust` then reports all runtime as user time ("if stime is 0 assume all runtime is userspace"), which Linux itself does for a task that has had no system tick. Upgrade: account syscall time at kernel exit once syscalls can be preempted.
- `CLOCK_*_COARSE`/`time()` are derived from monotonic time floored to a tick (plus the set-time floor above), not read from tick-updated copies.
- CPU-clock `clock_nanosleep` (`CLOCK_PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID`, pid-encoded clocks) with a future expiry stops loudly (`Outcome::Unimplemented`) after Linux's validation errors and after the answered-at-once cases above: a sleep on the caller's own CPU time cannot finish without signals or threads.
- Process CPU time (`CLOCK_PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID`, the SCHED/PROF/VIRT clocks, `times`) starts at 0 when `/init` starts. In Linux PID 1 is the `kernel_init` task that execs `/init`, and `exec` keeps `sum_exec_runtime`/`utime`/`stime`, so a fresh `/init` there already shows the initcalls' CPU time. No pinned mapping exists between our boot phases and `kernel_init`'s share (everything before `rest_init` belongs to the idle task there), so the account is not seeded with an invented number; the oracle checks only advance and an upper bound. Upgrade: seed from the boot-phase timestamps once the kernel has a `kernel_init` task.
- The tick credits all missed periods to the mode found at the late tick (`hrtimer_forward`-style catch-up); Linux calls `account_process_tick` once per timer interrupt with one `TICK_NSEC`, so after a long interrupts-off section the `utime`/`stime` split and total differ. Upgrade with the scheduler-driven tick.
- The alarm clocks answer as if a wakeup-capable RTC existed whenever the CMOS RTC read at boot (QEMU: matches Linux on the reference). Linux's `alarmtimer_rtc_add_device` additionally requires `rtc->ops->set_alarm` and a parent with `device_may_wakeup`, so on bare metal the pinned kernel may return `-EINVAL`/`-EOPNOTSUPP` for the alarm clocks where this kernel succeeds; physical qualification must compare it (blind spot, not yet observed). `settimeofday` (timezone, `timekeeping_warp_clock`), `adjtimex`/`clock_adjtime`, `timer_*`, `alarm`, `*itimer` are not implemented (loud stop).
- `sysinfo` load averages still assume a constant `nr_running` of 1 (`NR_ACTIVE`, replayed from the boot clock), so time spent in `nanosleep` counts as runnable where Linux counts it as sleeping; the loads are over-reported after long sleeps. Upgrade: record sleep intervals in `LoadAvg` or use the runqueue counters.
- Dynamic posix-clock ids are `-EINVAL` because no posix-clock device exists; with a VFS file table the `get_clock_desc` rules (`ENODEV`/`EACCES`/`EOPNOTSUPP`) apply to real devices.
- Capabilities are constants (init is root with all capabilities) until the ids lane adds credentials; threads, time namespaces, suspend (`CLOCK_BOOTTIME == CLOCK_MONOTONIC`) and compat (32-bit) time syscalls (`ia32_emulation=0`) do not exist.

### Evidence

`kernel` host tests: 280 passed on the rebased tree (15 `syscall::time`, 3 `cputime`; `Clock::set_realtime`). Review round 1 added the literal-valued host tests `coarse_realtime_reads_the_value_just_set`, `interrupted_sleep_records_the_restart_block` and the answered-at-once cases of `cpu_clock_sleeps_are_loud_not_faked`, and ten oracle checks (`settime-*`, `cpu-nanosleep-*`) that Linux passes first: pinned 6.18.57 reference kernel, exact harness-built init/initramfs of the `time-fix-a` kernel-test run, `-- time`, TCG `qemu64`, TCG repeat and KVM `host` all printed `init: tests time passed=118 failed=0` with identical test lines (`target/linux-reference/init-reference-007/`, local evidence, not committed; commands in `oracle-time-*.cmd`). Gates: `target/gates.sh` run `time-gate-004` (logs `target/gates-time4/`, untracked) on the final code, all `exit=0`: fmt, host 1.88 Clippy/tests (xtask 76 + baremetal 10), nightly workspace Clippy (`-D warnings`) and tests (kernel library 280), `kernel-test` 25/25 cases matched, both target Clippy profiles, `xtask check`, `xtask status`. The `time` group on that run's `sse` image (command line `-- time`, script `target/time-run4/run.sh`) printed 119 lines (118 checks + summary) **byte-identical** to the `time` rows of `xtask/oracle/expected.tsv` on TCG `qemu64` and KVM `host`. Earlier rounds: run `time-gate-001`/`-003` and `target/time-run2/` (108 checks, same result). The group also runs as the `kernel-test` case `oracle-time` (`oracle_case!(time)`, added after rebasing onto `main` with PR #19's generic harness; its 119 lines equal the `time` rows of `expected.tsv`; run name in the integration record below). Blind spots: one host, TCG and KVM only; the sleep-interruption path and the restart block have host tests only (nothing can interrupt a sleep yet); genuine CPU-clock waits stop loudly and are not exercised; `stime` is never exercised; the alarm-clock RTC rule is unverified on bare metal.

### Answers to the five design questions

*Preexisting crate?* None fits: the work is Linux semantics over our own timekeeper; `syscalls` supplies `Sysno`/`Errno`. *Bundle?* One group file for eight syscalls sharing `clockid_to_kclock`, `put/get_timespec64` and the sleep driver; the accounting is a separate pure module because the tick interrupt shares it. *Simplify?* One sleep path for hrtimer and alarm clocks (no suspend), one `Now` snapshot for every clock read, `Process` gains only the `restart` block. *Secure?* Every user access goes through `usercopy`; clock ids are decoded as `int` (upper register half ignored); the realtime setter requires `CAP_SYS_TIME` after validity and cannot set a time before monotonic or beyond `TIME_SETTOD_SEC_MAX`; deadlines saturate at `KTIME_MAX`; CPU clocks of other pids are `-EINVAL`, not information leaks; nothing a user passes can panic (the loud stops are Linux-implemented paths we lack, documented above). *Improve?* Per-task accounting and system-time attribution with the scheduler, a tick-updated coarse copy, real alarm timers with suspend, `timer_create`/itimers on the same `Kclock` decoder.

## Signal wiring

Roadmap `WP-M3.4`, second slice: the [signal model](#signal-model) is now reachable from the freestanding kernel. Branch `feat/signals` (worktree `sig-wire`). A signal sent by `kill`, a forced fault or an interval timer reaches a handler in ring 3 on **every** return to CPL3 (syscall, exception, interrupt), `rt_sigreturn` restores the interrupted state, and the process dies with Linux's text when the default action is fatal. Everything below is for one single-threaded process on one CPU under the declared configuration (`noxsave`/FXSR, `ia32_emulation=0`, `CONFIG_RSEQ=n`); none of it is SMP, ia32/x32, XSAVE or physical evidence.

### What is wired

| Mechanism | Where | Linux 6.18.57 source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| One `pt_regs` type | `cpu::PtRegs` is `signal::Regs` (`#[repr(C)]`, 21 words) | `arch/x86/include/asm/ptrace.h` |
| Full `pt_regs` on every exception and interrupt entry | one naked stub for all 32 exception vectors and the six external vectors (`entry!` in `cpu.rs`); `orig_ax` is `-1` (the hardware error code moves to the handler's third argument, as `error_entry` does) | `arch/x86/entry/entry_64.S` (`error_entry`, `idtentry`), `arch/x86/include/asm/idtentry.h` |
| Exit-to-user work | `userspace::exit_to_user` after the syscall handler, after an exception from CPL3 and after an interrupt that hit CPL3: `sync_timers`, `handle_pending_signals`, then the interval-timer wake-up, re-checked against the clock | `kernel/entry/common.c:19-60` (`exit_to_user_mode_loop`), `arch/x86/kernel/signal.c:214-253` (`arch_do_signal_or_restart`) |
| `signal::Cpu` | `cpu::UserCpu`: `_fxsave64` / `_fxrstor64` under `target_feature(fxsr)` (as `init_user_fpu` already does), `fpu_reset` is the Linux initial image, `mxcsr_mask` from the FXSAVE area (0 means `0xffbf`), `lar` for the `force_valid_ss` test (the one new `asm!` site) | `arch/x86/kernel/fpu/{core,init,signal}.c`, `arch/x86/kernel/signal_64.c:28-48` |
| Per-process state | `Process::signal: Box<State>` (the two pending queues are ~16 KiB; `Box::try_new`, failure is a `FAIL` stop) | `kernel/signal.c`, `include/linux/sched/signal.h` |
| Syscalls | [`syscall/signal.rs`](../kernel/src/syscall/signal.rs): `rt_sigaction`, `rt_sigprocmask`, `rt_sigpending`, `sigaltstack`, `kill`, `tkill`, `tgkill`, `rt_sigqueueinfo`, `rt_tgsigqueueinfo`, `rt_sigreturn`, `rt_sigsuspend`, `rt_sigtimedwait`, `pause`, `setitimer`, `getitimer`, `alarm` (`restart_syscall` is the time lane's, `syscall/time.rs`) | `kernel/signal.c` (entry points listed in the model section), `kernel/time/itimer.c` |
| Blocking waits | `signal::wait` (`pause`, `rt_sigsuspend`, `rt_sigtimedwait`, `sleep_until`, `wait_event_interruptible`) over one trait method, `Task::wait`: halt until an interrupt or a deadline | `kernel/signal.c:3823-3874, 4900-4941`, `kernel/time/hrtimer.c` (`do_nanosleep`) |
| `ITIMER_REAL` | `signal::itimer::RealTimer` in `State`; expiry is a wake-up programmed into the clockevent (`timer::set_itimer_ns`) and acted on by `State::sync_timers`, which sends `SIGALRM` (`SI_KERNEL`); an interval timer is re-armed when `SIGALRM` is dequeued from the shared queue | `kernel/time/itimer.c:156-185, 225-290`, `kernel/signal.c:659-660` |
| Synchronous faults | `userspace::exception` -> `raise_fault`: `info::exception` / `info::page_fault` -> `State::force_fault`; `#DB` reads and resets DR6, `#MF`/`#XM` read the FXSAVE image (`fpu_exception_code`) | `arch/x86/kernel/traps.c`, `arch/x86/mm/fault.c`, `arch/x86/kernel/fpu/core.c` |
| `show_unhandled_signals` | printed when the signal is forced, not when it kills: `unhandled_signal()` is true for the global init whether or not a handler exists, so the pinned kernel prints a line per fault (`init[1]: segfault at ...`, `traps: init[1] trap ...`); a ten-per-five-seconds `printk_ratelimit` ([`ratelimit.rs`](../kernel/src/ratelimit.rs), with the `callbacks suppressed` line) | `arch/x86/mm/fault.c:740-760` (`show_signal_msg`), `arch/x86/kernel/traps.c:227-250` (`show_signal`), `kernel/signal.c:560-575` (`unhandled_signal`), `lib/ratelimit.c`, `kernel/printk/printk.c:4699` |
| Bad `rt_sigreturn` frame | `Task::rt_sigreturn` prints `init[1] bad frame in rt_sigreturn frame:(____ptrval____) ip:.. sp:.. orax:..[ in init[..]]` (`report::BadFrame`, same limiter, `callbacks suppressed` named `signal_fault`) before the forced `SIGSEGV` (`SI_KERNEL`) | `arch/x86/kernel/signal.c:368-383` (`signal_fault`), `arch/x86/kernel/signal_64.c:277` |
| Return-frame check | the CPU decides, as in Linux: every stub and `syscall_entry` reach one `iretq` (`cpu::iret_site`); when the CPU refuses the frame (non-canonical RIP, unusable CS or SS, a compatibility RIP beyond its limit, ...) the `#GP`/`#NP`/`#SS` is raised at that address, `cpu::fixup_bad_iret` replaces the kernel frame by the user's five words from the stale frame, and the exception is handled as the user's (`SIGSEGV`/`SIGBUS` with the error code the CPU reports, `show_signal` line with the refused `ip`/`sp`). A non-canonical RSP is *not* refused by `iretq`: user mode resumes with it and faults on the first stack access, as on Linux (pinned `tools/testing/selftests/x86/sigreturn.c` depends on it) | `arch/x86/entry/entry_64.S:1074-1090` (`.Lerror_bad_iret`, `native_irq_return_iret`), `arch/x86/kernel/traps.c` (`fixup_bad_iret`) |

`iretq` is the only way back to ring 3 (the shortcut that `sysret` is unused predates this slice), so a handler entry or `rt_sigreturn` that rewrites `rip`/`rsp`/`cs`/`ss`/`rflags` is installed by the same instruction. The result of a syscall is stored in `regs.ax` before `exit_to_user` runs, so `ERESTART*` results are visible to `restart_for_handler` / `restart_without_handler`; `orig_ax` is the syscall number on `SYSCALL` entry and `-1` on every other entry.

### Interfaces for the other lanes (the seams)

These were written for the lanes as separate branches; all four are merged (ids #19, time #20, mm #21, VFS #22) and each seam is now used, see [Integration with the merged lanes](#signal-wiring-integration-with-the-merged-lanes). What the seams are, for the next lane:

* **Pending signal / send signal.** `Context` is a `signal::Task`, so a syscall has `ctx.state()`: `State::signal_pending()` is `signal_pending(current)`, `State::send_sig(sig)` is `send_sig(sig, current, 0)` (`SI_USER` with this process's pid and uid, thread-directed). A blocking read or write waits with `signal::wait::wait_event_interruptible(ctx, |ctx| ready)`: `Ok(())` when the condition holds, `Err(-ERESTARTSYS)` when a signal is pending first (condition first, as the macro).
* **Sleeping.** `signal::wait::sleep_until(task, deadline_ns) -> Option<remaining_ns>`: `None` when the deadline passed, `Some(left)` when a signal is pending. `Task::wait` brackets the halt with `CPUTIME.sleep_begin/end`, so `pause`, `rt_sigsuspend`, `rt_sigtimedwait` and the I/O waits are not charged as system time either.
* **Page faults.** `userspace::page_fault` runs the mm model (`Mm::handle_user_fault`, with `Vfs::fault_page` for private file pages): `Handled` retries the instruction, `Segv { code }` / `Bus` become `info::page_fault` and `State::force_fault`. The user-memory view the signal code writes frames through is `FaultPages`: the same fault-aware view as `Syscall`'s `UserPages` (a kernel access to a non-resident page takes the fault Linux would), built from disjoint borrows of `Process` so it can be held next to `&mut process.signal`.
* **Timers.** `timer::set_itimer_ns` publishes the `ITIMER_REAL` expiry as a clockevent wake-up (a second source next to `wait_until_ns`'s deadline; only reprogrammed when it changed); `timer::wait_event(deadline)` is one interruptible halt. The CPU-time interval timers (`ITIMER_VIRTUAL`, `ITIMER_PROF`) stop loudly (`Outcome::Unimplemented`): the time lane's `cputime` is merged but the interval-timer expiry is not ported.

### Mechanisms and their five design questions

**Entry and exit-to-user** (`entry!`, `iret_site`, `exit_to_user`, `fixup_bad_iret`). 1. *Preexisting crate?* The `x86_64` crate's `extern "x86-interrupt"` lowering was the previous mechanism and cannot be used: it saves only what its handler clobbers and its error-code lowering misaligned calls (the retained 007 witness); no crate builds a `pt_regs`. 2. *Bundle?* One stub body for all vectors (two arms: with and without a hardware error code), one Rust handler, one `exit_to_user` for syscalls, exceptions and interrupts; the previous separate "non-error compiler ABI" and "error-code naked" entries are one mechanism now. 3. *Simplify?* Interrupts stay masked for the whole delivery (Linux enables them in `exit_to_user_mode_loop` to allow preemption; with no scheduler there is nothing to preempt), no `sysret` fast path, no `swapgs` (single address space, FS/GS bases zero as before), no FRED, no KPTI. 4. *Secure?* The stub trusts nothing from the frame; the one `iretq` (`iret_site`) is recognised by its address when the CPU refuses a frame and the exception is re-attributed to the user (`fixup_bad_iret`, `.Lerror_bad_iret`), so no user-supplied `sigcontext` can fault the kernel; the CPU, not a model of its rules, decides what is refused (the earlier pre-validation wrongly refused a non-canonical RSP and missed a compatibility RIP beyond its limit); the refused frame is read from `BOOT_STACK` only after bounds and alignment checks, and sixteen consecutive refusals stop the kernel loudly instead of nesting frames without bound; a handler installed through the native `rt_sigaction` always gets the native frame, also when the interrupted code runs in compatibility mode (`is_ia32_frame` depends on `SA_IA32_ABI`, never set with `ia32_emulation=0`); the signal state is reached only under `PROCESS`; every fatal path ends in `init_died`. 5. *Improve?* Measure the extra register traffic of the full `pt_regs` on IRQs; the clockevent is only reprogrammed when the interval-timer expiry changes, so a syscall-heavy loop costs the `PROCESS` lock and two compares (the clock is read only while an interval timer is armed or a signal is pending).

**`UserCpu`** (`FXSAVE`/`FXRSTOR`/`lar`). 1. *Crate?* `core::arch::x86_64::_fxsave64`/`_fxrstor64` are used; no intrinsic or crate wraps `lar` (checked: `x86_64` 0.15.5, `core::arch`, `raw-cpuid`), so it is the one `asm!` site added by this slice, with the full proof in the source and in the [register](#assembly-necessity-abi-and-unsafe-proof-register). 2. *Bundle?* One `FxImage` type (16-byte aligned, shared with the initial-state image), one `FPU_READY` flag that `UserCpu::new` asserts. 3. *Simplify?* FXSR-only, no XSAVE/PKRU/AVX, no lazy FPU: user FP state is live in the registers whenever the kernel runs because the kernel is soft-float. 4. *Secure?* `fxrstor` re-checks the MXCSR reserved bits the caller already checked (a reserved bit would be a ring-0 `#GP`) and falls back to the init state; the restored image is copied to an aligned buffer; `lar` takes a register selector, so no selector value can fault. 5. *Improve?* Replace `lar` by an intrinsic if `core::arch` ever grows one.

**Blocking waits, `ITIMER_REAL` and the clockevent** (`signal::wait`, `signal::itimer`, `timer::{set_itimer_ns,wait_event}`). 1. *Crate?* None applies (no hrtimer crate with these semantics; reimplementing `schedule()` is not needed for one task). 2. *Bundle?* The three blocking syscalls, `sleep_until` and `wait_event_interruptible` are one loop shape over one trait method (`Task::wait`), and the wake-up sources (a deadline and the interval timer) share the existing clockevent arming (`rearm` takes the earliest). 3. *Simplify?* A blocking syscall halts (`sti; hlt`) with `PROCESS` and `TABLES` held and interrupts enabled for the halt: **no interrupt handler that can run while the task is halted may take `PROCESS` or `TABLES`** (today none does; `exit_to_user` is entered only from CPL3 interrupts, where no lock is held; see the lock-order note in `services.rs`). The interval timer is data in `State` and the hardware only provides a wake-up: expiry is evaluated by `sync_timers` at every kernel exit and wake, never in the interrupt handler, so no signal state is touched from interrupt context and no new lock exists; timer slack (50 us) and the freezer are not modelled. 4. *Secure?* A user cannot make the CPU halt forever by accident of ordering: the pending check and the halt both run with interrupts masked (`sti; hlt`), a deadline already due never halts, and a stale due timer is consumed before the next wait; the only unbounded wait is the one Linux has (`pause`, `rt_sigtimedwait` without timeout, `rt_sigsuspend`); `ktime` arithmetic saturates at `KTIME_MAX`. 5. *Improve?* `wait_event` re-arms the LAPIC twice per sleep; a combined arm would halve the MSR writes when sleeping becomes hot.

**Fault routing and `show_unhandled_signals`** (`raise_fault`, `resolve_page_fault`). 1. *Crate?* None. 2. *Bundle?* One path for every ring-3 exception and the bad-`iret` case; `report::user_exception` keeps providing the message table. 3. *Simplify?* The line is printed from `raise_fault` for every fault signal (init is always "unhandled"), limited by the Linux `printk_ratelimit` model; the `Code:` line and the other `show_unhandled_signals` callers (`SIGSYS`, `SIGBUS` `do_sigbus`, which prints nothing) are not added. 4. *Secure?* A vector that is not a plain user fault (NMI, `#DF`, `#MC`, `#NM`) is a kernel failure, never a user death; `cr2` is read before anything that can fault. 5. *Improve?* `Code:` line (`show_opcodes`); one global limiter instead of a field of the one process once a second process exists.

**Syscall group** (`syscall/signal.rs`). 1. *Crate?* `syscalls::Sysno` only. 2. *Bundle?* One file, one `dispatch`, no argument decoding beyond register truncation (the pure functions own it). 3. *Simplify?* `kill(0)`/`kill(-1)` and the CPU-time itimers are loud `Unimplemented` stops, not guesses. 4. *Secure?* Every pointer goes through `usercopy`; checked order is that of Linux (see the model section). 5. *Improve?* `pidfd_send_signal`, `signalfd`, the obsolete `sigaction`/`sigprocmask` forms and `rt_sigtimedwait_time32` are not dispatched (Linux has the first two; the last two are `-ENOSYS` under the declared configuration or unreachable from x86_64).

### Shortcuts (each is also a `shortcut:` or `NOTE` at its site)

* Interrupts are masked while signals are delivered (Linux enables them in `exit_to_user_mode_loop`).
* The `show_signal_msg` line has no `Code:` line (`show_opcodes`) after it; the pinned kernel prints one.
* `setitimer(which, NULL, ..)` does not print Linux's `printk_once` warning.
* Timer slack (`timer_slack_ns` 50 us) is not added to `rt_sigtimedwait`/`nanosleep` deadlines: the wake is exactly at the deadline.
* `ITIMER_VIRTUAL`/`ITIMER_PROF` and `kill(0)`/`kill(-1)` stop the kernel loudly.
* `RLIMIT_SIGPENDING` of init is `max_threads / 2` computed from the frame count (`fork_init`); the ids lane's `Attributes` owns the rlimit table and should supply it.
* A refused `iretq` frame nests one exception frame on the boot stack per refusal (Linux copies the frame back to the stack top, so a handler that keeps returning to a refused frame, `SA_NODEFER` on a non-canonical handler address, loops there forever); this kernel stops loudly after sixteen consecutive refusals (`FAIL bad iret loop`). Not exercised.
* `signal_fault`'s `frame:%p` is printed as `(____ptrval____)`, what the reference kernel prints under TCG before its pointer-hash key exists; with entropy (KVM) it prints a per-boot keyed hash that no other kernel can reproduce.
* Group stop, ptrace, core dumps, `TIF_SINGLESTEP`, `SA_EXPOSE_TAGBITS` semantics, `SIGSTOP` default action (init ignores it; any other process would stop) remain as in the model section.

The `signals-gate-002` kernel ELF is sha256 `40c19595...a6f3` and `/init` `1c021c02...f003`. A manual `cargo test -p xtask` run under heavy host load once failed `kernel::tests::child_dies_when_spawning_thread_exits` (a timing test of the harness, unrelated to this change) and passed on the immediate rerun and in every gate run. Per-case serial logs are untracked under `target/kernel-tooling/signals-gate-002/cases/<case>/serial.log`. This record is added after the gate run as a docs-only commit.

**Review round 1** (findings on `40bb199`; commits `64b329c`, `2419599`, `d36f591`, `cpu: Clippy` fix). The pre-validation of the `iretq` frame was removed: it refused a non-canonical RSP, which the CPU and Linux accept (pinned `sigreturn.c` depends on it), and could not know every frame the CPU refuses. Every return now ends in the one `iret_site`; a refused frame faults there and `fixup_bad_iret` re-attributes the fault to the user. Also: `signal_fault`'s `bad frame in rt_sigreturn` line, `SEGV_MAPERR` for any fault at or above `TASK_SIZE_MAX`, the interval-timer expiry re-checked after arming (loop again when it is already due), the clock read skipped when nothing can expire, and the locking rule for halting with `PROCESS`/`TABLES` held written into `services.rs`. Smoke additions (all also run on the pinned Linux): TSC lower bounds on `pause`, `rt_sigtimedwait` and a periodic timer, `getitimer`/`alarm`, 300 microsecond-scale timers, a non-canonical RSP that user mode resumes with, a compatibility RIP beyond 4 GiB (skipped on TCG, whose iret does not check the limit), and an unreadable `rt_sigreturn` frame. Results:

* Gate run `sigfix-gate-002` (`target/gates-sigfix2/`, `target/gates.sh`, on `1c211f3`; the evidence commit after it is docs only): every step `exit=0` (`fmt`, `host-clippy`, `host-test` xtask 76 + baremetal 10, `nightly-clippy`, `nightly-test` kernel library **280** tests, `kernel-test` **28/28** matched, target Clippy of both kernel profiles, `check`, `status`). Hashes: freestanding kernel ELF `d869b9e6...3c26d7` (the `qemu-self-test` ELF `dd4fa944...97209ab`), `/init` `cab194c4...f843b`. An earlier run `sigfix-gate-001` failed only the two target-Clippy steps (a `doc_markdown` and a `manual_is_multiple_of` lint in `fixup_bad_iret`), fixed before `-002`.
* Pinned Linux 6.18.57, same harness-built `/init` (`target/sigfix-linux/`, untracked): TCG `qemu64` and KVM `-cpu host` both print `init: signals ok`. The hostile-frame lines are the same as this kernel's apart from stack addresses and, on KVM, the compatibility-RIP `#GP` (`ip:100000000 error:0`, which this kernel reproduces on KVM); the bad-frame line is `init[1] bad frame in rt_sigreturn frame:(____ptrval____) ip:.. sp:1000 orax:f in init[..]` on TCG and a hashed pointer on KVM.
* Oracle `signal` group, branch alone (`target/sigfix-oracle/`, the `signals` case's disk image with `-- signal`): the 51 checks that run before the kernel stops loudly at `gettid` (the ids lane) are identical to `expected.tsv`, in order; the other 150 need other lanes, as before.
**Review round 2** (adversarial verification of `kernel/src/signal/`, commits `d72262a`..`ac2c04a`). Pending queues now reserve 31 slots for classic signals (one entry per signal per queue, so a forced fault's `siginfo` is never lost behind 64 queued RT signals) and cap only real-time entries at 64 per queue; the shortcut line above says `min(RLIMIT_SIGPENDING, 64 per queue)` for RT. Ten tests added, each checked to fail under its mutation (queue cap off by one both ways and the old shared-length cap, eight `restore_sigcontext` offset swaps, either leg of the altstack overflow test, private-queue flush, `legacy_queue` over the union, `kill`/`rt_sigqueueinfo` target swaps, the `handle_pending_signals` loop condition, the `Stop` mapping, the FXSAVE alignment check, the 64-bit `cs` conjunct, the `error_code` store and the `cr2` keep). Gate run `sig-rvfix-1` (`target/gates-sig-rvfix-1/`, on `ac2c04a`): every step `exit=0`, kernel library **291** tests, `kernel-test` 28/28.
