Not exercised by any of this: SMP, a second process, ia32/x32/XSAVE frames, physical hardware, performance, and the four VFS-dependent oracle checks.

### Oracle run

The `signal` oracle group is 201 checks (`xtask/oracle/expected.tsv`, recorded on the pinned Linux). It also calls syscalls of other lanes, so on this branch alone it runs 51 checks and the kernel stops loudly at `gettid` (`FAIL syscall unimplemented nr=186`, the ids lane's). It was therefore run on a **scratch integration tree** (local branch `scratch/sig-integ`, not for merging: this branch merged with `feat/syscalls-ids`, `feat/syscalls-time` and `feat/mm-syscalls`, conflicts resolved by hand: the by-value `Task::Pages` view became a `FaultPages` borrow of the mm model so frame writes fault pages in, `resolve_page_fault` became `handle_user_fault`, and `Clocks::sleep_until` became `signal::wait::sleep_until`), built and run by the real harness (`xtask kernel-test`, run `integ-001`: **29/29** cases matched, including `oracle-ids` and the three `signals*` cases), then `-- signal` on that run's harness-built kernel (sha256 `1d9133ea...97f8`) and `/init` (`a56674f0...cdcd1`) under QEMU TCG `qemu64` and KVM (`-cpu host`):

* **190 of 190** lines produced before the run reaches the first VFS syscall are identical to the recorded Linux lines, in order (through `nanosleep-remaining-restart`; then `FAIL syscall unimplemented nr=293`, `pipe2`).
* With the pipe block of the scratch `/init` removed (the four `read-*` checks), **197 of the 201** expected check lines are produced, identical and in the same order, none different and none extra, `init: ok`, exit 33, on TCG and on KVM.
* **Not verified (4):** `read-eintr-without-restart`, `read-eintr-handler-ran`, `read-restarted-with-sa_restart`, `read-restart-handler-ran` need `pipe2`/`read`/`close` from the VFS lane (a blocking `read` must wait with `wait_event_interruptible` and return its `-ERESTARTSYS`); the exact summary line `init: tests signal passed=201 failed=0` follows from them (the reduced run prints `passed=197`).
* Which checks depend on which unmerged lane: `gettid` (ids) is needed from `tgkill-missing-tid` (#51) on; `clock_gettime`/`nanosleep` (time) by `sigtimedwait-timeout-elapsed`, `sigtimedwait-woke-early` and the four `nanosleep-*` checks; `mmap`/`mprotect`/`munmap` (mm) by the `segv-prot-none-*`, `segv-read-only-*`, `read-only-read-ok*` and `segv-exec-nx-*` rows of the 24 fault checks; `pipe2`/`read`/`close` (VFS) by the four above. The 201 checks pass in the integration tree with every lane except VFS; nothing in the signal code is known to be missing for the four.

Cross-checks on the **pinned Linux 6.18.57** itself (`target/signal-wire-001/`, untracked): the `signals` smoke prints the same `init: signals ok` there, with the same fault lines (`segfault at 0 ...`, `trap invalid opcode`, `trap divide error`) and, for the three hostile `rt_sigreturn` frames, `traps: init[1] general protection fault ip:8000000000000000 ... error:0`, `... error:10 ...` (bad CS selector) and `... error:0` (null SS), which this kernel reproduces to the byte except for stack addresses. Evidence directory: serial logs of the three runs above, the comparison output, the Linux smoke log, `SHA256SUMS` and the head commits. The integration below added `oracle_case!(signal)`: the case compares all 201 lines and the summary exactly, and passes.


### Signal wiring: integration with the merged lanes

Branch `feat/signals` rebased onto `main` fbd89b4 (ids #19 with the `oracle_case!` harness, time #20, mm #21, VFS #22). Everything the signal branch left as a seam or a loud stop is now wired; the stops that remain are listed at the end.

| Change | Where | Linux 6.18.57 source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| mm `Segv`/`Bus` outcomes are forced signals with the model's siginfo: `SEGV_MAPERR`/`SEGV_ACCERR` with the address, `SIGBUS`/`BUS_ADRERR` for a private file page past `i_size`; `show_signal_msg`'s line and init's death when nothing handles it are as before | `userspace::page_fault` -> `info::page_fault` -> `State::force_fault` | `arch/x86/mm/fault.c` (`__bad_area_nosemaphore`, `do_sigbus`), `mm/shmem.c` (`shmem_fault`) |
| One fault-aware user-memory view for syscalls and signal code: `FaultPages` (mm model, environment, VFS and tables as disjoint borrows) replaces the non-faulting `&mut UserSpace` the signal code used, so a frame written to an untouched altstack page or an `rt_sigaction` argument in `.bss` faults in; `user_fault` takes the parts instead of `&mut Process` | `userspace.rs` | `arch/x86/mm/fault.c` (kernel-mode access to a user address) |
| `SIGPIPE`+`EPIPE`, `SIGXFSZ`+`EFBIG` replace the loud stops; blocking pipe and console waits are interruptible (`-ERESTARTSYS`, partial write count) | `syscall/fd.rs` | `fs/pipe.c` (`anon_pipe_read/write`), `fs/read_write.c:1709-1721`, `fs/attr.c:254-277`, `drivers/tty/n_tty.c` |
| `sleep_until` returns early on a pending signal; `restart_syscall` resumes the stored restart block; `rt_sigreturn` resets it; 514/516 are converted at delivery | `syscall/time.rs` (`restart_syscall`), `signal/wait.rs`, `userspace.rs` | `kernel/time/hrtimer.c` (`do_nanosleep`, `hrtimer_nanosleep_restart`), `kernel/time/alarmtimer.c`, `arch/x86/kernel/signal_64.c` (`restore_sigcontext`) |
| `signal::RestartBlock` (single-variant placeholder) and `sys_restart_syscall` are deleted: one restart block, the time lane's | `signal/{state,wait,frame}.rs` | |
| `oracle_case!(signal)` | `xtask/src/kernel.rs` | |

**Five design questions.** *Crate?* None applies. *Bundle?* One user-memory view (`FaultPages`) for syscalls, frames and `rt_sigreturn`, one restart block, one sleep primitive; nothing parallel was added. *Simplify?* `interruptible_wait` is the single place that decides between "sleep until a signal" and the loud `Blocked` stop (no signal pending and no timer armed). *Secure?* Faults taken by the kernel on behalf of a signal frame still never signal the task from kernel mode (`FaultPages` refuses with a short copy; the frame writer then forces `SIGSEGV` as Linux's `setup_rt_frame` failure does); a signal frame on a file-backed page past `i_size` is a refused write, not a kernel bug. *Improve?* The wait loop is a halt with `PROCESS`/`TABLES` held, as documented above; the next lane that needs a second task must move it out of the locks.

**Evidence** (all produced by the real harness on this tree, serial logs under `target/kernel-tooling/<run>/cases/<case>/serial.log`, untracked):

* `kernel-test` run `sig-int-d1` on the code commits: **34/34** debug-exit cases matched. `oracle-signal`: 201 `init: test signal.*` lines plus `init: tests signal passed=201 failed=0` identical to `xtask/oracle/expected.tsv`, `init: ok`, exit 33, no `FAIL`; `oracle-ids`, `oracle-time`, `oracle-mm`, `oracle-fs` and every other case keep passing (the first run of this integration, `sig-int-pre`, matched 30/34 and failed exactly the four signal cases at `signal.altstack-handler-runs-on-it` (`oracle-signal`: init killed by `SIGSEGV`, exit code 11; `signals`: `FAIL onstack-sp`), the first handler run on a not yet touched altstack page, where the signal code still wrote frames through a non-faulting view; after `FaultPages` the same checks pass).
* The `signals` smoke gained a block that runs `SIGPIPE`, `SIGXFSZ` and `SIGBUS` handlers (`sigpipe-*`, `sigxfsz-*`, `sigbus-*`, including the `BUS_ADRERR` address and that a clamped write sends nothing). Run `sig-int-d2`: **34/34** matched with it (`signals`, `signals-nolapic`, `signals-kvm`, `oracle-*`). The same harness-built `/init` and initramfs (`initramfs.cpio` sha256 `8128a40d...bcbb`) print `init: signals ok` and exit 0 on the pinned Linux 6.18.57 under TCG `qemu64` and KVM `-cpu host` (`target/sig-int-linux/`, untracked).
* Gate run `sig-int-c2` (`target/gates.sh`, logs in `target/gates-sig-int-c2/`, tree `d0f074e`; the record commit after it is docs only): every step `exit=0` (`fmt`, `host-clippy`, `host-test` xtask 78 + baremetal 10, `nightly-clippy`, `nightly-test` kernel library **399** tests, `kernel-test` **34/34** matched, target Clippy of both kernel profiles, `check`, `status` 23 `in_progress` / 2,600 `not_started`, unchanged). `oracle-signal` in that run: 201 check lines and the summary line identical to `expected.tsv`; `oracle-mm` 121, `oracle-ids` 157, `oracle-time` 118, `oracle-fs` 284 checks each plus their summary. Harness-built `/init` sha256 `1ec41518...2fe5`. The run `sig-int-c1` on the previous tree failed only `fmt` (one `expect(` call in the new smoke block not wrapped), fixed before `-c2`. The pinned-Linux smoke run above used the `sig-int-d2` `/init`, built before that formatting-only change.
* Fix round 1 (review of the integration; gate run `sig-int-fix1b`, every step `exit=0`, nightly library 401 tests, `kernel-test` 34/34 matched, logs in `target/gates-sig-int-fix1b/`; run `sig-int-fix1` failed only `nightly-clippy` on one `as i64` in the new test, fixed before `-fix1b`). (1) `nanosleep`/`clock_nanosleep` on an hrtimer clock now wait to the hard expiry (soft + `timer_slack_ns`, 50 us) and `rmtp` carries `hard - now`, as `do_nanosleep`'s `hrtimer_expires_remaining` does; the restart block and the reported remainder agree, a signal in the slack window gives a non-zero remainder and `-ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK`, an uninterrupted sleep ends at the hard expiry (alarm clocks have no slack and are unchanged). Host test `nanosleep_remainder_and_wakeup_use_the_hard_expiry` goes through the real `signal::wait::sleep_until` (`FakeClocks::real_sleep`). (2) The restart-block reset after `rt_sigreturn` runs only when `restore_sigcontext` was entered (`Sigreturn::reached_sigcontext`), so a refused frame no longer clears a pending `nanosleep` restart. (3) `fd::interruptible_wait` re-evaluates "nothing can wake the task" on every wake-up, so an `ITIMER_REAL` that expired into an ignored or blocked `SIGALRM` ends in `Outcome::Blocked` instead of halting (host test `a_wait_whose_only_wakeup_expired_into_nothing_stops_loudly`); stale "nothing sleeps" / `Stop::SigPipe` / "no syscall sleeps yet" text corrected. Not changed (informational, not substantiated by execution): `exit_to_user` re-delivers without returning to ring 3 when a periodic `ITIMER_REAL` interval is shorter than one delivery and `SIGALRM` is `SA_NODEFER`; handler frames then stack until the stack rlimit ends the process with `SIGSEGV`, whereas Linux runs the handler between expiries (a QEMU-visible difference only for sub-microsecond intervals).
* Blind spots: still one process, one CPU, TCG and KVM only; `SIGPIPE`/`SIGXFSZ` were only exercised with handlers (their default actions are the same `init_died` path as every other fatal signal); no physical hardware; no performance data.

**Remaining loud stops** after this integration: `kill(0)`/`kill(-1)`, the CPU-time interval timers, group stop, a file-backed `MAP_SHARED` write and the other mm/VFS stops listed in their sections.

## Consumed native-word bitmap revision

This section is historical005 behavior. The current one-buddy cutover removes the cyclic hint/address-order policy and its old verdict marker; see [current routing](#routed-implementation-status).

After director acceptance of the integrated-004/diagnostic-001/source-build-001 software checkpoint, implementation resumed with the existing allocator's consumed foundation rather than another isolated demonstration. Those artifacts stay immutable. [The core contract](subsystems/core.md#2-common-primitives-and-waitwakeup-machinery--m1m3) records the source map, five design questions, ordered acceptance and ownership transitions; [licensing](licensing.md#source-provenance-record-and-import-workflow) records the exact grants and notices.

`kernel/src/bitmap.rs` translates pinned Linux `FIND_NEXT_BIT`/`_find_next_zero_bit` into a zero-allocation exclusive native-word view. Checked capacity, a logical-size sentinel, first-word offset masking and tail-bit exclusion preserve next-zero semantics. `replace` consolidates the old byte backend and returns prior ownership; invalid indices do not mutate. `kernel/src/memory.rs` now initializes complete aligned metadata words before references, reserves their backing pages, and searches `[hint,total)` followed by `[0,hint)` with physical alignment filtering. All reservation/allocation/release callers use this one view; no duplicate allocator, atomic-bit claim or concurrent AP execution is added.

Four actual test implementations cover capacity/empty/max-size boundaries, independent bitwise expectations over native-word boundaries and tail patterns, replacement/previous-state/nonmutation, and the translated upstream500-bit clear-iteration fixture. The QEMU-only production allocator test exhausts all free frames, removes two reservation-chain heads before zero-on-reuse can erase their links, releases upper last, then requires upper-first/lower-after-wrap allocation, both pages zeroed, exact recovery,8KiB alignment and an alignment-filtered `NoMemory` without accounting loss. The maintained verifier requires the exact `CHECK bitmap-search ok wrap_reuse=2 aligned=8192 alignment_exhausted=true` record after these checks; missing, wrong or suffixed proof records are rejected.

Independent static algorithm/provenance and unsafe-consumer reviews found no allocator/search defect within their scoped files. The consumer reviewer found a real fixed-record validator omission: generic prefix matching alone accepted a contradictory appended field. Whole-record equality and missing/appended-record regressions corrected it; the reviewer independently closed that finding by source inspection. `integrated-005/artifact-review/independent-source-reviews.json` binds those bounded reviews, not legal or whole-kernel certification.

The fresh maintained **`target/kernel-tooling/integrated-005/`** run actually returned0 with **8/8** expected33/35 outcomes and no timeout. Its positive guest exhausted/restored **116,653** frames, passed the exact cyclic-reuse/alignment marker, all21 table-allocation failure points,15-GPR exception/IRQ checks and PF0/3/17. It used the existing exact toolchain and a fresh private build/output, not copied004 results:

```text
env PATH="/bin:$PATH" cargo +1.88.0 run -p xtask --locked --offline -- kernel-test --output target/kernel-tooling/integrated-005 --timeout-seconds 30
```

Self-test ELF SHA-256: **`cccd7851c762ca6e8a3151f7f8bdb4280d367901fc660f1613925385964a3dc8`**; diagnostic ELF: **`a0954fbeed1aac2a2ecd7eef987454196555cd3138d48e55113001a930539d82`**; PNG: **`b1187fe71f80f008bcd279f14f3a5ed3ddc4065a1ac57a2f9e088eaee95f2a9e`**. Director independently opened this1024x768 image and its result/manifest: noFAIL/cropping, complete checkpoint, usable116704/metadata4/initialfree116700, finalFREE116679/OWNED_TABLES21, four guards/W^X/noimagealias and PITwraps2/delta0. Before/extracted/after ELF hashes agree; HLT1/IF0/EFLAGS0x82/executableRIP and QMPquit0 cleanup remain bounded observations, not guest-exit proof. `default-boot/director-visual-review.json` records later acceptance without changing capture-time pending fields.

Fresh eight-command nm/readelf/objdump inspection of **both** new ELFs is retained under `integrated-005/artifact-review/`: ET_EXEC RX/R/RW, NX stack, no undefined/relocations/interpreter, complete Rust/core provider map and no loaded foreign runtime/FP/vector code. Project scalar `memset`, compiler interrupt returns, and every default port site were inspected; default has PIC/PIT/delay only, noCOM1/f4. The new search lowers to four nonzero-guarded `F3 REX.W 0F BC` sites per profile without consuming their flags. [Intel SDM253667-093US, Vol2B4-731](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/929354/253667-093-sdm-vol-2b.pdf) specifies BSF execution on CPUs lacking TZCNT, preserving these guarded results. Existing compiler-generated aggregate `rep movsq` sites are explicitly recorded, not mistaken for SIMD or a foreign helper. This separate manual provider review is not an automatic harness claim.

Actual strict gates: host Rust1.88 Clippy and **46 tests/2suites** passed; the first nightly attempt then failed Clippy `manual_bits` on the new word-width constant. The unsuppressed failure remains in `artifact-review/host-gates-initial-failure.txt`; replacing `size_of::<usize>()*8` with `usize::BITS as usize` fixed it without a lint allow. The resumed nightly workspace strict-Clippy/**55tests/3suites**/format/check/status chain and **both** fresh freestanding strict-Clippy profiles passed. The inherited `core` softfloat future-incompatibility warning remains unsuppressed. `artifact-review/gates.json` and raw passed output record exact commands:97,294 inventory entries,2,623 workstreams,6in_progress/2,617not_started,16skills,56Markdown and1,493 checked local file links;683 external destinations and all fragment anchors remain unvalidated. Those structural counts precede this result-summary edit, not a Linux completion percentage.

This remains one-BSP, non-atomic early allocation, not Linux buddy/NUMA/reclaim/SLAB, SMP synchronization, scheduling,32-bit/big-endian qualification, physical evidence or a performance comparison. Before AP concurrency, the console/fault globals, per-CPU descriptor/emergency stacks, allocator/page-table ownership and IRQ/TLB lifecycle contracts must be migrated; the old BSP proof cannot be reused as an SMP proof.

## Raw IRQ-save exclusion revision

After immutable integrated-005, [sync.rs](../kernel/src/sync.rs) adds an allocation-free native atomic ownership bit and non-transferable guard. The actual [PIC/PIT consumer](../kernel/src/cpu.rs) disables local maskable interrupts before acquiring it, serializes each latch/low/high transaction, and releases raw ownership before restoring local exclusion. It is not an unused synchronization demonstration or a substitute for Linux qspinlock, RT/preemption/lockdep, CPU hotplug or weak-memory qualification. No AP is released by this revision.

| Actor / resource | Ownership, visibility and legal operations |
| --- | --- |
| BSP controller initialization | Before AP release, serialize the complete PIC/PIT programming transaction; saved-IF guard keeps the local IRQ from interrupting it. The existing one-BSP/APIC-disabled legacy policy is not SMP interrupt routing. |
| Ordinary PIT reader / masking | Same raw lock protects each shared port transaction. No allocation, scheduling, user access, fallible destruction or arbitrary callback occurs in normal critical sections. |
| Timer IRQ | Acknowledge PIC EOI without a blocking controller lock. During the bounded bootstrap probe only, attempt `try_lock` once and atomically report its actual result. Interrupt-gate IF remains clear. |
| NMI, fault, panic, emergency output | Never enter this nesting domain or take the blocking controller lock. Console remains an atomic-layout/atomic-cursor, raw-MMIO character sink; it permits interleaving rather than claiming atomic whole records or Linux printk. Its sole initialization is now an unsafe at-most-once, complete-resource-lifetime boundary. |
| Raw lock / compiler | Successful native CAS is Acquire, release-store unlock is Release, failed CAS and polling are Relaxed with no payload access. Field destruction orders raw unlock before IRQ exclusion release. Final restoration has a compiler-only `SeqCst` fence plus non-`nomem` STI; that compiler contract and x86 ordering are distinct from DMA/weak-CPU barriers. |
| IRQ nesting | One BSP-local packed word holds depth and the first owner's IF. Checked entry and atomic final removal preserve that original state under arbitrary guard destruction order. `!Send`/`!Sync` prevents transfer but is not the nesting proof. This word and all other BSP-only state must become genuinely per-CPU before AP release. |

Director found a real safe-API flaw in the first unexecuted guard draft: two independent saved-IF guards allowed `drop(outer)` to enable interrupts while `inner` still promised exclusion. The packed nesting protocol replaces that LIFO assumption; source review independently closed the finding. Integrated-006 actually created two guards with initial IF0 and IF1, dropped the first while the second remained live, required continued masking, then required original-state restoration and zero nesting. Normal nested scopes and failed lock acquisition also retained their guards. Corrected-entry integrated-008 also exercised these consumers.

The hardware consumer test holds the real controller lock across two observed PIT reloads with zero IRQ delta, then arms the first pending timer IRQ to try that same lock. Correct release requires real resumed IRQ delivery and successful acquisition. The ninth self-test case, `test=irq-restore-early`, deliberately drops IRQ exclusion while retaining raw ownership and polls across two real PIT wraps; this crosses STI's interrupt shadow instead of assuming immediate delivery. The same acquisition invariant must fail with actual IRQ delivery and `lock_acquired=false`, then exit35. Default builds never select this mutation. Finite polling is a failure bound, not calibrated elapsed time.

The host consumer requires exactly one complete bitmap proof, exactly one complete IRQ-guard proof (including `out_of_order=true`), and exactly one restore observation. It rejects appended conflicting, duplicate, suffixed or truncated records. Independent review also caught the package consumer's stale eight-case snapshot; execution and packaging now share the actual case-name/expected-exit catalog, validate its count and capture every case. A real file-validation/copy/hash regression covers the new case, missing/failed receipts and count mismatch; these unit fixtures are explicitly not guest evidence.

Integrated-006 actually matched9/9 cases and received its own director visual/log review. Its positive consumer observed two blocked PIT wraps/delta0 and resumed wraps2/delta1/acquiredtrue; the genuine early-restore case observed wraps2/delta1/acquiredfalse then exited35. Four raw-lock host tests and two compile-fail ownership examples passed; host50/nightly63 tests and both freestanding strict-Clippy profiles passed after separately retained oversized-test and renamed-atomic-API failures. Its independent runtime review subsequently found the error-code entry defect below; this does not erase the narrower IRQ results or qualify physical use.

## Error-code entry defect, reproduction and repair

Independent review found that every hardware-error-code entry emitted by the exact compiler used eleven qword pushes before an ordinary call. [Intel SDM Vol3A6.14.2](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812391/325384-sdm-vol-3abcd.pdf) specifies pre-push16-byte alignment and unconditional SS/RSP saves: five frame words plus an error word yield entryRSPmod16=0, so those88 bytes leave pre-CALL8 instead of0. [Exact rustc ABI mapping](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/blob/5c543b0b8c73c7b72bc8284ced4fb22ead15734d/compiler/rustc_codegen_llvm/src/abi.rs#L796-L823) uses ordinary LLVM CCallConv for Rust/C. The terminal `unexpected`/formatting chain has no hidden realignment. Frozen004/diagnostic001 share the emitted defect. [LLVM44560](https://github.com/llvm/llvm-project/issues/44560) describes the same pattern, but its closed status does not establish an applicable compiler fix.

The missing real consumer was added before repair. **Integrated-007 actually returned1,9/10 matched:** an unarmed access to the verified unmapped guard entered the fatal #PF path; a naked ordinary-callee entry witness observed `rsp_mod16=0`, then `FAIL trap-call-abi`. Guest exit35 was correctly rejected rather than mistaken for the expected fault diagnostic. Its raw case, manifest, source and selfELF `ed671d527e9e6dd772dbe1720ae65f03cbf47f28185e76e1ba43bf424defa94d` remain immutable. This is a controlled emulator ABI observation, not a reported physical crash.

Only the broken error-code boundary was replaced: vectors8/10/11/12/13/14/17/21/29/30 use minimal function-local `naked_asm!`, saving nine ordinary caller-clobbered GPRs plus eight padding bytes. The hardware error/frame are at +80/+88; CLD precedes an aligned CALL into an explicit ordinary C-ABI **Rust** handler. Reverse pops, error removal and IRETQ restore the hardware context. Ordinary Rust preserves RBX/RBP/R12–R15; a new real returning-PF probe checks all15 distinct full-width sentinels, not merely the older BP/IRQ paths. Non-error compiler entries remain. No forced inlining, compiler/global setting change, foreign shim or standalone/global assembly was used.

**Integrated-008 actually returned0,10/10 matched.** Its fatal-PF case measured ordinary-callee entry residue8 and emitted the genuine vector14/error0 diagnostic; printed saved interruptedRSP is not raw handler-entryRSP. Positive execution preserved PF15GPRs, restored116633 frames and passed21 table-failure points plus IRQ/bitmap/exception consumers. SelfELF`32cdae419e5e050799fef9f3635860e21011c4d03fe65da934305531355df8f2`; defaultELF`beca60940b86cbf1bb8e0496d5eeab02a29ca47ad7abdf46a9a942618091117d`; PNG`db44fe6b28591dd01e61feded2aa0c7898ce311599f7793d2454ea6f124afd2e`. Director actually opened its legible1024x768/noFAIL image and read manifest/result/positive/fatal logs: finalfree116657/tables21, HLT1/IF0, wrapped guard record intact. Separate sidecars preserve original pending fields.

Actual corrected gates: host1.88 strict Clippy/**52 tests**, exact-nightly strict workspace Clippy/**65 tests**, **two compile-fail ownership docs**, both freestanding strict-Clippy profiles and formatting passed. The first target attempt's `doc_markdown` failure is retained at `target/kernel-tooling/abi-gates-001/target-doc-markdown-failure.txt`; it was repaired with backticks, not suppressed. Final raw chain is retained under `integrated-008/artifact-review/strict-gates.stdout-stderr.txt`. Existing core future-incompatibility warnings remain unsuppressed.

The separate held **diagnostic-002 software consumer** produced archiveSHA`dfa28cbcb6a6a5275153ebc6a314605dee58529587d7d7791208b9a8efde9866` from006. Read-only inspection/extraction verified4755 safe members,3783 checksums,4754 mtree records,23 project/3663 runtime files and45 files for nine cases. Fresh source-copy build0 had matching loaded segments but different wholeELF/map. `diagnostic-002/inspection/review.json` and `source-build-002/review.json` retain those facts; neither repairs or clears those withdrawn bytes.

Independent008 repair review found no actionable defect under BSP/CPL0/scalar/no-unwind assumptions: `abi-fix-002/independent-review/review.json` SHA`20ef8259abfa001a1de46fbca684342fe56b0066b570791441673ede667fb0f5`,107-file seal`2b7d4f34f8b9c22b447698d07631a6010a94e712e95745dfe66c48d381ea1bb6`. All20 error entries, complete terminal/return paths and the real witness were inspected. Hardware/pre-CALL residues are derived, not debugger measurements. Fresh author provider/FP/port review separately covers both008 profiles; the independent repair review is not a second complete provider audit.

Actual **diagnostic-003** replaces, never clears,001/002: archiveSHA`bda84047e66a4c3be71a4525b31389a95118a5df69cc23b322a4bbc3e291078f`, original defaultELF`beca60940b86cbf1bb8e0496d5eeab02a29ca47ad7abdf46a9a942618091117d`. Real package0/read-only consumers verify4761 safe members,3788 checksums,4760 full mtree records,23 project/3663 runtime sources and50 files for all10 cases. Genuine BUILDINFO/PKGBUILD records distinguish assembly from earlier kernel compilation. Fresh source-copy build0/9.47s yieldsELF`e4ddb9b1aa5454eaf9f7a11a812d5c3f5dea6733e70667de7e5f418667b7fc52`: all3 loaded segments/metadata same, wholeELF/map different; actual source-root strings and nonloaded debug/symbol differences are recorded. Director opened the archive/metadata and accepted these records, not an independent checksum/rebuild audit. ## Executable interfaces and evidence status

Installed prerequisites actually exercised: exact nightly rustc `1.101.0-nightly (5c543b0b8c73c7b72bc8284ced4fb22ead15734d)`, LLVM 23.1.1, matching installed `rust-src`, bundled `rust-lld`, QEMU 11.1.2, Limine 12.9.3 and OVMF files under `/usr/share/edk2/x64`. Target `core`/`compiler_builtins` are rebuilt with this same compiler for `x86_64-unknown-none`; no host Linux `core`, stable/nightly mixture, global rustup mutation or `build.rs` is used. Kernel package MSRV declaration is 1.101; an older compiler is not claimed tested. Exact target specification is retained at `target/kernel-first-boot/target-spec.json`: kernel code model, red-zone disabled, soft-float and SSE/AVX disabled, panic abort, inline stack probes, 64-bit atomics. Because the target defaults to PIE, explicit static/no-PIE flags are required.

The host workspace's Rust1.88 declaration is an engineering tooling-baseline/metadata alignment, not a user-selected Rust version or the runtime ABI pin. A Rust1.88 host gate must select `xtask`/`baremetal` (or `--workspace --exclude kernel`), because the kernel package declares1.101. The nightly tests recorded here do not prove a1.88 pass. `cell9` snapshots preceded the root declaration's1.85→1.88 update; kernel implementation, runtime manifest and link flags stayed frozen, but the maintained harness must capture fresh source/manifest identities and execute its own runs rather than relabeling manual results.

From the repository root, the author used:

```text
cargo test -p kernel --lib --locked
cargo -Zbuild-std=core,compiler_builtins -Zbuild-std-features=compiler-builtins-mem rustc -p kernel --bin linux-rs-kernel --target x86_64-unknown-none --target-dir target/kernel-dev-build --release --locked --features qemu-self-test -- -Crelocation-model=static -Clink-arg=--no-pie -Clink-arg=-Tkernel/linker.ld -Clink-arg=--no-undefined -Clink-arg=-Map=target/kernel-dev-build/kernel.map
```

The diagnostic profile substitutes `--features freestanding`, using a separate target/output directory. `qemu-self-test` implies `freestanding`; neither is a default feature. Ordinary host workspace/all-target checks therefore build the testable library but do not try to link privileged entry as a host executable. Explicitly enabling `freestanding` for a non-x86_64-none target is rejected at compile time.

The actual ELF is `<target-dir>/x86_64-unknown-none/release/linux-rs-kernel`. The external Limine EFI application, `/linux-rs-kernel` and `/limine.conf` were placed into a project-local FAT image with installed `mkfs.fat`/mtools. No mounts or host devices were used. A real test entry is:

```text
timeout: 0
serial: yes

/linux-rs bootstrap
    protocol: limine
    path: boot():/linux-rs-kernel
    resolution: 1024x768x32
```

The author actually invoked QEMU with `-machine q35 -accel tcg -cpu qemu64 -m 128M -smp 1`, readonly CODE pflash `/usr/share/edk2/x64/OVMF_CODE.4m.fd`, a fresh project-local copy of `OVMF_VARS.4m.fd`, the raw FAT file, `-device isa-debug-exit,iobase=0xf4,iosize=4 -display none -serial file:<new-log> -monitor none -no-reboot`, bounded by external `timeout` (30–60 seconds). `-no-shutdown` is deliberately omitted: initial runs using it retained a stopped VM until timeout. Only the self-test feature compiles port `0xf4` and COM1 access. The non-self-test image uses the validated 32bpp RGB framebuffer and safe CLI/HLT; it has neither debug-exit nor assumed serial hardware.

The maintained Rust build/test harness is owned separately in `xtask`; its reproducible interface/evidence record is [kernel tooling](kernel-tooling.md). Manual development artifacts are isolated from harness outputs; subsequent manual builds use `target/kernel-dev-build`, not a shared canonical ELF. A fresh immutable per-run ELF/hash must be captured before staging images.

IRQ and breakpoint probes place different values in all 15 scalar GPRs and compare every register after hardware return; RSP/callee saves must balance for the probe to return. Timer counts are atomic monotonic event counts, **not** a calibrated Linux clocksource, high-resolution timekeeper or performance result.

Host tests execute the actual production range and RSDP/SDT/MADT parsers. The exercised corpus includes empty/truncated headers, table length overruns, corrupt checksums, zero/overlong MADT record lengths, unknown valid MADT records, overlapping/unaligned/overflowing memory regions, adjacent reserved/usable records, canonical-address holes and address overflow. Five named host tests passed; this does not execute hardware paths or qualify Rust UB universally. QEMU self-tests additionally test scalar `memset` boundaries/canaries, real allocator exhaustion and every real page-table allocation failure.

## Maintained integration and retained harness failure

The first real maintained invocation was `cargo +1.88.0 run -p xtask --locked --offline -- kernel-test --output target/kernel-tooling/integrated-001 --timeout-seconds 30`. It returned 1 after all eight cases and the default capture: seven cases matched, while `cpu-without-pat` exited 35 without timeout but the serial validator reported missing `FAIL cpu error=Pat`. All artifacts remain in that original directory.

Raw-byte inspection, rather than the control-filtered text display, identified two OVMF cursor-home sequences (`ESC[001;001H`) directly before the early CPU diagnostic after the second BdsDxe CRLF. No concatenated Limine text was observed. The repaired host validator accepts only complete leading CSI framing and retains anchored record/token checks, rejects embedded/malformed controls, and applies the same framing to forbidden success/failure diagnostics. Kernel sources and the required rejection behavior are unchanged. The production-verdict regression covers the observed boundary and false-positive alternatives; neither exit-only acceptance nor arbitrary substring matching is introduced.

After that repair, `cargo fmt -p xtask`, host Rust 1.88 strict Clippy for `xtask`/`baremetal`, and `cargo +1.88.0 test -p xtask -p baremetal --all-targets --locked --offline` passed with **41 tests in two suites**. These host results are distinct from the five kernel parser tests, from `cell9`, and from any later maintained guest/package evidence.

The repaired validator passed the real fresh `integrated-002` maintained run: all eight debug-exit cases matched and the default framebuffer/HMP capture completed. Director separately viewed and accepted that precise screenshot. Independent source review then found a conditional tool-provenance mismatch: `/bin/readelf` invocation was recorded literally while the runtime inventory used canonical `/usr/bin/readelf`. The manifest producer now records a canonical identity/hash and a separate invocation path, preserving multicall semantics. ## Final owned strict lint gate

After the director's read findings, integration verification and repository policy required strict Clippy before final freeze. `cargo fmt -p kernel`, `cargo clippy -p kernel --lib --tests --locked -- -D warnings` and `cargo test -p kernel --lib --locked` completed successfully; the five production-parser tests passed. The actual freestanding release target was also linted successfully with both `qemu-self-test` and, in its separate private target directory, `freestanding`:

```text
cargo clippy -Zbuild-std=core,compiler_builtins -Zbuild-std-features=compiler-builtins-mem -p kernel --bin linux-rs-kernel --target x86_64-unknown-none --target-dir target/kernel-dev-build --release --locked --features qemu-self-test -- -D warnings -Crelocation-model=static -Clink-arg=--no-pie -Clink-arg=-Tkernel/linker.ld -Clink-arg=--no-undefined -Clink-arg=-Map=target/kernel-dev-build/kernel.map
```

For the diagnostic lint invocation, the target directory/map were `target/kernel-dev-build/physical` and the feature was `freestanding`. Clippy is an external Cargo subcommand: placing the `-Zbuild-std` flags **before** `clippy` initially lost them and failed with missing `core`; forwarding them **after** `clippy` built/used the matching source correctly. No prebuilt stable core or global target installation was substituted. The inherited `core` soft-float future-incompatibility warning remains visible and unsuppressed even when owned-crate `-D warnings` passes.

Concrete fixes include checked address-to-pointer-size conversions before allocator publication, a typed `AtomicU32` pixel color, exact-size ACPI array chunks, complete accessor caller migration, precise error/must-use contracts, static-only BSS stack initialization and actual bootstrap-phase extraction. Narrow `#[expect]` sites document only: WRMSR's EDX:EAX split and IDT16/16/32 address packing; consuming the unique non-Copy frame token on release; the aligned-word cast after `memset`'s byte prologue; and Clippy's large-array warning on the constant used solely by the four permanent BSS stacks. There is no blanket correctness-warning suppression. `memset` explicitly extracts the ABI-defined low byte, including for negative integers.

Both final profiles were actually linked again after these changes, and every manual QEMU case was rerun against the immutable `cell9/self-test.elf`. These runs used `-serial stdio` redirected into newly created per-case logs, fresh private OVMF variables/image copies and a30-second subprocess deadline. Earlier failure and success artifacts remain intact. The strict lint gate and local boot evidence are not independent security/architecture review, a physical pass, M1 completion or full Linux implementation.

## Runtime and instruction closure

**Historical initial/cell9 closure:** the paragraphs below describe that frozen lineage. Current buddy/image consumers introduced a real `memcpy` dependency, now supplied by the reviewed scalar Rust provider in the [publication checkpoint](#publication-checkpoint); the former “not added” statement is not current source status.

The inspected ET_EXEC artifact has separate R-X/R--/RW- PT_LOAD segments and a non-executable GNU_STACK; no relocation entries or undefined symbols were reported. Full symbols/disassembly/layout are retained in manual development artifacts. The maintained harness captures ELF/program headers and hashes, but full fresh-snapshot symbol/disassembly/provider review remains a separate integration gate, not an automatic harness claim. The kernel panic symbol is historically named `__rustc::rust_begin_unwind`; its actual body calls the allocation-free panic/stop path. That name is not an unwinder, personality routine or unwind support. `.eh_frame` is discarded; debug-only `.debug_frame` is offline data.

The mutable Rust readonly-test sentinel gives the input `.rodata` section a W flag, but its PT_LOAD is R-- and the actual CPU write fault is checked. Program permissions, not the input section flag, control the loader/owned mappings. LLVM emits `rep movsq` for ordinary Rust value moves inside `kernel_main`/`BootInfo::take`; these are compiler-generated instructions, not linked assembly-source providers. The final scalar Rust `memset` has no recursive self-call or REP provider.

An actual symbol/disassembly inspection exposed `compiler_builtins::memset` selecting avoidable `rep stos*` inline assembly through `compiler-builtins/src/mem/{mod,x86_64}.rs` (compiler_builtins 0.1.160, feature `arch`; feature `c` is not selected). The final kernel instead provides the required `memset` in scalar Rust, with aligned volatile u64 stores and byte edges to prevent recursive libcall lowering. No speculative wall of runtime stubs is retained: memcpy/memmove/memcmp/bcmp are not added without an emitted requirement. Inspect the final symbol/link map after every compiler/profile/config change; a rebuilt compiler-builtins archive is not permission to link its unreviewed architecture routines.

The matching `core` manifest declares `MIT OR Apache-2.0`; this integration elects MIT for selected core code, retaining the toolchain's `share/doc/rust/{COPYRIGHT-library.html,licenses/MIT.txt}` provenance/notices for artifact distribution review. Compiler-builtins has the separate conjunctive license described in the project's licensing plan; it is built as a compiler input, but only actually linked providers may be called qualified. The CPU layer now links `x86_64` and `raw-cpuid` from crates.io (vendored; see [CPU layer on audited crates](#cpu-layer-on-audited-crates)); no other crates.io crate is linked. Final artifact-specific legal/SBOM/source-distribution approval remains open.

The matching `core` build reports future-incompatibility warnings from unused SSE/AVX intrinsic definitions on the soft-float target (Rust issue 117938). These are not suppressed; their report was inspected. Final executable disassembly must remain free of FP/vector instructions, CRT/libc/OS calls and unreviewed compiler helper providers. Compiler/runtime license review and reproducibility remain release gates; local successful linkage is not legal or toolchain-wide qualification.

## Assembly necessity, ABI and unsafe proof register

All authored kernel function-local assembly sites are in `kernel/src/cpu.rs` (the separate userspace `_start` is recorded under [First userspace program](#first-userspace-program); rows marked *crate* below moved into the audited `x86_64` crate: see [CPU layer on audited crates](#cpu-layer-on-audited-crates) for their register). Every authored site has a local safety comment. All execute only in this privileged x86_64 BSP cell, never unwind, and never establish an SMP lock. No standalone assembly, `global_asm!`, foreign runtime or C-backed dependency exists in the kernel tree. Review remains open; the table records engineering contracts, not self-issued architecture/security approval.

| Site / necessary operation | Entry and registers/flags | Stack, memory, ordering and return | Exercised evidence / residual |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `_start`: CLI/CLD, install RSP, call Rust | Limine base5 long-mode CPL0, image/BSS live, no FP ABI; writes RSP/RBP and IF/DF, SysV call clobbers apply | No compiler prologue. Stack top page-aligned; CALL creates SysV RSP mod16=8. RBP=0; kernel main never returns; UD2 catches an erroneous return. Rust cannot install its own RSP before a compiler prologue. | Real ELF entry/disassembly and every candidate boot; pre-IDT NMI/physical failure coverage remains open. |
| *crate* `validate`: CR0/CR4 reads, CR0.EM/TS write and, only if a loader set it, a CR4.OSXSAVE clear (with it clear XSAVE/XGETBV are #UD and AVX is unavailable, so no YMM/ZMM state reaches ring 3, as `noxsave`; a loader's setting can no longer stop a diagnostic boot) (the former `mov rsp` read is now a containment check on the address of a live local in the current frame, not a read of RSP itself) | CPL0, IF=0; explicit scalar output registers; MOV preserves flags | Read sites are `nomem,nostack,preserves_flags`. Write preserves PE/PG/WP/reserved bits and excludes memory reordering. EM/TS make unspecified firmware xstate inaccessible; OSFXSR/OSXSAVE remain off. No supported Rust intrinsic sets these registers. | Actual CPU rejection/readback and scalar-only code inspection; full xstate/task context/mitigation support remains open. |
| *crate* MSR access (`Msr::read`/`write`; former `read_msr`/`write_msr`) | EFER/APIC_BASE/PAT only after relevant CPUID checks; ECX input, EDX:EAX output/input; CPL0, flags preserved | No stack. Reads `nomem`; writes intentionally memory-clobbering compiler boundaries. APIC_BASE write only disables xAPIC for the explicit UP legacy-PIC cell; x2APIC is rejected. These register operations have no supported Rust intrinsic. | NX/PAT checks, masked-PAT negative, working legacy timer; not APIC/x2APIC or physical cache-controller qualification. |
| *crate* CR3 activate/readback (`Cr3::write_raw`/`read_raw`) | Unsafe root publication requires live aligned owned tables; CPL0 IF=0, PCIDE/PGE=0 from base5; scalar root input/output | MOV CR3 has compiler memory clobber, flushes nonglobal translations before execution continues. Current code/stack/descriptors/metadata/display keep identical addresses. No root/page reclamation while active. Readback is `nomem,nostack,preserves_flags`. No Rust MMU intrinsic suffices. | Owned CR3 equality, actual guard/RO/NX faults; no SMP shootdown/PCID/global mapping claim. |
| `interrupts_off` and `halt` are *crate* (`interrupts::disable`, `hlt`); `wait_tick` and the mask-test STI stay authored | CLI/STI change IF; HLT preserves flags; CPL0, valid IDT for enabling interrupts | CLI/STI/wait omit `nomem` so memory work cannot cross exclusion. HLT stop has IF=0 and no ordinary memory work; NMI remains possible. STI/HLT exploits architectural one-instruction inhibition, then CLI restores early context. No Rust equivalent controls IF/HLT. | Real IRQ delivery/masking and external deadlines; no physical idle/power/latency qualification. |
| `out_byte` / `in_byte` / `io_wait` | Owned PIC ports20/21,a0/a1; PIT40/43; legacy delay80. Only UART3f8–3ff is gated to QEMU self-test; PIT reads also exist in the diagnostic image. DX port, AL input/output; flags unchanged | `nostack,preserves_flags`, deliberately not `nomem`; device configuration/EOI is ordered against compiler memory work. No arbitrary exposed port API or port-I/O intrinsic. **Superseded:** these accessors are now `x86_64::instructions::port::Port` accesses bracketed by compiler fences ([interrupt controllers and timekeeping](#interrupt-controllers-and-timekeeping)). | Timer/EOI/mask and bounded UART output; physical port/device availability must be proved before a user trial. |
| QEMU-only `exit` OUT DX,EAX | Feature `qemu-self-test`, explicit virtual device at f4, EAX10/11, DXf4; IF=0 | No stack/flags change, compiler memory clobber. Returns to safe halt if no device responds. Entire function/callers absent from diagnostic build. Rust cannot issue port I/O. | Actual exits33/35; strictly no physical f4 use or shutdown implementation claim. |
| *crate* GDT/segments/TR load (`load_unsafe`, `CS`/`SS`/`DS`/`ES::set_reg`, `load_tss`); the far return now loads KERNEL_CS 0x10, data 0x18, TR 0x40 | Initialized permanent GDT/TSS; scalar selector/pointer inputs, one scratch register (`lateout`) in the crate's CS reload; CPL0 IF=0 | LGDT reads the descriptor. The far return pushes/pops exactly16 bytes and loads CS 0x10; DS/ES/SS 0x18, TR 0x40. LTR's busy-bit write is to owned GDT memory with no Rust references. No supported intrinsic installs these descriptors/segment state. | Own descriptors, BP/PF/IRQ execution; IST reentry/overflow/double-fault/NMI injection remains open. |
| *crate* LIDT (`tables::lidt`) | Initialized256-entry IDT, canonical handlers, CS 0x10 (copied from the live CS by `set_handler_addr`); descriptor pointer input, flags preserved | `readonly,nostack,preserves_flags` reads the descriptor and changes IDTR, never claimed `nomem`. No Rust intrinsic supplies this operation. | Real selected vectors and external IRQ; complete baseline entry coverage remains open. |
| Exception and interrupt entry, every vector (`entry!`, two arms) | Interrupt gate (IF=0 on entry); the CPU always pushes the five-word frame in 64-bit mode and aligns RSP to 16 bytes first, so RSP%16 is 8 at entry without a hardware error code and 0 with one (vectors 8, 10-14, 17, 21, 29, 30); no inputs (the vector is an assembler constant) | Pushes `orig_ax` (-1; with an error code it is read into RDX and overwritten by -1) then the fifteen GPRs, which is `PtRegs` below the hardware frame; `sub rsp,8` makes the CALL aligned (callee RSP%16==8); RDI=&mut `PtRegs`, ESI=vector, RDX=error; every GPR and the return CS/RIP/RFLAGS/RSP/SS are restored from `PtRegs` (a handler or signal frame may have rewritten them) and the stub leaves through `jmp` to the single `iret_site` (below); CLD for the C ABI; no red zone, no unwind, no FP/SIMD. Replaces the compiler `x86-interrupt` entries and the nine-register error-code stub: one stub, the full register file is needed by signal frames and `rt_sigreturn`. | Every kernel-test case (IRQ/PF/BP GPR proofs, terminal-PF call alignment, `init-segfault`, the `signals*` cases); `iretq` back to a rewritten frame in all three `signals` cases. Fresh independent architecture/unsafe review of this stub is required; physical hold remains. |
| `iret_site` (the one `iretq`; Linux `native_irq_return_iret`) | Reached by `jmp` from the `entry!` stubs and `syscall_entry` at CPL0, IF=0, every GPR restored, RSP at the five-word frame (RIP, CS, RFLAGS, RSP, SS) | Reads and writes no register; pops the frame; does not return. One address so that a refused frame (`#GP`/`#NP`/`#SS` at this address, RSP unchanged: SDM, faults leave state as before the instruction) can be recognised by `cpu::fixup_bad_iret`, which copies the user's five words from the frame into the exception frame (bounds/alignment-checked on `BOOT_STACK`, vectors 10-13 only, kernel CS and this RIP only, ring-3 CS in the stale frame) and counts consecutive refusals (limit 16, loud stop). No intrinsic exists for `iretq`. | `signals*` cases: bad RIP, CS, SS (all three `signals` cases) and, on KVM, a compatibility RIP beyond 4 GiB (`#GP` at the `iretq`; TCG does not check the limit and the case is skipped there); a non-canonical RSP resumes in user mode (TCG and KVM). Pinned Linux prints the same lines for all of them (`target/sigfix-linux/`). |
| *crate* Page-fault CR2 read (`Cr2::read_raw`) | CPL0 #PF handler, scalar output, flags unchanged | `nomem,nostack,preserves_flags`; read before fallible work. Expected fault requires armed tuple: vector14, exact CR2/RIP/error; otherwise terminal. No Rust intrinsic reads CR2. | Actual errors0/3/17, readonly contents retained; fault-aware usercopy and task faults remain open. |
| `probe_read/write/execute/return` | Unsafe controlled-test mappings only; RDI address, RSI write value, RAX read result; same CPL, IF=0 | Minimal naked fault PC is stable and has no prologue; read/write touches exact tested8 bytes, NX jump targets an owned page containing RET. Recovery RET uses the existing call return address; no hidden stack adjustment/unwind. Ordinary Rust cannot perform a controlled illegal memory/fetch access with a specified recoverable hardware PC without invoking language UB. | Real guard/RO/NX tests; unarmed/mismatched faults stop. |
| GPR BP/IRQ/PF probes | All15 GPRs receive distinct full-width sentinels; six SysV callee-saved values first preserved; scalar result gates success | Shared naked test sets/compares all named registers. PF pads its ordinary CALL to a RIP-relative unmapped-guard read without overwriting a sentinel; exact recovery RET resumes all15 comparisons. BP uses INT3; IRQ uses STI/HLT/CLI. Saves/pops/RET are balanced, no business logic or FP moves to assembly. | Actual008 returning-PF15 proof plus BP/IRQ15 checks, not SMP/weak-memory/extended-state qualification. |
| Ordinary-call alignment witness | Self-test-only naked C-ABI callee reads entryRSPmod16 before a prologue; RAX/flags are caller-clobbered | MOV/AND/RET observe without realignment. The genuine unarmed-PF test requires residue8 and the actual terminal vector/error diagnostic; a bad witness is a failed test even if exit35 matches. | Actual007 residue0 failure precedes actual008 residue8/genuinePF success; the witness is not a workaround for a broken caller. |
| Deliberate INT3 (*crate* `int3`), INT40 and UD2 terminal negatives | Valid IDT; no armed BP permit, unassigned interrupt slot or invalid-op handler; CPL0 | INT40 and UD2 stay authored `noreturn`; INT3 is the crate's `int3` (`nomem, nostack`, not `noreturn`) followed by `halt()`, see the crate table for why that is acceptable; no unwind; architectural traps have no normal-Rust replacement. Shared unassigned entries use a private sentinel but print `unknown-external`, never a fabricated actual vector. | Explicit vector3/vector6 and honest unknown-interrupt exit35 logs, no success marker. |
| `syscall_entry` (IA32_LSTAR target), first-userspace slice | `SYSCALL` from CPL3 only: RCX=user RIP, R11=user RFLAGS, RAX=number, args RDI/RSI/RDX/R10/R8/R9; IF/DF/TF/AC/NT/IOPL cleared by SFMASK (Linux `MSR_SYSCALL_MASK`); user RSP still live | One global scratch word holds the user RSP (shortcut: per-CPU/`swapgs` at SMP), RSP becomes the guarded `BOOT_STACK` top (= TSS.RSP0); 21 pushes build a Linux `pt_regs`, `sub 8` gives CALL alignment, C-ABI handler writes `pt_regs.ax`, restore and `iretq` (shortcut: SYSRET fast path). No red zone, no unwind, not reentrant. Rust cannot switch stacks before a prologue or build the frame. | Real `user/init` and 12 tiny inits (manual QEMU, qemu64 and `-cpu max`); SMP, signals and `int 0x80` are open. |
| `syscall32_ignore` (IA32_CSTAR target) | SYSCALL from compatibility mode on AMD only; RCX/R11 as above | `mov eax,-ENOSYS; sysret` (0F 07, SYSRETD); no stack/memory use. Prevents ring 0 jumping to address 0 through a zero CSTAR. | Disassembly only (assembled and linked in both profiles); **never executed**: no compat-mode test program or AMD CPU. |
| `UserCpu::ss_is_valid_user_data`: `lar` of the user `ss` (the `force_valid_ss` test and the exit-path frame check) | Any CPL; a 16-bit selector in a register (user-controlled, any value); output a 32-bit register preset to 0, unchanged when LAR fails (ZF clear), so a failure reads as rights 0 as Linux's explicit zeroing does; ZF clobbered | `nomem, nostack`: reads only the descriptor-table entry the selector names, architecturally; no fault for any register-operand value (null, out of range, LDT and system selectors just clear ZF); no control flow, no unwind. No intrinsic and no crate wrapper exists (`core::arch`, `x86_64` 0.15.5, `raw-cpuid` checked); a software GDT walk would duplicate the hardware's selector rules. | `kernel-test` `signals*` (`bad-return` cs/ss cases), host fake in the signal tests; Linux's masks (`AR_DPL_MASK \| AR_S \| AR_P \| AR_TYPE_MASK`, accessed bit ignored) asserted in the source. |
| `UserCpu::fxsave` / `fxrstor` / `fpu_reset` and `mxcsr_mask`: `core::arch::x86_64::{_fxsave64,_fxrstor64}` in `#[target_feature(enable = "fxsr")]` fns | CPL0, CPUID FXSR/SSE/SSE2, CR4.OSFXSR=1, CR0.EM=TS=0 (`FPU_READY`, set by `init_user_fpu`, asserted by `UserCpu::new`); 16-byte aligned 512-byte image (`FxImage`); FXRSTOR images have no reserved MXCSR bit (re-checked against `mxcsr_mask`, else init state) | One 512-byte store or load; x87/XMM state only; no stack effect; no unwind. These are intrinsics (no `asm!` of ours); the kernel stays soft-float, so user FP state is live whenever the kernel runs. | `signals` (FXSAVE image in every frame, `fpu_reset` at handler entry), host fake. |
| `iret_to_user` (ring-3 entry) | CPL0, TSS.RSP0 valid, RDI=RIP, RSI=RSP; RFLAGS pushed 0x202, CS 0x33, SS 0x2b | CLI, five-word IRETQ frame on the abandoned boot stack, all 15 GPRs zeroed (Linux `elf_common_init`), no return, no unwind. | Real ring-3 entry in every manual run. |
| *crate* MSR writes (EFER.SCE, STAR, LSTAR, CSTAR except on Intel, SYSENTER_CS/ESP/EIP zero, SFMASK, FS/GS/KERNEL_GS bases), CR0/CR4 (SMEP, SMAP, UMIP, OSFXSR, OSXMMEXCPT, MP/NE, EM/TS clear), DR0-3/DR6/DR7 scrub, EFER.FFXSR clear, DS/ES/FS/GS null loads | `enable_syscalls`/`enable_user_protection`/`init_user_fpu`/`scrub_debug_registers`/`enter_user` are `unsafe fn` with contracts; every bit CPUID-gated; CPL0, IF=0 | Crate `wrmsr`/`mov cr`/`mov dr` blocks have no `nomem`; CR `update` preserves other bits. Disassembly (both profiles, kernel-test `p3-test-001`): `wrmsr` 13 = 8 in `enable_syscalls`, 3 in `enter_user`, 1 in `init_user_fpu` (EFER.FFXSR cleared before `FXRSTOR`), 1 pre-existing in `start_timer`; one `mov dr6` (now behind `reset_dr6`, shared by the boot scrub and `take_dr6` for `#DB`; the only authored asm added for the debug registers: no crate or intrinsic writes DR6; CPL0, one register input, `nomem, nostack, preserves_flags`, value `DR6_RESERVED` 0xffff0ff0 after DR7 is cleared); no `swapgs`, `syscall`, `sysenter`, `sysretq`; `iretq` 14; `sysretd` 1. | CR4 readback printed in `CHECK userspace exec`; `#DB` IST and scrubbed DR7 never executed against a real breakpoint. |
| `load_initial_fpu_state`: `FXRSTOR` of the 512-byte Linux initial image (`core::arch::x86_64::_fxrstor64` in a `#[target_feature(enable = "fxsr")]` fn) | CPL0, CPUID FXSR/SSE/SSE2, CR4.OSFXSR=1, CR0.EM=0 (set just before); input: pointer to an immutable 16-byte-aligned image (FCW 0x37f, MXCSR 0x1f80, rest zero) | One memory read of the image, writes x87/XMM state only; no stack effect; no unwind. The only FP/SIMD instruction in either ELF (`objdump`: one `fxrstor64`, no xmm/ymm/zmm/mm/x87 operand elsewhere, no `fninit`); the kernel stays soft-float, so ring 0 never modifies user FP state. | `user/init`'s `sse` mode (FCW/MXCSR/XMM5/XMM15 initial values, x87 add, SSE2 multiply) ran on the candidate (case `sse`) and on the pinned Linux oracle with identical results. |
| PIT mask-progress sampling | PIT channel0 is owned and initialized in mode2; IF temporarily1, restored0; DX/AL port operations as above | Latch command43:0 and paired40 reads observe the actual down-counter; two observed increases establish reload progress. Two-million-sample bound is only a failure limit, never calibrated elapsed time. IRQ handler never accesses PIT commands. | Positive wraps2/IRQdelta0; genuine unmasked mutation wraps2/IRQdelta1 fails the same assertion. Frequency/drift/timekeeper and physical qualification remain open. |

### Shared ownership and context proof

**Historical initial/cell9 ownership record:** the empty-lock-DAG statement below predates the consumed raw PIC/PIT controller lock and saved-IF guard. Current [IRQ ownership](#raw-irq-save-exclusion-revision) and [publication consumers](#publication-checkpoint) supersede it; BSP-only restrictions still apply and no private SMP candidate is landed.

Only BSP executes. No AP is started, no scheduler/preemption/reclaim/heap/I/O work exists, and device DMA is not handed kernel-owned buffers. The loader/firmware remain privileged trust dependencies; this is not protection from malicious firmware/DMA. Initial usable RAM is transferred once; metadata and every issued page are initialized with raw `ptr::write_bytes` **before** the first typed slice/frame borrow, without a duplicate zeroing pass. The allocator bitmap occupies actual usable pages and reserves itself before frame issuance. A non-Copy frame token gives the only live mutable byte borrow. Release consumes it; leaks can consume pages but cannot produce a second safe owner. Tables consume tokens into an encoded root/child ownership tree. Unpublished failure cleanup frees children before the parent and never frees mapped leaf RAM; active roots are not reclaimed.

Timer IRQs touch only a relaxed atomic counter and owned PIC EOI, not allocator/formatter state. Counter operations need no payload publication. Fault/BP permits use release/acquire for their expected tuple/one-shot state. Framebuffer geometry/color fields publish before the address release store; emergency readers acquire that address. Pixel writes use outside-Rust-allocation volatile MMIO, never references to device storage. Reserved framebuffer bytes are tail padding, not assumed initialized fields. Whole physical/virtual extents are checked for overflow, canonicality, class and pixel bounds. No locks are present, so this cell's lock-order DAG is empty; enabling APs, DMA, RT or task allocation requires new shared context/ownership mechanisms, not reuse of BSP assumptions.

Firmware access is not a generic safe RAM-slice facility. `BootInfo::acpi_table_bytes` is unsafe, returns a borrow bounded by `&BootInfo`, and requires actual initialized immutable RSDP/SDT storage with no firmware/SMM/AP/DMA/software writers or reclamation for that borrow. FACS/arbitrary NVS/runtime data is forbidden by the caller contract; reserved class alone proves neither initialization nor immutability. `firmware::discover` and its root/SDT traversal explicitly carry and discharge this external trusted-firmware contract, return only scalar summaries, and let no table references escape. Main names the trusted EFI/Limine/firmware phase and starts no runtime service/AP/DMA while discovering tables. Malicious or concurrently mutating firmware is not claimed safe. The GPR runtime probes use distinct Rust-static full64-bit patterns, with nonzero varied high and low halves and full64-bit comparisons; they no longer test only zero-extended32-bit values.

## CPU layer on audited crates

`kernel/src/cpu.rs` now uses `x86_64` 0.15.5 (`default-features = false`, feature `instructions`) and `raw-cpuid` 11.6.0 (admission records in [dependencies](dependencies.md#admitted-exact-configurations)). **Deleted:** the hand-packed `TablePointer`, `TaskState`, `Gate`, descriptor bit math, `read_msr`/`write_msr`, the `lgdt`/CS-reload/`ltr` and `lidt` asm blocks, five `mov crN` sites, the `mov rsp` read, the open-coded `cpuid` bit tests and the `cli`/`hlt`/`int3` asm (`asm!` sites in `cpu.rs`: 23 to 11; `cpu.rs` 192 lines removed, 207 added including the layout table, compile-time checks and SAFETY text). **Kept in-house:** port I/O (the crate's `Port<T>` is `nomem`, ours deliberately is not), `LocalInterruptGuard` and its `sti` (the crate's `interrupts::enable` is `sti; nop` and `are_enabled` is a `nomem` `pushfq`), `wait_tick`/`observe_timer_periods`/`irq_guard_test` STI sites, every naked entry stub and probe, `paging.rs` (untouched). The IDT is our own `[Entry<HandlerFunc>; 256]` filled with `Entry::set_handler_addr`, not the crate's `InterruptDescriptorTable` (its struct cannot hold reserved/error-code vectors and panics on indexing them), so the naked error-code stubs from the [error-entry repair](#error-code-entry-defect-reproduction-and-repair) are unchanged.

**GDT layout (Linux 6.18.57 `arch/x86/include/asm/segment.h`, 64-bit, read from the pinned source tree):** entry 1 `KERNEL32_CS` 0x08, 2 `KERNEL_CS` 0x10, 3 `KERNEL_DS` 0x18, 4 `USER32_CS` 0x23, 5 `USER_DS` 0x2b, 6 `USER_CS` 0x33, 7 null, 8-9 TSS 0x40 (SYSRET order). Descriptor words are the crate's `DescriptorFlags` constants, which equal Linux `desc_defs.h` `DESC_CODE64`/`DESC_DATA64`/`DESC_CODE32` (+`DESC_USER`): I recomputed `0x00af9b..`, `0x00cf93..`, `0x00cf9b..`, `0x00affb..`, `0x00cff3..`, `0x00cffb..` from the pinned macros, and `cpu.rs` pins them with compile-time `const` assertions; `install_gdt` asserts each `append` returns the expected selector. Changed values: IDT gate selector 8 to 0x10, CS reload 8 to 0x10, data selectors 16 to 0x18, TR 24 to 0x40, `interrupt_handler`'s kernel-CS check `cs != 0x10` (any other CS, including ring 3, is still treated as unexpected: ring-3 handling is a later change). Code descriptors now have the accessed bit set (0x9b, as Linux; the old 0x9a made the CPU write the GDT on first use). FS/GS selectors are left as loaded by Limine, as before. No serial record changed.

Crate privileged `asm!` actually linked (all **read** in `vendor/x86_64-0.15.5/src`, instructions confirmed present by `objdump`). "Matches" means the options are right for this kernel's use.

| Operation (crate function) | Instruction and options | Matches the operation? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RDMSR (`Msr::read`; PAT 0x277, APIC_BASE, `Efer::read`) | `rdmsr`, ECX in, EAX/EDX out, `nomem, nostack, preserves_flags` | Yes: pure register read. `unsafe fn`; callers prove the MSR exists (CPUID checked) |
| WRMSR (`Msr::write`; APIC_BASE only) | `wrmsr`, `nostack, preserves_flags`, **no `nomem`** | Yes: memory clobber keeps compiler memory work from crossing a CPU-state change. Only the enable bit changes; other bits are written back as read |
| CR0/CR2/CR3/CR4 reads (`Cr0::read`, `Cr2::read_raw`, `Cr3::read_raw`, `Cr4::read`) | `mov r, crN`, `nomem, nostack, preserves_flags` | Yes. Safe `fn` in the crate; valid only at CPL0, which is the only context here |
| CR0 write (`Cr0::write` via `write_raw`) | `mov cr0, r`, `nostack, preserves_flags`, no `nomem` | Yes. `write` re-reads CR0 and keeps reserved bits, so only EM/TS change |
| CR3 write (`Cr3::write_raw`) | `mov cr3, r`, `nostack, preserves_flags`, no `nomem` | Yes: memory ordering against the TLB flush is kept; flags 0 as before; frame validated aligned by `PhysFrame::from_start_address` |
| LGDT (`GlobalDescriptorTable::load_unsafe`) | `lgdt [r]`, `readonly, nostack, preserves_flags` | Yes: reads the descriptor, changes GDTR, not claimed `nomem`. Table is a permanent static with atomic entries |
| CS reload (`CS::set_reg`) | `push sel; lea tmp,[55f+rip]; push tmp; retfq; 55:`, `lateout(reg) tmp`, `preserves_flags` | Yes: stack used (no `nostack`), no `nomem`; pops exactly the 16 bytes pushed; disassembly shows `mov ecx,0x10; push rcx; lea; push; retfq` |
| SS/DS/ES reload (`SS/DS/ES::set_reg`) | `mov seg, r16`, `nostack, preserves_flags` | Yes. FS/GS are not reloaded |
| LTR (`load_tss`) | `ltr r16`, `nostack, preserves_flags`, no `nomem` | Yes: LTR writes the busy bit into the GDT, so no `nomem`. Precondition: descriptor not already busy (first and only load) |
| LIDT (`tables::lidt`) | `lidt [r]`, `readonly, nostack, preserves_flags` | Yes |
| CS read (inside `Entry::set_handler_addr`) | `mov r16, cs`, `nomem, nostack, preserves_flags` | Yes; gates are filled after the new CS is live |
| CLI (`interrupts::disable`) | `cli`, `preserves_flags, nostack`, no `nomem` | Yes: same barrier property as the former site |
| HLT (`hlt`) | `hlt`, `nomem, nostack, preserves_flags` | Yes: identical to the former options |
| INT3 (`interrupts::int3`; terminal negative test only) | `int3`, `nomem, nostack` | **Weaker than ideal**: the handler's memory effects are not ordered by the options. Acceptable only because the sole caller is the terminal fatal case followed by `halt()`; do not use it where code after the trap depends on memory the handler writes |
| CPUID (`raw-cpuid` over `core::arch::x86_64::__cpuid_count`) | compiler intrinsic, scalar clobbers declared | Yes: same intrinsic as before. `CpuId::new()` runs a few CPUIDs, so it is used only in `validate`/`start_timer` |

Crate functions that are safe `fn` but privileged and **not** used (no wrapper needed yet): `LStar`/`Star`/`SFMask`/`GsBase`/`KernelGsBase::write`. Add `unsafe fn` wrappers with SAFETY contracts before the first syscall-entry slice uses them. Other crate points: `VirtAddr::new` panics on a non-canonical address (used only on our own higher-half statics; user-controlled addresses must use `try_new`); `Efer`/`Cr0` helpers re-read before writing.

### CPU-layer evidence (worktree `arch-crates`, 2026-10-19)

Final source is the branch head after "make the CPU-layer crates optional behind freestanding". Every command below returned 0: `cargo fmt --all --check`; `cargo +1.88.0 clippy -p xtask -p baremetal --all-targets --locked --offline -- -D warnings`; `cargo +1.88.0 test -p xtask -p baremetal --locked --offline` (10 + 52 tests); `cargo +nightly clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked --offline -- -D warnings`; `cargo +nightly test --workspace --locked --offline` (10 + 59 + 52 + 2 doc tests); both target-specific strict Clippy commands (`arch-clippy-{qemu-self-test,freestanding}-003`); `check` (7 vendored packages verified) and `status`; fresh `env PATH="/bin:$PATH" cargo +1.88.0 run -p xtask --locked --offline -- kernel-test --output target/kernel-tooling/arch-003 --timeout-seconds 60` matched **11/11** cases. `arch-003`'s default-boot screenshot (opened and read) shows the banner and every `CHECK`/`IRQ`/`TIMER` record through `CHECKPOINT BOOTSTRAP-COMPLETE FREE_PAGES=112306 OWNED_TABLES=22`, the same records as before the migration; human review remains pending. Retained failures and superseded runs, not erased: the first target Clippy attempts (`arch-clippy-*-001`) failed with `clippy::too_many_lines` on `install_descriptors` (split into `install_gdt`/`install_idt`) and, without `--config`, with unresolved std dependencies; `arch-001` and `arch-002` also matched 11/11 but are superseded by `arch-003` (a split-only refactor, then the optional-feature manifest change, altered the bytes). A `kernel-package` run (`package-arch-001`, exit 0) staged 260 `vendor/` files and `.cargo/config.toml` into the corresponding-source bundle; the package consumer unit tests passed.

Follow-up: with the main checkout also carrying the identical `.cargo/config.toml`, `kernel-test --output target/kernel-tooling/arch-004` from this nested worktree matched 11/11 (exit 0) after `reject_build_overrides` was taught to accept an identical enclosing-checkout config; strict clippy, host 1.88 (10+53 tests), nightly workspace (10+59+53+2) and `check` also returned 0.

Fresh clone: `git clone --branch feat/arch-crates` into a scratch directory, then `CARGO_HOME=<empty directory> CARGO_NET_OFFLINE=true cargo +1.88.0 run -p xtask --locked --offline -- check` returned 0 and `... -- kernel-build --output target/kernel-tooling/fresh-001` returned 0 (the empty `CARGO_HOME` stayed empty; only the pinned toolchains were used). Its ELF has the same executable segment extent as `arch-003`'s but a different whole-file hash (`980ec0ec...`; embedded build paths differ), so it is not claimed bit-identical. It does not cover `kernel-test`'s two external CPIO inputs.

ELF inspection (`arch-003`): freestanding SHA-256 `ec78dcbabd0777d34be3637a6ce7a55c038a83197a4ad4a7b142bd80f4e58456`, `qemu-self-test` `4d9c09c1624b1f2342de8a94a30674a2fc806df894dd44747e25654c1469d7e8`. `nm -u` is empty for both; no XMM/YMM/MMX/x87/FXSAVE/XSAVE/LDMXCSR instruction in either disassembly; the crates contribute no `rep` string instruction (the two `rep movs` are the existing compiler-emitted ones). Privileged instructions present (freestanding): `lgdt`, `lidt`, `ltr`, `retfq`, `mov ss/ds/es`, `mov` to/from CR0/CR2/CR3/CR4, `mov r, cs`, 3 `rdmsr`, 1 `wrmsr`, 5 `cpuid` (was 4: `raw-cpuid` adds the construction-time maximum-leaf query), 12 `iretq`, 4 `ud2` (base 5), `cli` 64 and `hlt` 51 (base 63 and 50; inlining of the crate wrappers shifts a site), `sti` 20 and `pushfq` 20 unchanged. Port I/O is unchanged against a build of the base branch (`fix/tools-audit` head, `base-001`): 28 `out dx,al` and 10 `in al,dx` in both, no `out dx,eax` (the `qemu-self-test` profile was not rebuilt on the base, so only its absolute counts, 36/11/54 for out-al/in-al/out-eax, are recorded). Disassembly of the GDT install shows the reviewed sequence `mov ecx,0x10; push rcx; lea; push; retfq; mov cx,0x18; mov ss/ds/es; mov cx,0x40; ltr`.

Not done: independent architecture/unsafe/security review of the crate asm and the new GDT layout; `cargo audit`; upstream-tag-to-crates.io-archive diff; ring-3 execution of the new user selectors (nothing enters ring 3 yet, so `USER*_CS`/`USER_DS` and the DPL-3 descriptors are asserted only by value, not exercised); IST/double-fault/NMI injection (unchanged open item); physical hardware (none; no diagnostic-003 clearance applies to these bytes).

## Independent read findings and remaining qualification

The director's independent static read identified disconnected bad-map evidence, missing host feature isolation, a missing paging request, checksum-only malformed-data testing, PAT assumptions, unspecified framebuffer bytes, an overbroad assembly-necessity assertion, fabricated shared-vector diagnosis, references formed before RAM initialization, an overbroad static firmware slice, uncalibrated spin-based mask evidence and zero-high-half register patterns. Fixes use one production validator, a required freestanding feature, a real revision1 four-level paging request, actual parser corpora, every-point table rollback, PAT rejection/readback, full framebuffer bounds/padding, honest unknown-vector reporting, raw-before-reference initialization, narrow unsafe firmware borrows, actual PIT reload observations with a failing unmasked mutation, and full64-bit all-GPR probes. Investigating the compiler ABI removed the initial handwritten IRQ wrapper rather than rationalizing it. These findings/fixes are retained; formal architecture/memory/unsafe/security acceptance remains open, not inferred from author tests or this record.

The physical diagnostic payload is an experimental native-Limine ELF, now distributed in the separate unsigned ALPM archive above; neither artifact is a Linux-compatible system or replacement kernel package. A user-operated path is external Limine12.9.3 loading the separate diagnostic ELF from user-owned recoverable media with a32bpp framebuffer; it must not replace the current default entry/kernel. Systemd-boot cannot directly execute this ELF: native EFI/PE/Linux image work remains required. No physical install/boot/firmware/device write was performed. Before any workstation trial, independently review the exact image, confirm framebuffer/controller/firmware compatibility, user console and known-good manual recovery, and complete the candidate's QEMU suite. No serial hardware is assumed: only tty0 was observed, so framebuffer diagnostics are essential. Missing physical report/recovery authorization blocks physical qualification.

Still open: complete M1 CPU/platform/firmware/boot paths, calibrated clocks and physical results; SMP/NUMA/RT/hotplug, mature Linux-compatible allocation/accounting/reclaim and mappings; Linux process/ELF/syscall/signal/userspace semantics; every storage/filesystem/network/security/driver/architecture facility; full source-to-behavior registries, formal reviews, reproducibility/license release closure, safe packaging/recovery and matched pinned-Linux statistical performance. They remain mandatory, not waived or declared implemented by this cell. The baseline6.18.57 full-kernel port is **not complete**.

## Kernel services foundation

Branch `feat/kernel-foundation` (PR #13: services, heap and process/syscall commits, docs and review-fix commits on top of `main`). This is the base every later syscall, memory, VFS, task and signal feature builds on; it adds no syscall and changes nothing `/init` can observe. Evidence for the gate results is recorded under [Evidence](#kernel-services-foundation-evidence).

### What changed

| Area | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Boot state | `BootInfo`, `Allocator<'_>` and `Tables` were locals of `kernel_main`, whose frame is reused as init's kernel stack (`TSS.RSP0`) | `services.rs`: `BOOT` (write-once `Once<BootInfo>`, lock-free `&'static`), `FRAMES` (`Service<Allocator<'static>>`), `TABLES` (`Service<Tables>`), all behind the IRQ-saving `IrqMutex` built on `cpu::IrqSpinLock` (the only `cpu.rs` edit is its visibility, see below). Installed once by `publish_services`. |
| Table updates | "every table write precedes CR3 publication"; `user_space()` was `unsafe` and returned a copyable handle | The user half may be edited after activation (`paging.rs` module doc): `map_user`, `unmap_user`, `protect_user`, `map_user_anon`, `unmap_user_free` (later replaced by the `mm_*` operations, see [memory-mapping wiring](#memory-mapping-wiring)), with `invlpg`, atomic PTE read-modify-write and rollback. `UserSpace<'a>` now holds `&mut Tables`, so `user_space()` is safe and no edit can overlap a frame reference. |
| Frame splitting | arguments block was one order-2 reservation | `Allocator::split_reserved_order` (Linux `split_page`) makes its pages independently freeable; every user data frame is an order-0 reservation. |
| Heap | none | `kernel::heap::KernelHeap` (host-tested policy) over vendored `buddy_system_allocator` 0.13.0, growing from buddy frames; `kalloc.rs` is the `#[global_allocator]`. |
| Allocation API | n/a | kernel builds `alloc` with `--cfg=no_global_oom_handling`: `Box::new`, `Vec::push`, ... do not exist. |
| Process | `userspace::State { space, vmas }` behind a private spinlock | `userspace::Process { pid, tgid, mm, vmas, brk }` in `PROCESS: Service<Process>`; `syscall::dispatch(&mut impl Context, number, args)` |
| Syscall layout | `kernel/src/syscall.rs` | `syscall/mod.rs` (dispatch, `Outcome`, ENOSYS tables, `Context`/`Console`), `syscall/io.rs` (`write`), `syscall/process.rs` (`getpid`, `exit`, `exit_group`), `syscall/testing.rs` (host fakes) |

`cpu.rs` was touched in exactly four places and only by visibility: `IrqSpinLock` (struct, `new`, `lock`) and `IrqSpinGuard` became `pub(crate)`, plus the doc comment above them. No APIC/timer/IDT/entry code changed.

### Lock order and contexts

One DAG, documented in `services.rs`: `PROCESS` -> `TABLES` -> heap -> `FRAMES`; `FRAMES` is a leaf (it calls nothing that locks); nothing is held across a user copy that could fault (the kernel never touches user addresses), a callback or a blocking operation. All are `IrqMutex`: local interrupts are masked while held and restored after unlocking (`cpu.rs` guard order), so an interrupt handler can never deadlock against an interrupted holder on one CPU. NMI, `#DF` and `#MC` are not excluded and must never take these locks. **Allocation context: task context only.** The heap is deadlock-free if called with interrupts enabled, but there is no atomic reserve or GFP-style flag yet, so interrupt handlers MUST NOT allocate; the panic and fault paths only `log!`. The allocator never sleeps, reclaims, does I/O or uses reserves (`memory.rs`). Proof of the `Sync` arguments: `IrqMutex<T: Send>` (payload only reachable through a guard that holds the spinlock with IF masked); `Once<T: Send + Sync>` (single writer by CAS, Release store / Acquire load); `BootInfo: Send + Sync` (immutable data over reserved loader memory, no interior mutability); `Allocator: Send` (exclusive owner of its metadata, pointers are identities never dereferenced); `KernelHeap<S: Send>` is automatically `Send` because the vendored free list carries its own `unsafe impl Send` (the review found a redundant impl of ours, removed). Each has a `SAFETY` comment at the impl.

### Post-activation page-table rule

Kernel half: written only before `activate`, immutable after. User half: a not-present -> present change needs no invalidation (hardware caches no absent entry; SDM Vol. 3 4.10.4.3) and a new intermediate table is zeroed before it is linked; clearing or tightening a present PTE is an atomic RMW (hardware sets A/D bits concurrently) followed by `invlpg`, and only then may the frame be reused or freed. Empty intermediate user tables are not freed (no teardown exists; `shortcut:` in `paging.rs`). **SMP needs a TLB shootdown in place of the local `invlpg`, acknowledged before the frame is freed** (documented at the call sites; not built: one CPU). The `usercopy` TOCTOU contract is now true by construction: resolution and copy happen under one `&mut Tables` borrow, so unmap/protect cannot run between them.

### Fallible heap

Candidates read, the decision and the admitted configuration are in [dependencies](dependencies.md#kernel-heap-buddy_system_allocator): `talc` is rejected because its mandatory `allocator-api2` does not compile under `no_global_oom_handling`; `linked_list_allocator` cannot grow by discontiguous chunks; `slabmalloc` needs a pager plus a second allocator for large objects; `buddy_system_allocator` is admitted (MIT, no dependencies with default features off). Policy in `kernel/src/heap.rs`: growth by whole frame chunks (>= 64 KiB, up to the 4 MiB frame cap, Linux `KMALLOC_MAX_SIZE` analogue; `ORDERS` is 23 so the heap's largest block equals the frame cap), `None` for every failure (oversized, source refusal), no panic on any layout, power-of-two rounding (<= 50% internal fragmentation), chunks never returned (`shortcut:`). Probe evidence (rustc 1.101.0-nightly 5c543b0b8, scratch crate outside the repository, flags as the maintained build): `Vec::try_reserve`, `Vec::push_within_capacity`, `Box::try_new` compile, `Box::new` and `Vec::push` fail with `E0599`.
