* QEMU: `kernel-test` run `vfs-int-2` (30/30 cases; serial logs `target/kernel-tooling/vfs-int-2/cases/`, untracked) matched `oracle-mm` (`init: tests mm passed=121 failed=0`, every check line identical to the pinned Linux), `oracle-fs` (`passed=284 failed=0`), `oracle-ids` (157), `oracle-time` (118), `demand`, `init-segfault`, `unimplemented-syscall` and the unchanged boot-failure cases.
* Gates: `target/gates.sh` run `vfs-int-2` (logs `target/gates-vfs-int-2/`, untracked; same workstation, user and toolchain installation as earlier records, not independently attested) on the tree whose last code commit is `90cc018` (this paragraph is the only later change): `cargo fmt --all --check`, host 1.88 strict Clippy and tests, nightly strict Clippy and tests (kernel library 359 tests), `kernel-test` 30/30, target Clippy for both kernel profiles, `xtask check` and `xtask status` all `exit=0`. Run `vfs-int-1` on the first rebased tree had failed `nightly-clippy` and both target Clippy lines (an unused `self` and an unused `mut`, fixed in a focused commit) with `kernel-test` 30/30 and the rest `exit=0`.
* Exercised under QEMU by the `fs` group: private file mappings of `/init` (ELF magic through a fault, equality with `pread64`, zero-filled tail of the last page, copy-on-write write not visible to `read`), the closed/directory/console/write-only/unaligned/offset-past-end/length-zero errors and `/dev/zero` private. Not exercised: `SIGBUS` from a file fault in ring 3 or from a kernel access (`FileFault::Bus` is host-tested only), a file mapping of a file other than `/init` that the program then faults in, `MAP_POPULATE` of file pages (a loud stop, host-tested), the loud stops themselves (writable `MAP_SHARED`, writing a touched mapped file: host-tested through `dispatch`), the `user_fault` `NeedsFile` path for files other than `/init`, SMP.


### VFS and memory-mapping review round 1

Findings fixed in `feat/vfs-spine` (host tests in `syscall::vfs_tests`):

* `write(2)` loud-stop guard moved after the user copy (see above); tests: a source buffer inside the file's own untouched mapping, and `EFAULT` without a stop.
* `mmap` of a tmpfs file touches `atime` (`mm/shmem.c` `shmem_mmap` -> `file_accessed`, honouring `O_NOATIME`); test: `fstat` after `mmap`.
* `MAP_POPULATE`/`MAP_LOCKED` of a file: loud stop (above); test.
* Pins are reconciled after every `brk`/`mmap`/`munmap`; test: `MAP_FIXED` anonymous and `/dev/zero` over a file VMA release the description.
* Private `/dev/zero` and segfault line: loud stop (above). Growing `ftruncate` no longer stops; test.
* Comments: `vfs/mapping.rs` and `syscall/memory.rs` agree that a read-only `MAP_SHARED` mapping is served as private (test added), `Loaded::exec` documents that the reference stays as `exe_file`, the stale `LoadSpace` row is corrected.
* Refuted or not done: `ETXTBSY` (not implemented, listed above); the `user_fault` path stays binary-only, covered by QEMU for `/init` only.


## Interrupt controllers and timekeeping

Roadmap slices: WP-M1.2 (LAPIC/x2APIC/IOAPIC, TSC calibration, HPET/PM timer, LAPIC clockevent) and the WP-M2.6 core (monotonic/boottime/realtime clock). Both stay `in_progress`; this is a bounded BSP cell, not Linux's irq/time subsystems. It exists because the target workstation (AMD Ryzen 9 9900X, AM5, UEFI, HPET, no serial port) needs real time and cannot be assumed to route the legacy 8259/PIT; the earlier cell cleared `IA32_APIC_BASE.EN` and waited for IRQ0 through the 8259.

### What exists

| Path | Content |
|---|---|
| [`kernel/src/firmware.rs`](../kernel/src/firmware.rs) | MADT decoder (LAPIC address + type 5 override, IOAPICs, interrupt source overrides, NMI sources, LAPIC/x2APIC NMIs, processor counts incl. x2APIC entries, `PCAT_COMPAT`), HPET and FADT (PM timer port, century register, flags) decoders, ISA routing (`Madt::isa_route`), tolerant root walk (`walk_root`) |
| [`kernel/src/intc.rs`](../kernel/src/intc.rs) (library) | Linux's x2APIC decision as a pure function (`plan_apic_mode`), LVT/SPIV/IOAPIC-entry encodings, vectors |
| [`kernel/src/time.rs`](../kernel/src/time.rs) (library) | `clocks_calc_mult_shift`, `Cyc2Ns`, CPUID 0x15/0x16, PIT/HPET/PM-timer calibration math, LAPIC scaling, `mktime64` + CMOS decoding, the `Clock` core |
| [`kernel/src/apic.rs`](../kernel/src/apic.rs) | LAPIC (xAPIC MMIO / x2APIC MSR), IOAPIC and 8259 register access |
| [`kernel/src/timer.rs`](../kernel/src/timer.rs) | TSC read/calibration, HPET/PM-timer references, PIT, CMOS RTC, clockevents, tick, `ktime_get_ns`, `ktime_get_boottime_ns`, `ktime_get_real`, `wait_until_ns`/`sleep_ns`, the boot records |
| [`kernel/src/mmio.rs`](../kernel/src/mmio.rs) | the one 32-bit MMIO accessor pair |
| [`kernel/src/paging.rs`](../kernel/src/paging.rs) | **Only change**: `Tables::map_mmio` (maps one ACPI-described device page UC, RW, NX at `direct_map + physical` before CR3 publication; refuses an already-mapped page so a device page can never alias RAM, and refuses an active tree with `Error::Active`; the cache bits come from the UC entry of `IA32_PAT` that `cpu::validate` now locates and requires, not an assumed index 3) and the `PWT`/`PCD` constants; the module's update rules name `map_mmio` as the second pre-activation kernel-half writer |
| [`kernel/src/cpu.rs`](../kernel/src/cpu.rs) | PIT/PIC code removed (moved to `apic.rs`/`timer.rs`); IDT gains vectors 0x30 (ISA IRQ0), 0x37/0x3f (8259 spurious), 0xec (LAPIC timer), 0xfe (APIC error), 0xff (spurious); `timer_transaction`, `irq_lock_probe`, `in_dword`, `interrupts_on`, `wait_for_interrupt` (now the vendored `x86_64` crate's `Port`/`interrupts` with compiler fences, see the register below); the timer, spurious, error and 8259 vectors are served before the ring-3 exception branch (`external_interrupt`), as Linux serves IRQs at any CPL; the IRQ-guard proof observes tick periods on the TSC |
| [`kernel/src/main.rs`](../kernel/src/main.rs) | `prepare` + `map_mmio` before `activate`; `clock_and_irq_checks` replaces the PIT block; MADT/time-table records; modules `apic`, `mmio`, `timer` |
| [`kernel/src/boot.rs`](../kernel/src/boot.rs) | `BootInfo::has_option` (whole words before `--`: `nolapic`, `noapic`, `nox2apic`), `test=firmware-x2apic`, `test=force-x2apic` (self-test builds only) |

### Boot sequence

1. `firmware::discover` walks the XSDT. Null, unreadable or short entries and checksum failures become `WARN acpi table <sig> <problem>` and the walk continues (ACPICA with `ACPI_CHECKSUM_ABORT=FALSE`); only RSDP/XSDT errors are fatal. A malformed MADT/HPET/FADT is reported and ignored (no MADT: legacy PIC path), as Linux falls back when its MADT parse fails. Records: `CHECK handoff ... warnings=`, `CHECK madt ok ...`, `CHECK time-tables hpet= pm_timer= century=`.
2. `apic::prepare` reads CPUID and `IA32_APIC_BASE` (no MMIO) and applies `intc::plan_apic_mode`. `timer::map_mmio` maps the xAPIC page (address from `IA32_APIC_BASE`; the MADT's is only reported), every MADT IOAPIC and the HPET block before `Tables::activate`. A page that cannot be mapped, or whose address exceeds the CPU's `MAXPHYADDR` (CPUID 0x80000008; the PTE would have reserved bits and fault on first use), is counted and that device is absent; never fatal.
3. After CR3 (`timer::init`): 8259 initialised at vector base 0x30 and fully masked (`PCAT_COMPAT` or `probe_8259A`); every IOAPIC pin masked; LAPIC switched to the planned mode and set up like `setup_local_APIC` (all LVTs masked, TPR 0x10, ISR drained, SPIV = enable|0xff, LINT0 `ExtINT` only if firmware left it unmasked or no IOAPIC, LINT1 NMI, error vector); HPET started (legacy replacement cleared); TSC calibrated; RTC read; clock published; PIT IRQ0 routed through the MADT's pin and counted for 20 ms (`ioapic_timer`); clockevent chosen and started; three ticks awaited. Records: `CHECK apic ok`, `CHECK tsc ok`, `CHECK lapic-timer ok` (one-shot only), `CHECK clockevent ok`, `CHECK interrupt-controllers ok mode= event= ioapic_timer=`.
4. In the QEMU suite any failure here is `FAIL timer-init`. In the physical diagnostic profile a *bring-up* failure (no calibration source, a stuck reference clock, no tick) is `WARN timer-init`, the tick is masked, the interrupt-dependent checks are skipped (`WARN timer tick not delivered`) and the boot continues. Once bring-up succeeded, a later `require`/`unwrap` failure (for example `timer-period-vs-tsc` outside 14..60 ms, `timekeeping`) still stops the physical diagnostic like every other check there; "report and continue" covers only bring-up. No exception fixup exists yet (WP-M1.2 extable), so a `#GP` from an `IA32_APIC_BASE` write on unusual firmware is a fatal trap, not a `WARN`.
5. Proofs, unchanged in intent: IRQ-guard, all-GPR preservation across a real IRQ, 16 delivered ticks (`CHECK timer ok ... tick16_ns=` is the TSC time of 16 ticks, 14..60 ms enforced), mask then two periods with `irq_delta=0` (`TIMER mask observation periods=2`), then `CHECK timekeeping ok` (20 ms `sleep_ns`, monotonic never backwards over 1000 reads, realtime == RTC second + monotonic from one TSC sample, boottime == monotonic). The tick is masked before ring 3, and every vector this kernel owns (timer, ISA IRQ0, spurious, error, 8259 spurious) is served at any CPL, because `/init` runs with IF=1 and a tick latched across the shutdown must not be taken for a task exception.

### Linux 6.18.57 behaviour and where this differs

| Topic | Linux (file) | Here | Why / consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| x2APIC choice | `check_x2apic`, `try_to_enable_x2apic` (`arch/x86/kernel/apic/apic.c:1696-1875`): firmware-enabled x2APIC is noted; with no interrupt remapping and no hypervisor x2APIC the kernel switches back to xAPIC unless the APIC ID >= 255 (panic) or the mode is hardware locked; a recognised hypervisor enables it | Same decision, as a pure function with host tests. Difference: ID >= 255 stays in x2APIC instead of panicking. Hypervisor test is KVM only (`CPUID 0x40000000` signature) | No IOMMU/IR exists. Inference, not a measurement of this kernel: the Linux log of the host OS on the Zen 5 board shows no "x2apic: enabled by BIOS" message and `Fast TSC calibration using PIT`, and Limine's protocol document says base revision 5 disables firmware x2APIC before handoff, so the xAPIC path is expected there; no physical run of this kernel exists. x2APIC is exercised by KVM and the two `test=` cases only |
| LAPIC base address | trusts the MADT address and its type-5 override (`register_lapic_address`) | uses the address in `IA32_APIC_BASE` (the hardware truth); the MADT's is printed as `madt_lapic=` and a mismatch is visible, not acted on | the safer choice for a UC mapping we create ourselves |
| `nolapic` | leaves the LAPIC as firmware set it, ExtINT through LINT0 | clears `EN` (after leaving x2APIC) so the 8259 reaches the CPU directly, exactly what the earlier cell did | explicit option only; never the default |
| APIC tick | `lapic-deadline` if `X86_FEATURE_TSC_DEADLINE_TIMER`, else calibrated one-shot (`apic.c:413-505, 791-945`); tick emulated on one-shot devices | same selection; the periodic tick is emulated by re-arming for `next_tick += 1 ms`; no idle tick stop yet | HZ=1000 reference config |
| LAPIC timer calibration | counts 100 PIT/HPET broadcast ticks | 50 ms busy-wait on the already calibrated TSC, divider 16, same sanity floor (`period >= 1000000/HZ`) | no broadcast device or timer wheel yet; accuracy follows the TSC's |
| TSC order | `native_calibrate_tsc` (Intel CPUID 0x15), `cpu_khz_from_cpuid`, `cpu_khz_from_msr` (Intel MSR), `quick_pit_calibrate`, `pit_hpet_ptimer_calibrate_cpu`, refinement after 1 s (`tsc.c:663-935, 1316-1390`) | CPUID 0x15, 0x16, quick PIT, then HPET/PM-timer alone; PIT/CPUID-0x16 values refined synchronously over 50 ms against HPET/PM-timer and accepted within 1%; a CPUID-0x15 value with a reported crystal is final | the MSR path (Intel `MSR_PLATFORM_INFO`) is not ported; the quick PIT is tried up to three times (`pit_tries=` in `CHECK tsc`) and the PIT loop variant (`pit_calibrate_tsc`) is replaced by the reference-only path, which also covers `q35,pit=off` and the KVM case (observed `source=hpet` in run `apic-g2`, `source=pit` in `apic-g3`). Consequence: with a PIT present, a failing quick PIT and no HPET or PM timer, calibration fails (`Error::Calibration`) where Linux would still run `pit_calibrate_tsc`. Every reference wait is bounded in elapsed TSC time (`time::REFERENCE_LIMIT_CYCLES`, host test with a counter that never advances); a stuck reference prints `WARN tsc reference=<name> calibration failed: Stuck` and the next source or `Error::Calibration` follows. A published TSC below 100 MHz is rejected (our own floor; Linux has none). No hypervisor `calibrate_tsc` hook (kvmclock/VMware leaves) |
| Clock reliability | clocksource watchdog, `tsc_unstable`, TSC_ADJUST | none; `invariant=` (CPUID 0x80000007 EDX[8]) is only reported | single CPU, no second clocksource to compare; no suspend |
| PIT/8259 presence | `probe_8259A`, `has_legacy_pic()` gate `quick_pit_calibrate`; reduced-hardware ACPI has a null PIC | same (`PCAT_COMPAT` skips the probe; `HW_REDUCED_ACPI` means none); a missing PIC only disables PIT calibration and the IRQ0 check | `pcat_compat=0` no longer fails boot |
| `check_timer` | tries pin1/pin2/virtual wire/ExtINT, panics if none works | IRQ0 through the MADT pin only; the result is a record (`ioapic_timer`), never fatal, because the LAPIC timer does not depend on it | documented gap: no virtual-wire/ExtINT fallbacks |
| NMI | LINT1 NMI on the BSP | same; a MADT `lint != 1` NMI entry is parsed but not acted on (Linux only warns) | an NMI is still `FAIL trap vector=2` here: no NMI reason decoding or counting yet (the NMI path takes no timer, APIC or clock lock, so the lock-order argument holds) |
| Wall clock | `mach_get_cmos_time`: UIP-avoiding read, always BCD on x86, no 12-hour handling, `mktime64` | same, bit for bit including garbage-in behaviour; failing to read for 1 s gives 0 | RTC assumed UTC; FADT `no_cmos_rtc` is decoded but, as in Linux, does not gate the read |
| IOAPIC / MADT | `MAX_IO_APICS` 128, `mp_register_ioapic` skips zero/duplicate addresses; the entry loop stops at `entry + 2 >= end` | 32 IOAPICs, 32 overrides; excess entries are counted in `Madt::dropped`; pin count clamped to 120 (the most an 8-bit IOREGSEL addresses: a larger version-register value would alias the ID/version registers); a single trailing byte after the last entry is an error (stricter than Linux, in the safe direction) | `shortcut:` comment in `firmware.rs`; raise on a larger machine |
| Spurious vector | `handle_spurious_interrupt` (`apic.c:2124`): not acknowledged unless the vector's ISR bit is set | same (one ISR read) | a real interrupt that landed on 0xff must not block lower priorities |
| No interrupt remapping, MSI, affinity, IPIs, SMP | | not present | later slices (WP-M2.4, WP-M2.7) |

Differences from the earlier cell: vectors moved from 0x20 to Linux's `ISA_IRQ_VECTOR` 0x30; the tick rate is HZ=1000 (PIT latch 1193) instead of ~100 Hz; the observation unit is "tick period measured on the TSC" instead of "PIT counter wraps" (`pit_wraps=`/`blocked_wraps=` records are now `periods=`/`blocked_periods=`), because a one-shot or TSC-deadline device has no free-running counter. The legacy PIT path keeps the same property on `nolapic`: the TSC clock is calibrated from the PIT and the PIT then ticks against it, so a wrong PIT rate would fail `tick16_ns`.

### Unsafe / assembly additions (for the register above)

| Site | Why asm | Contract |
|---|---|---|
| `timer::rdtsc` (`lfence; rdtsc`) | **no hand asm**: `core::arch::x86_64::{_mm_lfence, _rdtsc}`; `_mm_lfence` is an out-of-line call (its `sse2` target feature cannot be enabled on this soft-float target's own functions), executing only `lfence` | CPUID.1:EDX.TSC checked in `init`; LFENCE raises only #UD without SSE2 (every x86-64 CPU has it) and is not gated by CR0.EM/TS or CR4.OSFXSR; the intrinsics have side effects, so the fence stays before the read as in Linux's `rdtsc_ordered()`; any context |
| `cpu::{out_byte,in_byte,in_dword,exit}` | **no hand asm**: vendored `x86_64` `instructions::port::Port` | the crate's asm is `nomem`; each access is bracketed by `compiler_fence(SeqCst)` so device sequences stay ordered against memory work as the earlier hand asm (not `nomem`) was; CPL0, ports are the owned PC devices / the FADT-named PM-timer block |
| `cpu::interrupts_on`, `cpu::wait_for_interrupt` | **no hand asm**: `interrupts::{enable, enable_and_hlt, disable}` | same fences; the crate emits `sti; hlt` adjacent, race-free through STI's shadow; BSP, IDT and a *verified* interrupt source installed (`timer::idle` spins instead of halting until ticks were seen) |
| `Msr` reads/writes (`apic.rs`, `timer.rs`) | the `x86_64` crate's wrappers | `apic::{rdmsr,wrmsr}` are now `unsafe fn`s whose call sites state the CPUID guard (`IA32_APIC_BASE`: APIC; ARCH_CAPABILITIES: CPUID.7); x2APIC registers are reachable only through `intc::LapicReg` (private field, so safe code cannot name an out-of-page offset or an arbitrary MSR; host test over every constant); `TSC_DEADLINE`: CPUID.1:ECX[24] |
| `mmio::{read32,write32}` | volatile access | only addresses returned by `Tables::map_mmio` (UC, supervisor-only, mapped for the life of the kernel) |

This slice adds no hand asm (the review showed `_mm_lfence` compiles on this target as an out-of-line `lfence; ret`, replacing the earlier `rdtsc` asm). No disassembly review of these sites is claimed beyond the compiler. The IDT entries reuse the audited `entry!` macro; no new naked function.

### Five design questions

1. **Preexisting crate?** `raw-cpuid` and `x86_64` (already admitted) are used for CPUID, MSRs and descriptor types. The `acpi` crate was reconsidered: 6.1.1 (MIT/Apache) has a MADT entry iterator and `validate()`, but its defaults pull `alloc` + the AML interpreter, adding `byteorder`, `log` and `pci_types` to the vendor set, and its API aborts on a bad checksum, the behaviour this slice must not have. It also has no ISA routing, fixed-capacity or tolerance policy. Its decoders were not audited line by line, only its manifest and surface; the earlier deferral is kept for the AML slice. `pic8259`/`uart_16550`/`x2apic` stay rejected for the reasons in [dependencies](dependencies.md#admission-records). No new crate was added.
2. **Bundle?** The MADT/HPET/FADT decoders share one checked SDT walk and one tolerance policy; LAPIC/IOAPIC/8259 share one register layer; time sources share one calibrator. The tick, deadline wait and `mask_timer` are one clockevent state. Pure arithmetic is split into the library so it is host-tested.
3. **Simplify?** One CPU, one clockevent, one waiter; calibration of the LAPIC timer against the TSC instead of a broadcast device; synchronous 50 ms refinement instead of a delayed work item; no clocksource watchdog; unmodified ISA routing defaults instead of the MP-table machinery.
4. **Secure?** All table input is bounds-checked and fixed-capacity (hostile-table tests: every known MADT type shortened, overruns, zero/odd lengths, duplicate/zero IOAPICs, table overflow, bogus HPET GAS, short FADT, 40 unreadable XSDT entries). Device pages are mapped only if absent from the direct map (no aliasing of RAM), UC, NX, supervisor-only. MMIO and port addresses come from checked ACPI/CPUID, never from user input; no allocation in IRQ context; the timer lock is never taken by a handler except the non-blocking probe; the spurious vector is acknowledged only when its ISR bit shows it was real; stray 8259 spurious IRQs are told from real ones by the in-service register. Device addresses are checked against `MAXPHYADDR`, the IOAPIC pin count is clamped to the addressable window, and `map_mmio` refuses an active tree. TSC/RTC are untrusted hardware values: a calibration of 0 or below 100 MHz or above `u32::MAX` kHz is rejected (`Error::Calibration`); every wait on an uncached reference is bounded in elapsed TSC time and a stuck reference is reported; the other polling loops (quick PIT, HPET start, ISR drain, RTC, LAPIC calibration, IOAPIC check, tick wait) are bounded by iteration or TSC time. Halting waits (`sleep_ns`/`wait_until_ns`) are used only after the tick was seen delivering; a clockevent that dies afterwards would hang a halted CPU (like Linux's idle loop), which the boot proofs avoid by using the spin forms.
5. **Improve?** Next: SMP bring-up (per-CPU timers, IPIs), hrtimer/timer-wheel and NOHZ, TSC_ADJUST and clocksource watchdog, HPET/PM-timer as fallback tick, MSI/IR, the MSR-based Intel calibration, ExtINT fallbacks for `check_timer`, NMI reason decoding, per-boot `vdso` clock data.

### Evidence and limits

Run `apic-g4` on this branch rebased onto main fdbac8b (after PR #17's independent review fixes; host Ryzen 9 9900X workstation, QEMU 11.1.2, TCG except the `kvm` case) with the current `gates.sh`, every line `exit=0`: `fmt`, `host-clippy`, `host-test` (xtask 74, baremetal 10), `nightly-clippy`, `nightly-test` (kernel library 162 tests, including the `firmware` MADT/HPET/FADT/hostile-walk, `intc` (typed registers, pin clamp, address width), `time` (bounded reference wait) and `boot` option tests), `kernel-test` **25/25** (no `-retry` case was needed; the `unavailable` path for a KVM case without `/dev/kvm` is implemented but was not exercised, since this host has KVM), `target-clippy-qemu-self-test`, `target-clippy-freestanding`, `check`, `status`. Per-case serial logs: `target/kernel-tooling/apic-g4/cases/<case>/serial.log` (untracked). The numbers below are from the earlier code-identical-in-behaviour runs `apic-g2`/`apic-g3` unless stated; `apic-g4` showed `source=pit pit_tries=1` for the base, `hpet-off`, `nolapic`, `x2apic-firmware` and `kvm` cases, `source=hpet pit_tries=3` for `pit-off`, and `reason=` `default`/`nolapic`/`hypervisor`/`no-interrupt-remapping` as pinned. Observed on the base case: `CHECK madt ok lapic=0xfee00000 cpus=1 ioapics=1 overrides=5 lapic_nmis=1 pcat_compat=true irq0_gsi=2` (the typical ISA IRQ0 -> GSI 2 override), `CHECK tsc ok khz=5399791 source=pit refined=true ... hpet_hz=100000000 pm_timer=0x608 rtc=true`, `CHECK lapic-timer ok bus_khz=1000019`, `event=lapic-oneshot`, `tick16_ns=15958518` (16 ticks in 15.96 ms), `slept_ns=20320306 realtime_skew_ns=0`. Observed per variant: `hpet-off` PIT calibration refined against the PM timer; `pit-off` `source=hpet` and `ioapic_timer=false` with a working LAPIC tick; `nolapic` `mode=legacy-pic event=pit-periodic` with `tick16_ns=15964148` (PIT rate agrees with the TSC); `noapic` no IOAPIC programmed; `x2apic-forced` x2APIC MSR backend; `x2apic-firmware` x2APIC enabled by the kernel then switched back to xAPIC by the Linux rule; `kvm` `mode=x2apic reason=hypervisor event=lapic-deadline`, `tick16_ns=15740521`. An earlier run (`apic-g1`) failed `empty-module` because the quick-PIT calibration fell back to the HPET under heavy concurrent host load. The quick PIT is now attempted up to three times (`pit_tries=`), the base case accepts `pit` or `hpet`, and `hpet-off` and `nolapic` **require** `source=pit` with one recorded harness retry on a source-only mismatch. The default (no serial) framebuffer capture shows every record and no `FAIL`.

Limits, stated plainly:

- **No physical evidence.** Nothing here ran on the Ryzen 9 9900X/AM5 board. Unverified there: IOAPIC pin routing of IRQ0 (Linux log shows pin 2), the quick-PIT accuracy over eSPI, HPET behaviour after reset, TSC-deadline use on Zen 5, xAPIC page access, the NMI line and any SMI effects on calibration. A failing tick is reported as `WARN timer-init` and the boot continues; a diagnostic run's framebuffer therefore shows which step failed.
- QEMU TCG here has no TSC-deadline timer and no invariant-TSC flag; the deadline path is exercised only by the KVM case (KVM host CPU), x2APIC only forced (TCG) or via KVM; CPUID 0x15/0x16 are exercised by arithmetic unit tests only (no Intel CPU model with the leaves was run). The TSC frequency reported inside QEMU is the host's, so the calibration accuracy claim is "agrees with HPET within 1%", not an absolute one.
- No SMP, no interrupt remapping, no hrtimer/timer wheel/NOHZ, no clocksource watchdog, no `adjtimex`/`CLOCK_*` ABI (no syscalls were added). `wait_until_ns` supports one waiter at a time and the legacy PIT path sleeps at tick granularity (up to 1 ms late).
- Device pages are mapped into the still-unpublished tree after `userspace::load` and before `Tables::activate`, so the foundation's rule that the kernel half is immutable after activation is unchanged; no post-activation MMIO mapping exists (a later hotplug/ioremap needs the services' post-activation paging ops and an explicit rule extension).


## Identity and process-attribute syscalls

The `ids` oracle group (157 checks, `-- ids`) now passes on this kernel with the **158 output lines (157 `init: test ids.<name> ok` plus `init: tests ids passed=157 failed=0`) identical to the pinned Linux 6.18.57's** (`xtask/oracle/expected.tsv`), in the generic `oracle-ids` [oracle-group case](kernel-tooling.md#oracle-group-cases) of `kernel-test`. Declared configuration as in [`config.rs`](../kernel/src/config.rs); a single task, one CPU, one user namespace, no LSM.

### What was added

| Piece | Where | Linux source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Per-task state: `Cred` (root, no supplementary groups), `Rlimits` (`INIT_RLIMITS`, `RLIMIT_NPROC`/`SIGPENDING` = `max_threads/2` from the managed pages (approximation, see shortcuts), `do_prlimit` validation), `FsContext` (umask 0022, cwd text `/`, the seam for the VFS lane), `comm`, `personality`, `clear_child_tid`, `robust_list`, ppid/pgid/sid = 0 | `kernel/src/task/attrs.rs` (`Attributes`, host-tested), held in `Process.attrs` | `init/init_task.c`, `include/asm-generic/resource.h`, `kernel/fork.c` `set_max_threads`/`fork_init`, `fs/fs_struct.c`, `kernel/groups.c` |
| 30 syscalls | `kernel/src/syscall/ids.rs`, one `.or_else` line in `syscall::dispatch` | `kernel/sys.c`, `kernel/groups.c`, `kernel/exec_domain.c`, `kernel/fork.c`, `kernel/futex/syscalls.c`, `kernel/sched/syscalls.c`, `arch/x86/kernel/process{,_64}.c`, `fs/d_path.c` |
| Load averages: `calc_load`, `LOAD_FREQ` = 5 s + 1 tick, `EXP_1/5/15`, the CPU's `calc_load_tasks` fold and the global update 10 ticks after the window, replayed by `LoadAvg::loads_at` from the boot clock (`jiffies = ktime_get_boottime_ns() / TICK_NSEC`) on every `sysinfo`, no interrupt hook (the tick is masked in user mode) | `kernel/src/loadavg.rs` (pure, `forbid(unsafe_code)`, 4 host tests including replay-equals-per-jiffy), `Process::load` in `kernel/src/userspace.rs` | `include/linux/sched/loadavg.h`, `kernel/sched/loadavg.c`, `kernel/time/tick-common.c`, `kernel/sys.c` `do_sysinfo` |
| `put_user` (one store, all or nothing) and `strncpy_from_user` | `kernel/src/usercopy.rs` (the one user-copy mechanism) | `arch/x86/include/asm/uaccess.h`, `lib/strncpy_from_user.c` |
| FS/GS base access: `loadseg(FS, 0)` + MSR write through the vendored `x86_64` crate (`FS::set_reg`, `FsBase`, `GsBase`); reads are the MSRs (Linux's `x86_fsbase_read_cpu`) | `kernel/src/cpu.rs` (4 functions), reached through `Context::{fs,gs}_base`/`set_*` | `arch/x86/kernel/process_64.c` |
| `Context` gains `tid`, `attrs`, the four base accessors and `machine()` (uptime, total/free pages for `sysinfo`) | `kernel/src/syscall/mod.rs`, `testing.rs`, `userspace.rs` | |
| The harness case kind | `xtask/src/kernel.rs` (`oracle_case!`, `User::Oracle`, `validate_oracle`) | see [kernel tooling](kernel-tooling.md#oracle-group-cases) |

Per-syscall summary (all numbers are x86-64): `getuid`/`geteuid`/`getgid`/`getegid` (102/107/104/108), `getresuid`/`getresgid` (118/120; three `put_user`s in order), `getgroups` (115; `int` size, `-EINVAL` below the count, stores per group), `getppid` (110), `gettid` (186), `getpgrp` (111), `getpgid` (121) and `getsid` (124) (lookup succeeds only for the caller, otherwise `-ESRCH`), `getpriority` (140; `PRIO_PROCESS` exact, `PRIO_PGRP`/`PRIO_USER` stop the kernel when they would succeed, `-ESRCH`/`-EINVAL` exact), `set_tid_address` (218), `set_robust_list` (273; only `len == 24`), `personality` (135; stored, and it really changes `uname`: `UNAME26` rewrites the release to `2.6.78`, `PER_LINUX32` the machine to `i686`), `sched_getscheduler` (145), `sched_get_priority_max/min` (146/147), `sched_getaffinity`/`sched_setaffinity` (204/203; CPU 0 only, including Linux's `len * 8` wrap in `unsigned int` and the order of `-EINVAL`/`-EFAULT`/`-ESRCH`), `getrlimit`/`setrlimit`/`prlimit64` (97/160/302; new limit validated, old value copied out after the store), `umask` (95), `getcwd` (79), `uname` (63; `copy_to_user` of 390 bytes, then the two overrides), `sysinfo` (99; 112 bytes, byte-scaled counters (pages with `mem_unit` 4096 if the scaling would overflow, as `do_sysinfo`), boot-clock uptime rounded up, load averages replayed from the boot clock), `arch_prctl` (158) and `prctl` (157).

**`uname` values.** `sysname` `Linux`, `nodename` `(none)` (`CONFIG_DEFAULT_HOSTNAME`), `release` `6.18.57` (the oracle's own build also printed exactly this; `UTS_RELEASE` of the pinned tag without `LOCALVERSION`), `machine` `x86_64`, `domainname` `(none)`. `version` is `#1 linux-rs`: Linux prints `#<build number> <flags> <date>`, this kernel has no build counter, so it names itself; the oracle only requires a non-empty string.

**`prctl`.** Implemented: `PR_GET_DUMPABLE` (1, `SUID_DUMP_USER`), `PR_GET_TIMERSLACK` (50000 ns, `init_task`), `PR_GET_NO_NEW_PRIVS` (0, with Linux's `-EINVAL` for any nonzero `arg2..arg5`), `PR_SET_NAME`/`PR_GET_NAME` (`strncpy_from_user` of 15 bytes, `strscpy_pad`, 16-byte copy-out; init starts as `init`), `PR_GET_TIMING` (0) and `PR_SET_TIMING` (0 for `PR_TIMING_STATISTICAL`, `-EINVAL` otherwise). Plain `-EINVAL`, exactly what `kernel/sys.c` and the pinned configuration give: the architecture hooks x86-64 does not define (`UNALIGN`, `FPEMU`, `FPEXC`, `ENDIAN`, `FP_MODE`, `SVE`, `SME`, `PAC`, `TAGGED_ADDR`, `RISCV`, `PPC` `DEXCR`, `sys.c:84-159`), the removed `MPX` pair, the shadow-stack options (`__weak` `-EINVAL`, `# CONFIG_X86_USER_SHADOW_STACK`), and the options compiled out by `# CONFIG_SCHED_CORE`, `# CONFIG_KSM` and `# CONFIG_SECURITY_YAMA` (`PR_SCHED_CORE`, `PR_SET/GET_MEMORY_MERGE`, `PR_SET_PTRACER`, which reach the switch's `default`). A declared configuration that enabled Yama, KSM or core scheduling would make those stateful. Every other number defined in `include/uapi/linux/prctl.h` (the rest of 1..78, `PR_SET_VMA`, `PR_GET_AUXV`; they have real behaviour or state) returns `Outcome::Unimplemented` and stops the kernel loudly; numbers Linux does not define (0, 17, 18, 48, 49, 79.., negative `int`s) get its `default: -EINVAL`.

**`arch_prctl`.** `ARCH_SET_FS/GS` (`-EPERM` at or above `TASK_SIZE_MAX`), `ARCH_GET_FS/GS`, `ARCH_GET_CPUID` (1). `ARCH_SET_CPUID` is `-ENODEV` (`set_cpuid_mode`) when the CPU is proven to lack CPUID faulting (AMD-family CPUs without `GP_ON_USER_CPUID`, CPUID `0x80000021` EAX bit 17, which includes the `qemu64` oracle CPU); on Intel (whose detection reads `MSR_PLATFORM_INFO` with `rdmsrq_safe`, for which this kernel has no fault fixup) and on CPUs that have faulting it, like the `XCOMP` family (xstate permission model), it stops the kernel. `ARCH_MAP_VDSO_*`, the `ADDRESS_MASKING` options and the `SHSTK` options are `-EINVAL` because the pinned reference configuration has `# CONFIG_CHECKPOINT_RESTORE is not set`, no `CONFIG_ADDRESS_MASKING`, and `# CONFIG_X86_USER_SHADOW_STACK is not set` (`shstk_prctl` is the `-EINVAL` stub). A finite `RLIMIT_CPU` also stops the kernel (it arms the POSIX CPU timer in Linux, which does not exist here) after the normal validation, storing nothing.

### Shortcuts (each is also a `shortcut:` or contract comment at its site)

* Credentials are fixed root and nothing can change them (`setuid` & co. are not implemented and would stop the kernel); `capable(CAP_SYS_RESOURCE)` is therefore always true and there is no LSM hook. Upgrade with the credentials/capabilities slice (`cred` becomes shared and copy-on-write).
* Rlimits are stored faithfully, but enforcement belongs to the subsystems that consume them (fd table, `mmap`, `fork`, signals); only the storage and the `getrlimit` family exist.
* `sysinfo` uptime is `timer::ktime_get_boottime_ns()` (the timekeeping lane's boot clock), rounded up to whole seconds as `do_sysinfo` does. `sysinfo` memory is `Allocator::total()`/`available()` (managed and free 4 KiB pages), `procs` is the one task (Linux's count includes kernel threads). The load averages are Linux's `calc_global_load` machinery with `nr_running + nr_uninterruptible` = 1 (init always counts; the interruptible sleeps are not subtracted yet, although Linux does not count a `TASK_INTERRUPTIBLE` task), evaluated lazily: the periodic tick is masked before init starts and nothing restarts it, so `sysinfo` replays every sampling window that elapsed on the boot clock since init started (`LoadAvg::loads_at`, a pure function of the jiffy count, equal to ticking every jiffy by the `a_late_replay_equals_ticking_every_jiffy` host test). They are 0 until the first window plus the 10-tick global delay (about 5 s after init starts) and converge toward 1 afterwards, as on Linux with one busy task. Not exercised by a QEMU case (the `ids` oracle run ends well inside 5 s); host-tested only. shortcut: the window starts when init starts (`sched_init` in Linux), the active count is a constant until a sleep subtracts itself or a second task exists (then the sampling must move to the scheduler tick, or sample at each state change), and the replay costs one step per elapsed 5 s window.
* `cwd` is the text `/`; `FsContext` is the seam for the real dentry/mount pair, and `getcwd` formats that text instead of walking `d_path`.
* `getpriority`/`sched_getscheduler` return the constants for a task with nice 0 and `SCHED_NORMAL`; the setters are not implemented and stop the kernel, so the constants cannot go stale. `getpriority` 20 is exact for `PRIO_PROCESS`. For `PRIO_PGRP` and `PRIO_USER` Linux returns the maximum of `20 - nice` over every task of the group or uid, and a booted Linux has kernel threads of uid 0 with nice -20 (`kworker/*H`), so a stock 6.18 reports 40 there; this kernel has no kernel threads, so a successful lookup stops the kernel loudly (`Outcome::Unimplemented`) instead of printing a different value, until scheduler state and kernel threads exist. `-ESRCH` and `-EINVAL` stay exact. The oracle covers only `PRIO_PROCESS`.
* `RLIMIT_NPROC`/`RLIMIT_SIGPENDING` use Linux's `set_max_threads` formula (`kernel/fork.c`: `THREAD_SIZE` 16 KiB, clamp to `MIN_THREADS` and `MAX_THREADS`; `max_threads/2`) but over every page the frame allocator manages, whereas Linux uses `memblock_estimated_nr_free_pages()` at `fork_init` (memblock memory minus early reservations). The values equal Linux's only for the same page count; a comparison across RAM sizes needs the memblock-equivalent estimate.
* `set_tid_address` and `set_robust_list` store the pointers; nothing consumes them until exit/futex exist (`mm_release` clears `clear_child_tid`, `exit_robust_list` walks the list).
* FS/GS selectors are loaded with the null selector before the base write as Linux does, but a user `mov %fs` of a GDT selector between calls is read back from the MSR, not tracked.

### The five design questions

* *Can I use a preexisting crate?* The syscall numbers and `Errno` come from the vendored `syscalls` crate (already used); the segment/MSR access from the vendored `x86_64` crate (`FsBase`, `GsBase`, `FS::set_reg`), so no new `asm!`. No crate offers Linux's `prlimit`, `prctl`, `uname` or `sysinfo` semantics, and a `bitflags`/`zerocopy` dependency would buy nothing for the handful of fixed-layout byte arrays built here (`to_le_bytes` into fixed buffers; layouts are asserted by tests). No crate computes Linux's `calc_load` fixed-point averages either (a dozen lines of integer arithmetic, host-tested against hand-derived steps). No new dependency, no `docs/dependencies.md` change.
* *Can I bundle functionality together?* One state struct (`task::Attributes`) and one group file instead of per-syscall modules: the calls share the lookup rule (`find_task`), the user-copy helpers and the limits/credentials state. The user-copy additions extend the single `usercopy` mechanism rather than adding a second one; the load averages are one small pure module (`loadavg`) replayed from the existing boot clock, not a second timer.
* *Can I simplify things?* Only the caller exists, so `find_task_by_vpid` is `pid == tid`, `getpgid`/`getsid` are field reads, and the affinity mask is bit 0. Constants stand where the setters do not exist (nice, scheduler policy, dumpable, timer slack). No heap use, no allocation: all buffers are fixed-size stack arrays.
* *Is this secure?* Every user address goes through `usercopy` (range check, page walk, no direct dereference). `put_user` is all-or-nothing as an x86 store is; `copy_to_user` keeps Linux's short-copy behaviour. `arch_prctl` refuses bases at or above `TASK_SIZE_MAX` (`-EPERM`), which is also what keeps the value canonical for the MSR write (`VirtAddr::new_truncate` cannot misbehave). Arguments are truncated to their C types exactly where Linux does (`int`, `unsigned int`, `pid_t`), including the `len * 8` wrap in `sched_getaffinity`. A user can no longer reach `Unimplemented` via an unknown option number (only through numbers Linux defines); that remains the documented single-init shortcut in `syscall::dispatch`. No FSGSBASE instructions (CR4.FSGSBASE stays clear). `strncpy_from_user` returns `-EFAULT` when the address space ends before a NUL while the caller would have accepted more bytes (`lib/strncpy_from_user.c:63-72`), tested at the `TASK_SIZE_MAX` boundary. The load-average state lives in `Process` (under the `PROCESS` service) and is replayed on a copy, so it adds no lock, no interrupt-context access and no edge to the lock-order DAG. `getpriority(PRIO_PGRP/PRIO_USER)` stops the kernel rather than return 20 where a stock Linux returns 40. `prlimit` indexes `rlimit[]` with a bounds check but no `array_index_nospec` (Linux has one in `do_prlimit`; the tree has no nospec primitive yet, noted as a `shortcut:` in `Rlimits::prlimit`). `prctl` answers Linux's `-EINVAL` for the options without x86-64 state instead of killing the kernel, so a probing program cannot reach `Unimplemented` through them. Not covered: any adversarial multi-task case, since there is one task.
* *How can I improve this?* Replace the cwd text with a dentry, the constants with real scheduler state, and add the setters (`setpriority`, `setuid` family, `sched_setscheduler`, `PR_SET_*`) together with the credential/capability model; feed `nr_running`/`nr_uninterruptible` from the runqueue into `loadavg` (and move the sampling to the scheduler tick) once a syscall can sleep, give `RLIMIT_NPROC` a memblock-equivalent page estimate, detect CPUID faulting on Intel with a fault-safe MSR read, and move `Attributes` into the `task_struct`/`cred`/`signal_struct` analogues when more than one task exists.

### Definition of done and status

| Item | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Every `ids` check passes with Linux's exact output | `oracle-ids`: 157 `init: test ids.*` lines plus the summary equal `expected.tsv` one for one | done (QEMU TCG `qemu64` only) |
| Errno and check order per syscall (argument truncation, `EFAULT`/`ESRCH`/`EINVAL` precedence, store-before-copyout in `prlimit64`, partial stores) | host tests in `syscall/ids.rs`, `task.rs`, `usercopy.rs` and `loadavg.rs` | done for the exercised and the neighbouring cases; no differential fuzz |
| `prctl`/`arch_prctl` options with real Linux state that this slice lacks stop loudly; options that are plain `-EINVAL` on x86-64 and the pinned configuration, and undefined numbers, return it | `prctl_options_names_and_unknown_numbers`, `arch_prctl_matches_process_64`, `a_finite_cpu_limit_stops_instead_of_storing` | done |
| Harness: one generic exact-line validator, one line per group | `oracle_case!`, `validate_oracle`; unit tests `oracle_case_requires_every_expected_line_exactly_in_order`, `oracle_info_rows_match_by_name_only` | done |
| Behaviour on real hardware, KVM, other QEMU CPU models, `sysinfo` uptime against a real clock | not run | not done |
| Independent review | not done | not done |

## Kernel-run bare-metal suite (A21)

Authority: [A21](decisions.md) (the user's 2026-10-20 request for "a bare metal test suite I can just execute while using the new kernel which does all sorts of things (preferably non-destructively) and logs the results"). User-facing procedure, meaning of each check and limits: [bare-metal-suite](bare-metal-suite.md#kernel-run-suite-a21); builder and harness: [kernel-tooling](kernel-tooling.md#kernel-run-bare-metal-suite-a21). **Prepared for the user's trial; nothing here is physical evidence.**

### What exists

* **Profile.** `kernel/Cargo.toml`: `boot-proofs` (the proofs that run on any machine; `qemu-self-test` and `baremetal-suite` both imply it, so every former `qemu-self-test` proof gate became `boot-proofs` with no change for the existing profile) and `baremetal-suite` (`freestanding` + `boot-proofs`, **no** COM1/debug-exit).
* **`kernel/src/baremetal.rs`** (binary crate, the privileged half): the 256 KiB lock-free capture of every console byte (`capture_byte`, claim-by-`fetch_add`, reentrancy-safe); the live-screen filter (`screen_byte`: a passing `init: test ... ok` line is drawn as one `.`, because every scrolled line redraws the whole framebuffer and the groups print ~700 of them); `record_proof` and the results store (`IrqMutex<Results>`, leaf lock); `suite_pause_ms=` and `suite_groups=` command-line options; the hardware inventory; the group loop (`run`, `start_next`, `group_ended`, `fatal`); the late proofs; the log replay and the summary screen. `kernel/src/pci.rs`: the read-only PCI scan with the side-effect argument in its module documentation.
* **`kernel/src/suite.rs`** (library, `forbid(unsafe_code)`, 12 host tests): the comparison of a group's output with `xtask/oracle/expected.tsv` (embedded with `include_bytes!`; the rules of the host `validate_oracle`, plus the console's `\r\n`), the FNV-1a digest, calendar conversion, bounded `Text`, PCI name tables.
* **`kernel/src/userspace.rs`**: `Rootfs`/`new_rootfs`/`load_rootfs` split out of the boot path (the boot path is unchanged), `load_group` (fresh rootfs from the retained archive copies, `-- <group>` as the command line), `teardown` (every user page unmapped and freed through the one `mm_unmap`, then `Process` dropped), `locks_free`; `init_died` and `stop` have a suite variant that ends the group instead of the boot; the `mm-usercopy` start-up proof is not run per group (the `mm` group covers it from ring 3).
* **Small supporting changes**: `main.rs` (`proof`/`proof_result`: outside the suite exactly `require`/`unwrap`, in the suite record and continue; `fail()` calls `baremetal::fatal` in the suite; exhaustion walk moved after the groups), `console.rs` (capture hook, screen filter, `clear_screen`, replay pager, `write_screen`), `firmware.rs` (ACPI table signatures kept), `boot.rs` (`option_value`), `cputime.rs` (`CpuAccount::start` resets a new task's accounting), `services.rs`/`kalloc.rs` (`Service::take`, `is_free`, `lock_free`), `paging.rs` (`Tables::has_zero_page`).
* **`user/init`**: two assertions made machine-independent without changing what Linux prints (`ids.sysinfo-ram` loses its 512 MiB ceiling only under the suite (fix round 2: the oracle and harness cells keep it); `time.monotonic-coarse-near-monotonic` reads the coarse clock first, since the other order fails on Linux itself when a tick falls between the reads, which a slow emulated CPU made a ~3% flake).
* **`xtask`**: `Target::Kernel { qemu_self_test, suite }`, the `baremetal-suite` command and `SUITE_CASES` with `suite::validate` (`xtask/src/kernel/suite.rs`).

### Behaviour that differs from Linux, loudly (test profile only)

1. `/init` ending in any way, or a kernel stop while a group runs, ends that group only (the kernel prints Linux's panic text first). A stop with a kernel lock held ends the suite with its results so far.
2. One boot runs several groups: a new `/init` on a new rootfs per group, the old process's pages freed, its intermediate page tables **kept** (`shortcut:` in `userspace::teardown`; Linux frees them with `exit_mmap`), and the clock keeps running (the reference recorded one boot per group; groups bound uptime to two minutes).
3. The screen shows `.` for a passing test line; the capture, replay and digest have every line.

### Safety argument for the added hardware access

Port I/O added: `0xCF8` (address, written) and `0xCFC` (data, read) only, standard-header offsets `0x00/0x08/0x0C` only, enable bit set, saved and restored; mechanism #1 presence is the `pci_check_type1` test without the mechanism-2 probe that writes `0xCFB`. No MMIO added. `SAFETY` comments name the contract (CPL0, BSP, IF clear, no other user of the ports). The capture buffers are atomics (no `unsafe`); the only new `unsafe` is `retain_archives` (the existing `module_bytes` contract) and the `pci` port calls.

### Five design questions

1. **Preexisting crate?** No new crate. `x86_64`'s `Port` and `raw-cpuid` (both admitted) do the I/O and CPUID; a PCI crate (`pci_types` and similar) was not admitted: the need is three register reads, not a PCI core.
2. **Bundle?** One mechanism per concept: the group runner reuses `userspace::{load_rootfs, start, init_died}` and the one `mm_unmap`; the comparison reproduces `validate_oracle` and the harness re-checks every passing group with the original; the replay and the summary share one capture.
3. **Simplify?** One profile and one result channel (the screen); no keyboard, serial, storage, network or log file; fixed-capacity results; the group list is a command-line option instead of a menu.
4. **Secure?** No write to any storage, firmware or device state (see above); bounded capture, bounded PCI scan, bounded line buffers, no user-controlled allocation; the heap copy of the initramfs is fallible; a kernel stop with locks held ends the suite instead of deadlocking. The suite weakens nothing in the normal profiles (`boot-proofs` is a rename of the former `qemu-self-test` gate, and the suite code is compiled only with its own feature).
5. **Improve?** A serial/USB-storage/network log once those drivers exist (the digest and the capture are ready for it); `exit_mmap`-style teardown that frees the page tables; the `signal` and `proc` groups when they are supported; per-vendor inventory (MSRs, SMBIOS, IOMMU) and PCIe ECAM/capability walking.

### Evidence

Source: branch `feat/baremetal-suite` at `a1b3160` (three commits on `main` fbd89b4; Ryzen 9 9900X workstation, QEMU TCG, KVM `-cpu host`; **no physical run**).

* Gates, `bash target/gates.sh "$PWD" bms-2 target/gates-bms-2`, every line `exit=0`: `fmt`, `host-clippy`, `host-test` (xtask 84, baremetal 10), `nightly-clippy`, `nightly-test` (kernel library 379 tests including 12 in `suite`), `kernel-test` **30/30 debug-exit cases and 4/4 baremetal-suite cases** (the 30 include the unchanged-expectation `oracle-ids` and `oracle-time` runs of the two relaxed assertions; the KVM suite case ran, not `unavailable`), `target-clippy-qemu-self-test`, `target-clippy-freestanding`, `check`, `status`. A first run (`bms-1`) failed exactly the four suite cases on the host's digest check (it began the transcript at the wrong line); the corrected start line reproduced the kernel's digest for all four logs offline before the rerun. `gates.sh` does not lint the new profiles, so `-D warnings` target Clippy of `baremetal-suite` and `qemu-self-test,baremetal-suite` was run separately (clean).
* Suite cases (`target/kernel-tooling/bms-2/cases/baremetal-suite*`): exit 33 (35 for `-stops`), 23/23 `PROOF ... PASS`, `ids` 158/158, `time` 119/119, `mm` 122/122, `fs` 286/286 on the kernel's comparison **and** the host `validate_oracle`, the recomputed log digest equal to the printed one; in `-stops` the `fault` probe ended `init signal 11`, the `io_uring` probe `kernel stop` (`FAIL syscall unimplemented nr=425`), and `ids` after them passed.
* The produced tree and its QEMU boot: `xtask baremetal-suite --output target/kernel-tooling/bms-final` (build id `a1b31603908f`): `usb/` holds `EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI` (376,832 B, sha256 `94308b4e...ad51`), `limine.conf` (174 B), `linux-rs-kernel` (10,911,792 B, sha256 `bf4612a4...eb6d`), `initramfs.cpio` (375,136 B), `SHA256SUMS`, `README.txt`; booted unchanged in QEMU to the final HLT; `tree-boot/summary-screen.png` shows `RESULT: ALL PASS (4 groups, 23 self-tests)` in about 19 s of guest time on 512 MiB (per-group times 88/218/98/148 ms; a 30 GiB machine adds the exhaustion walk and the replay pauses: unmeasured), and `tree-boot/page-NN.ppm` are the replay pages. A person read the screens; no program parsed them.
* Exploratory, not in the gates: with 8 GiB of guest RAM the two since-relaxed assertions failed (`ids.sysinfo-ram`, and once `time.monotonic-coarse-near-monotonic`), which is why they were relaxed; every other line passed.
* The signal lane (`feat/signals`) was not on `main` while this was written, so the `signal` group is listed as skipped; it joins `GROUPS` in `kernel/src/suite.rs` when its syscalls land.

### Review fix round 2 (time bounds, summary row budget, locks, wording)

Findings of the independent review of `a1b3160`/`b0cc95c`, each fixed at its root:

* **No dependence on an unpublished clock.** `console::pause` busy-waited on `ktime_get_ns`, a constant when `timer::init` fails before `clock_publish`, so the replay (and so the summary) never appeared. It now spins on the raw TSC (`timer::spin_ms`, calibrated rate, else an assumed 2 GHz, `shortcut:` named in the code), so the summary is reached after any boot-time failure, including `fatal` before the timer exists.
* **Per-group time bound.** `baremetal::expired` (raw TSC, 120 s, `suite_timeout_s=` on the command line) is checked by both tick handlers (after the EOI and re-arm) and by every iteration of `timer::wait_until_ns`; `timeout()` prints `FAIL group timeout after N s` and ends the group like a kernel stop (`Cause::TimedOut`, result cause `timeout`), or ends the suite with its results when a lock is still held (`groups_lock_free`). New harness probe `spin` (an `/init` mode that loops forever, `qemu-self-test` only) proves a hang becomes one failed group while the next group still runs and passes.
* **No clock, no `time` group.** If `timer::init` failed the `time` group (its sleeps can never end without the published clock) is not run: `SUITE group time not run: the clock is not running`, result cause `skipped, no clock`, counted as a failed group; the other groups and the summary still run.
* **Summary row budget.** The failure detail moved to the end of the screen (after the self-tests, footer and `SUITE complete`, before the final line) and is limited to the rows below the cursor (`console::rows_free`, which decodes `fbtext`'s cursor state; wrapped lines counted by `suite::rows_for`), shared fairly between the failing groups by the pure, host-tested `suite::RowBudget`, with an omission count; so no number of failing groups can scroll the heading, digest, inventory, RESULT line or group table off the screen.
* **Locks.** `ARCHIVES` is held across `load_group`, so it is documented as the front of the chain `ARCHIVES` -> `TABLES` -> heap -> `FRAMES` (module header and the DAG in `services.rs`; `RESULTS` is the leaf) and is now part of the free-lock test before a stopped group is ended (`groups_lock_free`). `ENTERED` is set on entry to `summary` itself, so a stop inside the summary halts instead of redrawing it.
* **Replay pages** carry the build id and log digest on every header (`-- linux-rs suite <build> <digest> replay page N`).
* **Uptime assertions.** `SUITE group <g> start uptime_s=<n>` records the boot time at each group start and passes it to `/init` as `-- <group> <n>`; `ids.sysinfo-uptime` and `time.monotonic-since-boot` count their two-minute bound from there under the suite and from boot elsewhere, and `ids.sysinfo-ram` keeps the 512 MiB upper bound everywhere except under the suite (so the oracle and harness cells check it again). The duration of the live phase on the real 2560x1440 display stays **unmeasured**.
* **Wording and tooling.** The never-does list now names the HPET restart and the PM-timer port; the README and the procedure ask for `lsblk -o NAME,SIZE,MODEL,TRAN,MOUNTPOINTS` (`TRAN=usb`), an unmount of any automount, and the copy from inside `usb/`; `target/gates.sh` also runs `-D warnings` target Clippy for `freestanding,baremetal-suite` and `qemu-self-test,baremetal-suite`.

**Fix round 2 evidence** (source `987930b` on `feat/baremetal-suite`, tree clean; QEMU only, no physical run):

* Gates, `bash target/gates.sh "$PWD" bms-fix-2 target/gates-bms-fix-2`, every line `exit=0`: `fmt`, `host-clippy`, `host-test` (xtask 84, baremetal 10), `nightly-clippy`, `nightly-test` (kernel library 380 tests, 13 in `suite`), `kernel-test` **30/30 debug-exit cases and 4/4 baremetal-suite cases** (`target/kernel-tooling/bms-fix-2`; the KVM case ran), `target-clippy-qemu-self-test`, `target-clippy-freestanding`, and the two new lines `target-clippy-freestanding+baremetal-suite` and `target-clippy-qemu-self-test+baremetal-suite`, `check`, `status`.
* `baremetal-suite-stops` (`suite_timeout_s=1 suite_groups=fault,io_uring,spin,ids`, exit 35): `spin` printed `init: spinning on purpose`, then `FAIL group timeout after 1 s`, result `cause="timeout"` after 1016 ms (ended from the timer tick in ring 3), and `ids` after it still ran and passed (158/158); the end of the serial log has `FAILURE DETAIL` with `io_uring stopped: FAIL syscall unimplemented nr=425` and `spin stopped: FAIL group timeout after 1 s` (the harness now requires those rows). A first run of this case on the pre-fix cursor arithmetic printed `... 2 more detail row(s) not shown` instead of the rows: `console::rows_free` had read `fbtext`'s state (cell number shifted left plus a wrap bit) as a cell number; fixed, and that run is why the harness checks the rows.
* The produced tree, `xtask baremetal-suite --output target/kernel-tooling/bmsf2-tree` (build id `987930bcf25e`): `usb/` holds `EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI` (sha256 `94308b4e...ad51`), `limine.conf` (174 B, `e2a0987b...`), `linux-rs-kernel` (11,044,136 B, `02c47d02...ee19`), `initramfs.cpio` (376,152 B, `1964c3f2...6e38`), `SHA256SUMS`, `README.txt`; booted unchanged in QEMU to the final HLT; `tree-boot/summary-screen.png` reads `RESULT: ALL PASS (4 groups, 23 self-tests)`, up 20 s; `tree-boot/page-NN.ppm` replay headers read `-- linux-rs suite 987930bcf25e <digest> replay page N` (the digest equal to the summary's). A person read the screens.
* No clock (manual, `target/kernel-tooling/bmsf2-noclock/final.png`, the tree's `disk.img` copied, QEMU TCG `-cpu qemu64,-tsc`): `PROOF timer-init FAIL`, `ids`, `mm`, `fs` pass, `time` shown as `FAIL 0/0 skipped, no clock`, `RESULT: FAIL - 3/4 groups, 14/15 self-tests passed`, the summary on screen after 13 s (the unfixed build never reached it: the `time` group hung in a sleep and the replay pager spun on the constant clock).
* **Not measured:** the live phase and the per-group `uptime_s` on the real 2560x1440 display; the 120 s bound and the 2 GHz fallback rate on any real CPU; a failing real group on the physical profile (the row budget is proven by the pure `RowBudget` host test and by the QEMU rows above, not on a small or large physical screen); a hang with the tick dead and no sleep involved.

### Not done (named, not hidden)

No physical run. No serial, storage or network log. `proc` is skipped (no procfs; it runs since the [pipes, `poll` and job control](#pipes-poll-and-job-control) stage, which did not need procfs); `signal` joined the suite on integration onto `main` ([below](#suite-integration-onto-main-signal-group)). One CPU only. Intermediate user page tables of a finished group are leaked. The summary is not machine-read: the QEMU screendumps are read by a person. No independent review of the suite yet.

### Suite integration onto main (signal group)

The suite branch (`feat/baremetal-suite`, six commits on the identity/time/mm/VFS tip) was rebased onto `main` `3e76422`, which adds PR #23 (signal delivery). Conflicts: `kernel/src/cpu.rs` and `kernel/src/main.rs` (main's `PtRegs` entry and `allocator_ext` feature line against the suite's `boot-proofs` feature rename: both kept, the `qemu-self-test` gate of the proof code became `boot-proofs`), `kernel/src/services.rs` (main's blocking-syscall lock paragraph and the suite's `ARCHIVES` order, both kept) and `CHANGELOG.md` (both entries kept). The routing prose needed no change: the suite touches no `planning/workstreams.tsv` row, and `cargo +1.88.0 run -p xtask --locked --offline -- status` reports the numbers all five documents already state (23 `in_progress`, 2,600 `not_started` of 2,623).

* **`signal` is a suite group.** `suite::GROUPS` is `ids, time, mm, fs, signal` (`UNSUPPORTED` is `proc` only), `xtask`'s `suite::ALL_PASS` has five entries, the group needs the published clock like `time` (`NEEDS_CLOCK`; its sleeps, `pause` and `ITIMER_REAL` waits never end without it), and the README written by `xtask baremetal-suite` and `docs/bare-metal-suite.md` say so. 202 lines are compared for it.
* **No signal state leaks between groups.** Handlers, masks, the pending queues, `sigaltstack` and `ITIMER_REAL` live in `Process::signal`, which `userspace::teardown` drops with the process; the next group's `start` builds `State::new_init`. The part outside the `Process` is the clockevent: `timer::ITIMER_NS` and `DEADLINE_NS`. `teardown` now calls `timer::clear_waits()` so a group that ended (by exit, fatal signal, kernel stop or the time bound) with an interval timer armed or a sleep abandoned does not leave a stale wake-up. The `spin` probe of `baremetal-suite-stops` now arms a 2 ms `ITIMER_REAL` before looping, so the existing `spin` -> `ids` ordering exercises exactly that: the timeout ends the group with the timer armed and `ids` after it still passes. A group ended by a fatal signal is recorded as `init signal N` (`Cause::Exited(code & 0x7f)`; the `fault` probe shows `init signal 11`).
* **`ids.sysinfo-ram` has an equivalent bound again.** The suite lane had removed the 512 MiB ceiling under the suite. The suite now passes the RAM its memory map declares (usable + ACPI-reclaimable + bootloader + kernel/modules bytes) as the fourth word of `/init`'s arguments (`-- <group> <uptime_s> <ram bytes>`, `userspace::load_group`), and the test bounds `totalram` by it (`ram_ceiling()`; 512 MiB when no word is given, so the reference run is unchanged). The assertion text on a pass is identical, so `expected.tsv` is unchanged.
* **Not fixed, named:** a group that times out while *sleeping* (a `pause` or `rt_sigtimedwait` that no signal ever ends) holds `PROCESS` and `TABLES` across `Task::wait` (the documented blocking-syscall exception in `services.rs`), so `groups_lock_free()` is false and the suite ends with the summary instead of starting the next group. `signal` is the last group, so no group is lost today, but a hung group before it would hide the later ones; forcing those two locks free from the timeout (sound only because the abandoned stack is never resumed) is the follow-up.

**Integration evidence** (QEMU only, no physical run; same workstation, user and toolchain installation as earlier records, not independently attested):

* Gates, `bash target/gates.sh "$PWD" bms-int-1 target/gates-bms-int-1` on `3673922` (the record commit after it is docs only; `bms-int-2` repeats the run on the final tree), every line `exit=0`: `fmt`, `host-clippy`, `host-test` (xtask 84, baremetal 10), `nightly-clippy`, `nightly-test` (kernel library 414 tests, xtask 84, baremetal 10, rt 2), `kernel-test` **34/34 debug-exit cases and 4/4 baremetal-suite cases**, the four `-D warnings` target Clippy profiles (`qemu-self-test`, `freestanding`, `freestanding,baremetal-suite`, `qemu-self-test,baremetal-suite`), `check`, `status`.
* The oracle cases `oracle-ids`, `-time`, `-mm`, `-fs`, `-signal` and every other debug-exit case matched. The `baremetal-suite`, `-two-displays` and `-kvm` cases each ended `RESULT: ALL PASS (5 groups, 23 self-tests)` with `ids` 158/158, `time` 119/119, `mm` 122/122, `fs` 286/286, `signal` 202/202 and `SUITE complete ok=true groups=5/5 proofs=23/23`; `baremetal-suite-stops` (`fault`, `io_uring`, `spin`, `ids`, exit 35) ended `fault` `init signal 11`, `io_uring` `kernel stop`, `spin` `timeout` after 1016 ms with the 2 ms `ITIMER_REAL` armed, then `ids` 158/158 pass.
* Pinned Linux, the final harness-built `/init` and initramfs, `target/linux-reference/init-reference-008/` (README, commands, logs, `SHA256SUMS`; untracked): `-- ids` and `-- time` under TCG `-cpu qemu64 -m 512M` equal `xtask/oracle/expected.tsv` line for line (158 and 119 lines); `-m 8G` with `-- ids 0 8589934592` and `-- time 0 8589934592` equal it as well (so the RAM word is what makes `ids.sysinfo-ram` hold on any RAM size); `-m 512M -- ids 0 536870912` equals it; the negative control (`-m 8G -- ids 0 536870912`) fails exactly `ids.sysinfo-ram`.

## Task model and process syscalls

Roadmap `WP-M3.3` (fork/exit/wait), `WP-M3.10` (job control) and the signal-state split that prepares `WP-M3.4` for more than one task; lane `task-model`, branch `feat/task-model`, the first four work packages of the process-lane design (`target/designs/process-lane.md`, a local planning artifact of the lane, not part of the tree): **WP0** the lock DAG and non-parity table, **WP1** the behaviour-preserving `signal::State` split, **WP2** the pure `Tasklist` model, **WP3** the process syscalls on that model plus `execve`/`execveat`. There is still no `fork`, no second kernel stack and no scheduler (other lanes): exactly one task, init, exists on the machine, so the model's multi-task behaviour is established on the host and the machine exercises the argument, `ECHILD` and exec paths. Nothing here is whole-kernel, SMP or physical evidence.

### What exists

| Piece | Where | Linux source (6.18.57) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **WP0.** The lock-order DAG (`TASKLIST -> VFS -> TABLES -> heap -> FRAMES`, `TASKLIST -> TIMERQ -> TIMER_HW`, `any -> RQ`; forbidden `VFS -> TASKLIST`, `TIMERQ -> TASKLIST`) and the table of differences T1-T8 | [`services.rs`](../kernel/src/services.rs), [process.md](subsystems/process.md#lock-order-and-non-parity-table-for-the-task-lane) | `kernel/fork.c`, `kernel/exit.c`, `kernel/signal.c` lock comments |
| **WP1.** `signal::State` is now a view `State<'a>{hand, sig, me, ids}` over `SigHand` (`sighand_struct`), `SignalStruct` (`signal_struct`) and `ThreadSignal` (the task's fields), owned together by `TaskSignals` (1:1 with the task, the only shortcut); `pid`/`tgid`/`uid`/`global_init` are derived by the owner of the task, not stored twice; `SignalStruct` gains the job-control bits and stop code `wait` reads | [`signal/state.rs`](../kernel/src/signal/state.rs) | `include/linux/sched/signal.h`, `signal_types.h` |
| **WP2.** A generational fallible `Arena`, `PidTable` (`idr_alloc_cyclic`: cursor, `RESERVED_PIDS` wrap, first-allocation reset; `init_struct_pid` = number 0), `TaskStruct` with the credential snapshot, `Tasklist::{new, fork, release_task}`, `forget_original_parent`/`reparent_leader`/`exit_notify`/`do_notify_parent(_cldstop)` with `SA_NOCLDWAIT`/`SIG_IGN` auto-reap, `wait_consider_task` with `wait_task_{zombie,stopped,continued}` and the `wait4`/`waitid` prepare matrices, `setpgid`/`getpgid`/`getsid`/`setsid`, `will_become_orphaned_pgrp`/`kill_orphaned_pgrp`, and the `kill` family (`check_kill_permission`, `__kill_pgrp_info`, `kill_something_info`) | [`arena.rs`](../kernel/src/arena.rs), [`task/`](../kernel/src/task/mod.rs): `pid`, `tasklist`, `exit`, `wait`, `jobctl`, `kill` | `kernel/pid.c`, `lib/idr.c`, `kernel/fork.c` (`copy_process`), `kernel/exit.c`, `kernel/sys.c`, `kernel/signal.c` |
| **WP3.** `getppid`/`getpgrp`/`getpgid`/`getsid`/`setsid`/`setpgid` read the model (the `ppid`/`pgid`/`sid` fields of `Attributes` are gone); `wait4`/`waitid` (argument, `ECHILD`, status/`rusage`/`siginfo` stores, the blocking wait on `Waker::child_event`); `Outcome::Exit{code, group}` through `Tasklist::{do_group_exit, exit_prepare}` (init still panics with Linux's text); `execve`/`execveat` | [`syscall/process.rs`](../kernel/src/syscall/process.rs), [`syscall/exec.rs`](../kernel/src/syscall/exec.rs), [`exec/bprm.rs`](../kernel/src/exec/bprm.rs), [`userspace.rs`](../kernel/src/userspace.rs) (`do_exit`, `Process.tasks`) | `kernel/exit.c` (`wait4`, `waitid`, `do_exit`), `kernel/sys.c`, `fs/exec.c` |

`Context` gained `tasks()` (the list, `current`, and the `Waker`), `replace_exe_file`, `exec_aux` and `start_thread`; `AddressSpace::pages` is a `&mut dyn Pager` so exec can install frames with contents through the one mapping-rule mechanism. The binary keeps the task list inside `Process` (no new lock), builds init's task at `start`, and takes the signal state of `current` from it, so there is one source of truth for signals.

### `execve` in the order of `do_execveat_common`

1. `getname` (`EFAULT`, `ENAMETOOLONG`, `ENOENT` for an empty name unless `AT_EMPTY_PATH`); `execveat` flag bits outside `AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW|AT_EMPTY_PATH|AT_EXECVE_CHECK` are `EINVAL`, after the name was copied.
2. `alloc_bprm` -> `do_open_execat`: the path walk and `MAY_EXEC` check through the VFS `open` (`ENOENT`, `ENOTDIR`, `EACCES` for a directory or a file without `x`), the whole file read (shortcut S11 of the process plan), the name of a path relative to a descriptor (`/dev/fd/N/path`).
3. `count(argv)`, `count(envp)` (`EFAULT`, `E2BIG`; a group exit under way is `ERESTARTNOHAND`), `bprm_stack_limits` (`string_budget`), then the strings in `copy_strings` order (filename, `envp` last-to-first, `argv` last-to-first; `EFAULT`, `E2BIG`), an empty `argv` getting `""`.
4. The stack block is built into a kernel buffer, so `E2BIG` still precedes `ENOEXEC`; `AT_EXECVE_CHECK` stops here with 0.
5. `exec_binprm`: `#!` then ELF acceptance (`ENOEXEC`, the interpreter lookup errnos, `Unsupported` for a valid script/dynamic image: a loud stop).
6. **Point of no return** (`begin_new_exec`): `do_close_on_exec`, `mm_release` of `clear_child_tid`/`robust_list`, `set_mm_exe_file`, `exec_mmap` (the old VMAs are unmapped, a fresh `Mm` built; `setup_arg_pages`, `elf_load`, the argument pages installed), `flush_signal_handlers` (handlers to `SIG_DFL`, `SIG_IGN` kept, mask and pending kept, altstack address cleared), `comm` from `kbasename`, `PF_FORKNOEXEC` cleared and the credential snapshot published, `flush_thread` + `start_thread` (FPU reset, TLS bases zero, registers zero except `ip`/`sp`/`cs`/`ss`/`flags`, as `iret_to_user` builds them). A failure after this point is `force_fatal_sig(SIGSEGV)`; the task never runs the old image.

### Unsafe / assembly

None added. `start_thread` uses the existing `UserCpu::fpu_reset` and the MSR wrappers `set_user_{fs,gs}_base`; the register image is written through the existing `PtRegs` the entry stub built, and the existing `iretq` site returns to it. The task model is `forbid(unsafe_code)`.

### Shortcuts (each is a `shortcut:` or contract comment at its site; the table in [process.md](subsystems/process.md#lock-order-and-non-parity-table-for-the-task-lane) is the register)

T1 one thread per group; T2 no ptrace/subreaper/pidfd; T3 the idle task is not a task; T4 capabilities derive from `euid == 0`; T5 `kill(0)`/`kill(-1)` still stop loudly through `sys_kill` (the model implements them); T6 pid numbering and `pid_max`; T7 the `execve` argument block is limited to four stack pages and several exec details are not modelled; T8 `task_is_stopped` is a `jobctl` bit; T9 `RLIMIT_NPROC` counts `nr_threads`, not per-user ucounts. In addition: `PidTable` searches a free number linearly (O(`pid_max`) per allocation, bounded by 32768); a `ENOMEM` can replace success in `setpgid`/`setsid` when the heap is exhausted (Linux's `attach_pid` cannot fail); reaped children's `rusage` carries only the two CPU times (the rest is zero); `utime`/`stime` of an exiting task are supplied by the owner (`TaskStruct::utime_ns`) because the CPU accounting is not yet per task.

### The five design questions

* *Can I use a preexisting crate?* No arena or pid crate qualifies (infallible growth, no generation check), recorded in [dependencies](dependencies.md#task-list-arena-and-pid-table-no-crate-admitted); `syscalls` supplies `Errno`/`Sysno`, `bitflags` was not needed.
* *Can I bundle functionality together?* One `Tasklist` owns pids, parentage, groups, sessions, credential snapshots and signal objects, because they share one lock in Linux and one set of invariants (a pid number lives while any link is attached); `wait`, `exit`, `jobctl` and `kill` are methods of it in separate files. `execve` reuses the loader's `LoadPlan`, `insert_stack`, `map_segments`, `stack::build` and the VFS `open`, and the boot loader's `open_exec_file` helpers moved into `exec::bprm` for both.
* *Can I simplify things?* Intrusive child lists (so reparenting cannot fail to allocate), a vector-backed pid table, fixed-size batches for auto-reaping, and the stack block built in a kernel buffer instead of copying strings between two mms. The simplifications that change behaviour are the shortcuts above.
* *Is this secure?* Every allocation is fallible and reserved before publication (`Tasklist::fork` cannot fail after the first link; `release_task` and `change_pid` never allocate); stale ids resolve to nothing; cross-task permission checks read `CredSnapshot`, never the owner's state (a test pins that no credential-changing syscall exists without a publish); user memory is reached only through `usercopy`; exec strings are bounded (`MAX_ARG_STRLEN`, the window, `count` polls for a fatal signal); `kill`/`wait` never touch a released task; an exec failure after the point of no return kills the task instead of returning into a freed image.
* *How can I improve this?* Wire `fork` (the model's `Tasklist::fork` and `Mm::dup`), the scheduler's `Waker`, `kill(0/-1)`, `pidfd`, capabilities and a real `RLIMIT_STACK`-sized argument window; replace the linear pid search with a bitmap; give each task its own page-table root so exec frees the old tables.

### Evidence

* `cargo +nightly test -p kernel --lib`: all host tests pass (the task, arena, pid, `wait4`/`waitid`/exec syscall tests among them: stale ids, truth tables of `setpgid`/`setsid`, status encodings, reparent order, orphaned groups, kill permissions, credential snapshot, the exec errno order and a full successful exec on the fake address space).
* `kernel-test` on this tree: 34/34 debug-exit cases matched, including `oracle-ids`, `oracle-time`, `oracle-mm`, `oracle-fs` and `oracle-signal` against the pinned Linux's lines (run `wp3-kt-001`, QEMU TCG `qemu64`); the WP1 commit alone also matched 34/34 (`wp1-kt-001`).
* Gates: `bash target/gates.sh "$PWD" taskmodel-g3 target/gates-taskmodel-g3` on tree `c9860e9` (the record commit after it is docs only): every step `exit=0` (`fmt`, `host-clippy`, `host-test`, `nightly-clippy`, `nightly-test` with 457 kernel library tests, `kernel-test` 34/34 debug-exit cases plus 4/4 `baremetal-suite` cases, the four target Clippy profiles, `check`, `status` 24 `in_progress` / 2,599 `not_started`). The branch was rebased onto `main` `d3d8f94` (the bare-metal suite merge) before this run; the earlier un-rebased runs `wp1-kt-001` and `wp3-kt-001` also matched 34/34.
* The `proc` oracle group cannot complete yet: its first test forks. Running a **local, uncommitted** variant of `user/init`'s `proc` group with only the fork-free tests (30 of its 135 rows) on this kernel printed exactly the pinned Linux's line for each: `wait4-no-children`, `wait4-missing-pid`, `wait4-missing-pgid`, `wait4-pid-truncated-to-int`, `wait4-int-min-32`, `wait4-bad-options`, `wait4-wexited-not-allowed`, `wait4-wnohang-no-children`, `waitid-no-children`, `waitid-bad-idtype`, `waitid-no-event-bits`, `waitid-bad-options`, `waitid-wnohang-only`, `waitid-fault-no-children`, `waitid-wrong-pid`, `waitid-pidfd-bad-fd`, `setpgid-missing`, `setpgid-negative-pgid`, and the twelve `execve-*`/`execveat-*` failure rows (`missing`, `not-executable`, `directory`, `not-an-elf`, `empty-file`, `through-file`, `fault-path`, `fault-argv`, `fault-envp`, `fault-argv-string`, `execveat-missing`, `execveat-bad-flags`); serial log retained as `target/proc-exp-001-serial.log` of the lane worktree. A second local variant had init call `execve("/init", ["/init","execd","third arg"], ["EXEC=1","B=two"])` itself after setting up inherited and close-on-exec descriptors, a handled and an ignored signal, a blocked signal, umask and cwd: the re-executed `/init execd` printed the oracle's `EXEC_OUTPUT` line field for field (`fd40=Ok(0) fd41=Err(EBADF) usr1=0x0 usr2=0x1 usr1_blocked=true umask=0o77 cwd=/fixture`) with `ppid=0`, which is correct for the process that is init itself (the oracle's child prints `ppid=1`), and exited 42 (`target/proc-exp-002-exec-serial.log`). The full group (135 rows) is not run and not claimed: the 105 other rows need `fork`, blocking and job control.

### Not done (named, not hidden)

`fork`/`clone`/`vfork`, kernel stacks and context switch, the scheduler and `Waker` implementation, blocking `wait` across tasks, `SIGCHLD` delivery to a real parent, job-control stops (`do_signal_stop`), `kill(0)`/`kill(-1)` wiring, `pidfd`, credentials/capabilities, `execveat(AT_EMPTY_PATH, "")`, the `proc`-group rows beyond the 30 above, SMP, RCU, physical hardware and performance. No independent review has happened (author round only).

## Scheduler core

Lane `sched` of the process plan (`target/designs/process-lane.md`, WP4; the plan's other work packages belong to the task-model lane and are not claimed here). Branch `feat/sched-core`. It adds the machinery that lets more than one task share the CPU and sleep: per-task kernel stacks, the register context switch, a FIFO run queue, wait keys, a timer queue, the idle loop and the tick-driven preemption point. The single user process (`/init`) now runs as a scheduled task on a pool stack; nothing it can observe changed (every oracle group still matches). Fix round 1 (after review): the bare-metal suite's per-group lifecycle ([below](#group-lifecycle-in-the-bare-metal-suite)), Linux's wake order and mode for key wake-ups, and a `kernel-test` case for the tick-driven preemption at the return to ring 3.

### What exists

| Piece | Where | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Scheduler model: `Rq`, `SchedId` (generational), `TaskState`, `WaitKey`, `WakeMode`, FIFO queue, `try_to_wake_up`, `signal_wake_up`, `prepare_to_wait[_exclusive]`, `wake_up_key(key, mode, nr_exclusive)`, `schedule`, `tick`, dead-slot `free`, group `reset` | [`kernel/src/sched/mod.rs`](../kernel/src/sched/mod.rs) | `forbid(unsafe_code)`, no allocation after `Rq::new`, host tests |
| Timer queue (`TimerQueue<K>`: sorted, fixed capacity, one entry per owner, FIFO among equal expiries, `clear`) | [`kernel/src/sched/timerq.rs`](../kernel/src/sched/timerq.rs) | 5 host tests |
| Tasks: `KSTACKS` pool use, `Thread` register contexts, `spawn`/`spawn_user`/`start_init`, `schedule`, `context_switch`, `finish_task_switch`, `ret_from_fork`, `sleep_current`, wake wrappers, `preempt_user`, `idle_loop`, `timer_interrupt`, `scheduler_tick`, and for the suite `reset_group`/`in_idle`/`locks_free` | [`kernel/src/ktask/mod.rs`](../kernel/src/ktask/mod.rs) | binary side (unsafe where the hardware needs it) |
| Stack pool, `set_kernel_stack`, `syscall_entry` on the task stack, `restore_regs_and_iret`, `switch_to_asm`, `ret_from_fork_asm`, `fixup_bad_iret` over the pool, per-task FP/segment state, `Segments`/`FxImage` | [`kernel/src/cpu.rs`](../kernel/src/cpu.rs) | three new naked sites, one changed |
| Guard pages of the pool left unmapped; clockevent armed for the timer-queue head (`WAKE_NS`) | [`kernel/src/paging.rs`](../kernel/src/paging.rs), [`kernel/src/timer.rs`](../kernel/src/timer.rs) | `CHECK paging ... guards=5` is unchanged: the 128 pool guards are checked by `sched-switch` |
| Keyed interruptible sleep for the signal wait API | [`kernel/src/signal/wait.rs`](../kernel/src/signal/wait.rs), `signal::Task::wait(key, deadline)` | `wait_event_interruptible(task, key, ready)`; `sleep_until`, `pause`, `rt_sigsuspend`, `rt_sigtimedwait` use `WaitKey::NONE` |
| `CONFIG_PREEMPT_VOLUNTARY=y` declared (with the reference config's `PREEMPT_DYNAMIC`, `NO_HZ_IDLE` differences stated) | [`kernel/src/config.rs`](../kernel/src/config.rs) | |
| Cases `sched-switch`, `sched-preempt` and `kstack-overflow` | [`kernel/src/ktask/selftest.rs`](../kernel/src/ktask/selftest.rs), `xtask/src/kernel.rs` | `test=sched-switch`, `test=sched-preempt -- signals`, `test=kstack-overflow` (self-test builds only; a `test=` word now counts anywhere before `--`) |

### Behaviour, with Linux 6.18.57 citations

* **Run queue** (`kernel/sched/core.c`): `__schedule` (6801) picks the next task; a still-runnable previous task goes to the tail (`Rq::schedule`), a sleeper or a dead task does not. `wake_up_new_task` (4831) queues a new task at the tail. `try_to_wake_up` (4159) / `wake_up_state` (4444) make a sleeper runnable; waking the current task only restores `TASK_RUNNING`. `sched_tick` (5597) charges the slice and sets `need_resched` when another task waits; `sysctl_sched_base_slice` is 0.7 ms (`fair.c:79`), under one tick at HZ=1000, so `SLICE_TICKS` is 1.
* **Task states**: `TASK_RUNNING/INTERRUPTIBLE/UNINTERRUPTIBLE/DEAD` (`include/linux/sched.h`). `signal_wake_up_state` (`kernel/signal.c:745`) wakes `TASK_INTERRUPTIBLE` only (the `WAKEKILL` stopped/traced case arrives with job control).
* **Wait channels**: `prepare_to_wait_event` (`kernel/sched/wait.c:290-320`), `__wake_up_common` (93-117), `__add_wait_queue` (`include/linux/wait.h:171-182`). The channel is a `WaitKey` stored in the sleeper's slot instead of a `wait_queue_head`, so a wake scans the slots; the empty key is woken by nothing. **Wake order is Linux's, not first-come first-served**: a non-exclusive waiter joins the *head* of the queue and the wake walks from the head, so the non-exclusive sleepers of a key wake *newest first*; an exclusive waiter (`prepare_to_wait_exclusive`) joins the tail, so the exclusive ones follow, oldest first, and `nr_exclusive` stops the walk after that many exclusive wake-ups (`wake_up` is 1; 0, as in `wake_up_all`, wakes every one). `wake_up_key(key, mode, nr_exclusive)` takes Linux's `mode`: `Normal` (`TASK_NORMAL`: `wake_up*`) wakes interruptible and uninterruptible sleepers, `Interruptible` (`wake_up_interruptible*`, the pipe/tty/poll wake-ups) only interruptible ones, and a waiter the mode does not admit is skipped without using the count (`try_to_wake_up` returns 0 and the scan continues). Blocking is Linux's re-check loop (`___wait_event`, `include/linux/wait.h:302`): prepare, check the condition and signals, `schedule()`, finish; spurious wake-ups are legal.
