Divergence (shortcut S5): Linux's `wait_event` keeps its entry queued across the iterations of the loop (`prepare_to_wait_event` adds it only while it is unqueued), so a waiter that is woken for a condition it does not find true keeps its place in the queue; here each `sleep_current` re-joins, so a non-exclusive waiter becomes the newest again (the next wake-up reaches it first, where Linux would reach it in its original place) and an exclusive one moves behind its peers. Unobservable today: there is one user task, and `wait4`/`waitid` on the parent's `wait_chldexit` key (`task::exit::chldexit_key`, woken by `SchedWaker::child_event`) is the only keyed user, with one sleeper; the pipe/wait lanes inherit the order and must fix it before they put two sleepers on one key.
* **Dead tasks** (`do_task_dead` 6953, `finish_task_switch` 5168): the exiting task marks itself `Dead` and calls `schedule()` once more; the next task, in `finish_task_switch`, frees its slot (the generation moves on, so old `SchedId`s name nothing) and with it the stack. The stack is therefore never reused while its owner still runs on it.
* **Context switch** (`context_switch` 5293, `arch/x86/entry/entry_64.S:177` `__switch_to_asm`, `arch/x86/kernel/process_64.c` `__switch_to`): save the user FP image and the segment selectors/bases of a ring-3 task, swap the bad-iret depth, restore the next task's, `set_kernel_stack(next)`, `switch_to_asm`. `fpu_switch` is eager (the kernel is soft-float, so user FP state is live in the registers whenever ring 0 runs); kernel threads and idle do not own FP state (`PF_KTHREAD`).
* **First run** (`ret_from_fork_asm` `entry_64.S:228`, `ret_from_fork` `arch/x86/kernel/process.c:151`, `copy_thread`): the stack of a new task is a `ForkFrame` (six callee-saved slots, the return address, a `pt_regs`); a kernel thread runs `func(arg)` then `do_task_dead`; a user task runs the exit-to-user work (pending signals, timers, a pending reschedule) and `iretq`s. `/init` itself starts this way; `enter_user` and its separate `IRETQ` site are gone, so `iret_site` is again the only `iretq` of the kernel (`fixup_bad_iret` depends on that).
* **Kernel stacks**: a pool of 128 stacks, each a 4 KiB unmapped guard page plus 64 KiB (`BOOT_STACK`'s type), is in the image's bss (about 8.5 MiB); the idle task is the boot context on `BOOT_STACK`, as `swapper` runs on its boot stack. `TSS.RSP0` and the `syscall_entry` stack (`KERNEL_STACK_TOP`) are set on every switch (`update_task_stack`). `fixup_bad_iret` accepts a frame inside any pool slot. A write to a guard faults; with no room for the exception frame the fault becomes a #DF on its IST stack and `unexpected` prints `FAIL kernel stack overflow slot=N` (Linux `handle_stack_overflow`). `STACK_END_MAGIC` (the lowest usable word) is checked on every switch-out.
* **Preemption model** (`kernel/Kconfig.preempt:36`, `kernel/entry/common.c:19`): ring 0 is never preempted. The tick sets `need_resched`; the return to ring 3 (`exit_to_user` -> `ktask::preempt_user`) and the idle loop honour it. The tick order in `lapic_timer_interrupt` (EOI, accounting, wake due sleepers, `rearm`) is unchanged and `exit_to_user` still runs after the EOI, so a switch inside the handler is sound: the entry stub's frame lives on the task's own stack. The switch taken inside the user-interrupt handler is exercised by the `sched-preempt` case (below); the syscall-return variant of the same call is not separately counted (ring 0 runs with IF=0, so `need_resched` is almost never set during a system call).
* **Timed sleep** (`schedule_hrtimeout_range`, `kernel/time/sleep_timeout.c`): `sleep_current(key, wake_at)` arms the task's entry in the timer queue, publishes the queue head to the clockevent (`WAKE_NS`), sleeps, and cancels the entry. The task's `ITIMER_REAL` expiry is folded into `wake_at`, so a sleeping task is still woken for it (nanosleep, `pause`, `rt_sigtimedwait` and `rt_sigsuspend` keep working: `signals`, `signals-nolapic`, `signals-kvm`, `oracle-time` and `oracle-signal` are cases of the suite). A deadline that was already due when published is never armed in the clockevent; the idle loop drains the queue before every halt, which catches it. `timer::wait_event` is deleted (one sleep mechanism).
* **Idle** (`do_idle` `kernel/sched/idle.c:276`): drain due timers, run what is runnable, else `sti; hlt` (`timer::idle`, with its one-instruction interrupt shadow, so no wake-up slips between the check and the halt). The periodic tick keeps running while idle (no `NO_HZ_IDLE`).

### Assembly sites (full proof blocks are the comments above each function in `cpu.rs`; disassembly below)

| Site | Why asm | Contract in one line |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `switch_to_asm(prev_sp, next_sp)` (new, naked) | exchanges RSP between two kernel stacks; no `asm!` may change RSP net | pushes RBP,RBX,R12-R15, stores RSP to `*prev_sp`, loads `next_sp`, pops them in reverse, `ret`; CPL0, IF=0, DF=0; SysV call; no red zone; `next_sp` was stored by an earlier switch or built by `spawn` |
| `ret_from_fork_asm` (new, naked) | first instruction of a task has no Rust caller; it moves RBX/R12 (frame) into argument registers | `mov rdi,rsp; mov rsi,rbx; mov rdx,r12; sub rsp,8; call ret_from_fork; add rsp,8; jmp restore_regs_and_iret`; RSP is 8 mod 16 at entry (168-byte `pt_regs` under a 16-aligned top, pinned by a `const` assertion), so the CALL is aligned |
| `restore_regs_and_iret` (new, naked, factored out of `syscall_entry`) | pops 15 GPRs and ends in `IRETQ`; shared so each entry path cannot forget one | `jmp`ed to with RSP at a complete `pt_regs`; ends in the one `iret_site` |
| `syscall_entry` (changed) | unchanged reasons | `lea rsp,[BOOT_STACK+size]` became `mov rsp,[KERNEL_STACK_TOP]`; the tail became `jmp restore_regs_and_iret`; SFMASK keeps IF=0 so no `schedule()` runs between `SYSCALL` and the load |
| `callee_saved_probe` (test only, `ktask/selftest.rs`) | puts chosen values into the callee-saved registers across a call that switches tasks | returns 1 when all six survive |

Review state: written by the lane author; **no independent architecture or unsafe review has happened** (the plan requires one per site before a claim beyond "exercised on QEMU"). Disassembly of every site, from the exact ELFs of `kernel-test` run `sched-001`, is kept at `target/evidence/sched/{freestanding,qemu-self-test}.asm-sites.txt` (whole-ELF listings next to them); the bytes match the sources above (`switch_to_asm` is 15 instructions, `ret_from_fork_asm` 7, `restore_regs_and_iret` 17).

### Group lifecycle in the bare-metal suite

The suite starts one `/init` per group in one boot (`baremetal::start_next`), so the scheduler must survive a group's end of any kind. The first version of this lane assumed one start per boot (`ktask::init` asserted on the second call, the group's end ran on the dead task's own pool stack and the next group's `/init` would have been spawned onto that same stack, and the clockevent's scheduler word and the run queue outlived a timed-out group). Now:

* `ktask::init` is once per boot (a second call finds the scheduler installed).
* `baremetal::run` runs on the boot stack, which is the idle task's. `userspace::run(loaded)` spawns `/init` as a task and idles (`ktask::start_init` -> `idle_loop`) until that task's `SchedId` no longer resolves, then **returns**; `baremetal::finish_group` runs on the boot stack: `userspace::teardown` (process, user pages, `timer::clear_waits` of the deadline, `ITIMER_REAL` and scheduler wake-up words) and `ktask::reset_group` (`Rq::reset`: every task slot freed with a new generation, queue empty, `need_resched` clear; `TimerQueue::clear`; the pending switch word; the bad-iret depth). The next group's `/init` therefore starts on a scheduler as `ktask::init` left it.
* A group ends in the task's context (`init_exited` from the syscall or exit-to-user path, `fatal`, or `timeout` from the tick on the task's stack): `group_ended` records the result and calls `ktask::exit_current()` (`do_task_dead`). The task leaves the CPU for good; the idle context's `finish_task_switch` frees its slot, so the abandoned syscall, fault or hardirq frame lives on a freed pool stack nothing refers to. Interrupts are masked first (`schedule()` asserts it).
* An end that is already on the boot stack (`ktask::in_idle`: the group failed before its task ran, or a handler interrupted the idle loop) tears down in place and calls `start_next`; that nests one frame chain per such end (shortcut, stated in `baremetal::run`).
* `userspace::locks_free` now includes `ktask::locks_free` (`RQ`, `TIMERQ`): a stop that abandoned a stack holding either ends the suite with its summary instead of the group, as for `PROCESS`/`TABLES`/`FRAMES`.
* A group whose task blocks forever inside a system call still holds `PROCESS`/`TABLES` on its sleeping stack, so its timeout ends the suite (summary), exactly as before this lane; it is not a regression, and it is not fixed here.

Evidence: `kernel-test` `baremetal-suite-stops` runs `fault` (init killed by SIGSEGV), `io_uring` (kernel stop), `spin` (a ring-3 busy loop cut by the 1 s bound from the tick handler on the task's stack, with a 2 ms `ITIMER_REAL` armed) and then `ids` 158/158 on the same boot; `baremetal-suite` and its `-two-displays` and `-kvm` variants run five groups back to back. Not exercised by any case: the in-idle end (a group that fails to load or start), a timeout while the task sleeps.

### Fix round 1 (review findings of `feat/sched-core`)

| Finding | Resolution |
| --- | --- |
| blocking: the branch did not merge with `main`'s bare-metal suite, and the suite's per-group start broke `ktask::init`, the idle assertion and teardown | Rebased onto `main` (d3d8f94); the lifecycle above; `userspace::run`/`start`/`ktask::start_init` return in place of `-> !` (a normal boot reports `FAIL init task left the run queue` if one returns); all ten gate lines re-run |
| should fix: key wake order documented as Linux's but it was first-come first-served | The model now has Linux's queue order and `nr_exclusive`/`mode` semantics (the Wait channels bullet above); tests `wake_up_key_wakes_only_that_key_newest_non_exclusive_first`, `exclusive_waiters_queue_behind_and_wake_oldest_first_up_to_the_count`, `wake_up_all_wakes_every_exclusive_waiter_too`, `interruptible_mode_skips_uninterruptible_sleepers_without_using_the_count`; the divergence that remains (a looping waiter re-joins the head) is named under shortcuts |
| should fix: the tick-driven reschedule at the return to ring 3 had no test | `kernel-test` case `sched-preempt` (below) |
| nit: wake mode | `WakeMode::{Normal, Interruptible}` on `wake_up_key` |
| nit: `CONFIG_PREEMPT_VOLUNTARY` declaration incomplete | `config.rs` now states `PREEMPT_DYNAMIC`/`PREEMPTION`/`PREEMPT_RCU` and that `NO_HZ_IDLE`'s idle tick stop is not modelled |
| nit: stack overflow recognised from CR2 at #DF | a guard-page CR2 now counts only with the faulting `rsp` on the same pool stack; `kstack-overflow` recurses with 6 KiB frames (larger than the 4 KiB guard) so that a build without per-page stack probes would jump the guard and fail the case |
| nit: `sleep_current` precondition | `debug_assert!` that interrupts are masked on entry |
| nit: stale `large_stack_arrays` reason | restated for the five single stacks and the pool |

`sched-preempt` (`test=sched-preempt -- signals`, exit 33): a kernel thread that is always runnable (it yields, with a one-instruction interrupt window so a sleeping `/init`'s timer still arrives) runs beside the `signals` smoke. The tick preempts `/init` at the return to ring 3 from the timer interrupt, `ktask::preempt_user` switches to the thread inside the user-interrupt handler, and `/init` is resumed each time; at `/init`'s death the kernel prints `CHECK sched-preempt ok kthread=ran user_return_preemption=interrupt init=resumed` only if the thread ran at least twice and at least two such preemptions really switched tasks, and the harness requires that exact record once together with `init: signals ok` and the normal success sequence.

### The seam for the task-model lane

The model lane (`feat/task-model`) owns `Process`/`Tasklist` and must not need to edit the switch. It plugs in at:

1. **A task is a `SchedId`.** Keep one per task in the model's task struct; never store the scheduling state. Create with `ktask::spawn_user(PtRegs)` (fork builds the child's frame from the parent's with `ax = 0`; the pool-exhausted `NoSlot` is fork's `-EAGAIN`, a named non-parity with `max_threads`). The caller of `ktask::start_init` today is `userspace::start`; `start_init` returns when init's task is gone and nothing else is runnable (never in a normal boot, once per group in the suite).
2. **Sleeping and waking.** `signal::Task::wait(key, deadline)` is the only blocking funnel; `ktask::sleep_current` implements it. Owners allocate a `WaitKey` class in `kernel::sched` (`CLASS_*`, one constant each) and call `ktask::wake_up_key(key, mode, nr_exclusive)` after changing the condition; `signal_wake_up(id)` belongs in `complete_signal`. Wakes take only the `RQ` lock, so they are legal under any other lock and in hardirq.
3. **Exit.** `ktask::exit_current()` is `do_task_dead`; it never returns, and the successor frees the slot.
4. **Per-task context that already switches**: user FP image, DS/ES/FS/GS selectors and bases, bad-iret depth, `TSS.RSP0`. **Not yet per-task, and the model lane's to add in `ktask::context_switch`**: the address space (`switch_mm`; today one `TABLES`), `CpuAccount` (`sched_in`/`sched_out`), `PROCESS`, the restart block.
5. **No guard may be alive across `schedule()` once a second user task exists.** Today `Task::wait` runs with `PROCESS` and `TABLES` held (module `services` documents why that is sound with one user task); the model lane must move the waits out from under them.
6. **Timers**: the queue is `TimerQueue<ktask::Timer>`; add the per-task `ITIMER_REAL` as a second `Timer` variant (capacity: one entry per owner kind per task, raise it in `ktask::init`).

### Shortcuts (each is also a `shortcut:` comment at its site)

* FIFO with a one-tick slice, no weights, no wake-up preemption, no EEVDF (S1 of the plan).
* A fixed pool of 128 stacks in bss (S3); the number follows no `max_threads`.
* Wait keys are scanned, O(tasks), and a looping waiter re-joins its key's queue on every sleep (non-exclusive at the head, exclusive at the tail) instead of keeping its entry, so a waiter woken spuriously loses its place among same-key peers (S5; the order of one wake-up is Linux's, see Wait channels).
* One CPU: lost-wakeup safety relies on IF=0 and on ring 0 not being preempted; `RQ`/`TIMERQ` take no barriers beyond the lock (S6). No RCU (S7).
* No RSB fill and no stack-protector canary switch in `switch_to_asm` (`X86_FEATURE_RSB_CTXSW`); upgrade with the mitigation lane.
* FS/GS bases are always rewritten on a switch-in of a ring-3 task (two `wrmsr`), not only when they differ.
* The tick keeps running in idle; no tickless idle.
* The `RQ` lock is taken on every return to ring 3 to read `need_resched`; a lock-free mirror is the upgrade.
* `schedule()` does not re-check `signal_pending_state` (Linux's `__schedule` does, for a signal that arrives between `set_current_state` and the call); with IF=0 there is no such window here.
* `Stop::Blocked` was untouched in this lane: a read that nothing can wake still stopped the kernel loudly. (Removed by [pipes, `poll` and job control](#pipes-poll-and-job-control): such a read now sleeps, as Linux does.)

### The five design questions

* *Can I use a preexisting crate?* The register and MSR access are the vendored `x86_64` and `raw-cpuid` crates already in use (`FsBase`, `GsBase`, segment registers). No scheduler, task or timer-wheel crate fits: the queue must be allocation-free after setup, generational and Linux-ordered, and the vendored `bitflags`/`slab`-style crates grow infallibly. `core::arch` supplies `FXSAVE`/`FXRSTOR`. Nothing was added to `vendor/`.
* *Can I bundle functionality together?* One `Rq` for states, keys, FIFO and wake-ups, one `TimerQueue` generic over the owner kind, one `ktask` for stacks, contexts and sleeping, one `restore_regs_and_iret` for both new return paths. The three naked sites share one proof format with `iret_site` and `syscall_entry`.
* *Can I simplify things?* Yes, and listed above: FIFO instead of EEVDF, key scan instead of wait entries, a static pool instead of a stack window, eager FP, no tickless idle. Each has an upgrade trigger in its `shortcut:` comment.
* *Is this secure?* Guard pages on every task stack (unmapped, checked at boot and by a real fault in `sched-switch`) plus an end marker; a stale `SchedId` never reaches a reused slot; the new asm has proof blocks and disassembly but **no independent review yet**; `fixup_bad_iret` still recognises a hostile `rt_sigreturn` frame by address and now accepts it on any pool stack; every wake is a state change under `RQ` only, so no lock-order cycle through the scheduler exists (`TIMERQ -> TIMER_HW`, `RQ` a leaf, never held together); the known gaps are no RSB fill, one CPU, and the guards held across `schedule()` by the single user task.
* *How can I improve this?* Move the waits out from under `PROCESS`/`TABLES`, add per-task address spaces and accounting at the switch (model lane), replace the key scan by wait entries and the FIFO by EEVDF once task counts or SMP require it, measure stack depth under the full oracle before fixing the stack size, and take an independent reviewer through the three asm sites.

### Evidence

Same workstation, user, toolchain installation and firmware as earlier records; QEMU only (TCG, plus the KVM cases on this machine); not independently attested.

* **Gate run `sched-g3`** (`target/gates.sh`, logs in `target/gates-sched-g3/`, tree `74f34cc`, rebased on `main` d3d8f94), every line `exit=0`: `fmt`; host 1.88 Clippy and tests (xtask 86, baremetal 10); nightly Clippy over the workspace; nightly tests (kernel library **434** tests, of which 20 are `sched::`, xtask 86, baremetal 10 and 2); `kernel-test` run `sched-g3`: **37/37 debug-exit cases matched** (the 36 of the first submission plus `sched-preempt`; every case of `main` including `oracle-ids` 158, `oracle-time` 119, `oracle-mm` 122, `oracle-fs` 286, `oracle-signal` 202 lines identical to the pinned Linux's) and **4/4 baremetal-suite cases matched** (`baremetal-suite`, `-two-displays` and `-kvm`: `RESULT: ALL PASS (5 groups, 23 self-tests)`, `SUITE complete ok=true groups=5/5`; `-stops`: `fault` init signal 11, `io_uring` kernel stop, `spin` timeout after 1017 ms, then `ids` 158/158 on the same boot); all four `-D warnings` target-Clippy profiles (`qemu-self-test`, `freestanding`, `freestanding,baremetal-suite`, `qemu-self-test,baremetal-suite`); `xtask check`; `xtask status`. A first run of this round, `sched-g2`, failed the four target-Clippy lines (`kernel_main` two lines over the line limit; `preempt_report` unused in the suite build) and was fixed by `74f34cc`; its logs are kept in `target/gates-sched-g2/`. The first submission's run `sched-g1` (tree `d11b64c`, on the stale base) is history: its two `+baremetal-suite` lines could not run there.
* `sched-switch` record (`target/kernel-tooling/sched-g3/cases/sched-switch/serial.log`): `CHECK sched-switch ok threads=7 switches=ok regs=callee-saved first_run_abi=aligned fpu=isolated order=fifo timers=ordered wake=keyed,signal,spurious stacks=recycled pool=exhausted,reused guards=unmapped guard_fault=exact`. `sched-preempt` (`.../sched-preempt/serial.log`): `init: signals ok`, then `CHECK sched-preempt ok kthread=ran user_return_preemption=interrupt init=resumed`, the init-death line and `RESULT success`. `kstack-overflow` (`.../kstack-overflow/serial.log`): `FAIL kernel stack overflow slot=0 vector=8 error=0x0 ... cr2=0xffffffff800b8ce8 rsp=0xffffffff800b8cf8`, QEMU exit 35, no `RESULT success`; the recursion now uses 6 KiB frames, so the per-page stack probes are what stops it at the guard.
* Mutation evidence (changed code in scratch worktrees, same harness and QEMU; logs and diffs in `target/evidence/sched/mutations/`): swapping the `r12`/`r13` pops of `switch_to_asm` fails only `sched-switch` (35/36 at the time); removing the next task's `fpu_restore` fails only `sched-switch` (35/36); **new in this round**, making `ktask::preempt_user` a no-op fails only `sched-preempt` (**36/37**; `FAIL sched-preempt hog_runs=277643 user_preemptions=0`, QEMU exit 35, the other 36 cases and the 4 suite cases still match). The cases therefore detect a register-restore, an FP-restore and a missing-preemption bug; they do not by themselves prove the switch correct for every register or interleaving.
* Disassembly of the asm sites: `target/evidence/sched/{freestanding,qemu-self-test}.asm-sites.txt` and the whole listings are from the exact ELFs of run `sched-001` (the first submission). For the ELFs of `sched-g3` (`target/evidence/sched/g3/{freestanding,qemu-self-test}.objdump.txt`) the instruction sequences of `switch_to_asm`, `syscall_entry`, `ret_from_fork_asm` and `restore_regs_and_iret` were compared with those after masking branch-target addresses: identical (16, 31, 8 and 17 instructions); only the call/jump targets moved. Read against the sources as before; no second `iretq` exists. The first-run call alignment is also observed at run time by the `first_run_abi=aligned` field.
* Not run / not done: no physical hardware; no KVM-specific scheduler case beyond the existing KVM cases (they matched in `sched-g3`); no independent review of the asm, the lock order or this fix round; no stack-depth measurement in this stage (taken in the [process lane closure](#process-lane-closure): 15,408 bytes deepest, in `fork`; the 6 KiB-frame overflow case shows the guard works); no SMP, so the lost-wake-up argument is the single-CPU one only; no performance measurement of the switch or of the idle path (the tick keeps running while idle); the suite's in-idle group end (a group that fails to load or start) and a timeout while the group's task sleeps are not exercised by any case; `sched-preempt` counts the interrupt-return preemption only, and no case makes the syscall-return variant switch.

### Integration with the task model

Rebased onto `main` after the task-model lane (PR #25) merged; the two seams that the lanes left open are connected, with no new oracle case.

* **`Waker` on the scheduler.** `userspace::SchedWaker` implements `task::exit::Waker`: `child_event(parent)` is `__wake_up_parent`, `ktask::wake_up_key(chldexit_key(parent), Interruptible, 1)` (`__wake_up_sync_key` on `signal_struct::wait_chldexit`, `TASK_INTERRUPTIBLE`, `nr_exclusive` 1, `kernel/signal.c` `__wake_up_parent`), and `signal_wake(task)` is `ktask::signal_wake_up` of the task's `SchedId`. `chldexit_key` is `WaitKey::new(CLASS_CHLDEXIT, parent slot)`; one thread per group, so the group is its task, and a reused slot costs only a spurious wake-up. Both take `RQ` and nothing else, as the DAG allows. shortcut: the `TaskId`-to-`SchedId` map is one entry (init, bound by `ktask::start_init`'s `bound` callback) because `fork` does not exist; store the `SchedId` in the task record with `copy_process`.
* **Blocking `wait4`/`waitid`.** `syscall::process::do_wait` calls `wait_event_interruptible(ctx, chldexit_key(current), ..)`, so it sleeps through `Task::wait` -> `ktask::sleep_current` on the same key `SchedWaker` wakes. Host evidence: `task::tests::child_exit_wakes_only_the_parents_wait_chldexit_sleepers` drives `exit_notify` with a `Waker` over a real `sched::Rq` (the parent sleeping on its key wakes, a sleeper on the child's key does not). The machine cannot yet exercise it end to end: no task other than init exists, so `wait4` returns `ECHILD` before sleeping.
* **Lock DAG** merged from both sides (`kernel/src/services.rs`, `docs/subsystems/process.md`): `TASKLIST -> VFS -> TABLES -> heap -> FRAMES`, `TASKLIST -> TIMERQ -> TIMER_HW`, `any -> RQ`, `RQ` and `TIMERQ` never held together, no guard across `schedule()` once a second user task exists. No cycle: `RQ` is a leaf that calls out of nothing, and `TIMERQ` drains before it wakes.
* **Routing**: 25 `in_progress` rows and 2,598 `not_started` of 2,623 (task model's 24 plus `ws-kernel-sched`).

## Process creation and exit

Lane `fork` of the process plan (`target/designs/process-lane.md`, WP5; branch `feat/process-fork`, on top of the task model and the scheduler core). `/init` can now create children: `fork`, and `clone`/`clone3` with only fork-equivalent flags, run end to end on the task list and the scheduler; a child has its own kernel stack, address space, descriptor table, `fs_struct` and signal state, runs, exits (a non-init task no longer stops the kernel), is reaped by `wait4`/`waitid`, and its orphans go to init. The lane's evidence is a new oracle group, `fork` (164 checks recorded on the pinned Linux 6.18.57 and identical on this kernel: 121 in the first version, 43 added by [fix round 1](#fix-round-1-after-the-independent-review)), the `forkleak` and `forkbomb` cases and the host tests below. It does **not** run the `proc` group: that group's pipe, stop/continue and `execve`-into-a-pipe rows need work owned by later lanes ([what the `proc` group does now](#what-the-proc-group-does-now)).
