### Behaviour, with Linux 6.18.57 citations

* **`fork`, `vfork`, `clone`, `clone3`** (`kernel/fork.c`: `SYSCALL_DEFINE0(fork)` 2691, `vfork` 2707, `clone` 2736, `copy_clone_args_from_user` 2756, `clone3_args_valid` 2853, `clone3` 2892, `kernel_clone` 2570, `copy_process` 1934): the legacy `clone` takes the low 32 bits of the flags and uses `CSIGNAL` as the exit signal; `clone3` rejects `size > PAGE_SIZE` (`E2BIG`) and `size < 64` (`EINVAL`), requires the bytes past the kernel's 88 to be zero (`E2BIG`, `EFAULT` first), validates `set_tid`, the exit signal (<= 64, the high half clear), `CLONE_INTO_CGROUP`, unknown flags, `CLONE_DETACHED`/low `CSIGNAL` bits (except `CLONE_NEWTIME`), `SIGHAND|CLEAR_SIGHAND`, `THREAD|PARENT` with an exit signal, and the stack pair (the child starts at `stack + stack_size`). `kernel_clone` then refuses `PIDFD` and `PARENT_SETTID` on one slot, and `copy_process` its flag relations in its order (`NEWNS|FS`, `NEWUSER|FS`, `THREAD` without `SIGHAND`, `SIGHAND` without `VM`, `PARENT` for global init, `THREAD` with `NEWUSER|NEWPID`, `PIDFD|DETACHED`); a signal pending before the fork restarts the call (`-ERESTARTNOINTR`). Only then does the kernel decide whether the request is the plain copy it implements; everything else (threads, shared tables, namespaces, `vfork`, `CLONE_SETTLS`, `CLONE_CHILD_SETTID`, `CLONE_PIDFD`, `set_tid`, cgroups, `CLONE_IO`, `CLONE_SYSVSEM`) is `Outcome::Unimplemented`, the loud stop of the whole kernel that every unimplemented syscall keeps (the policy is **unchanged** and a user decision about killing only the offending task is still open).
* **The copy** (`copy_process`): `dup_task_struct` comes first (the child's state block: `ENOMEM`), then the limits (`nr_threads >= max_threads`, `RLIMIT_NPROC` for non-root: `EAGAIN`; kernel/fork.c:2020, 2059, 2072), so `ENOMEM` beats `EAGAIN` as in Linux. Then, each step undone by the next one's failure: `copy_mm` (VMA model, then the pages), `copy_thread` (scheduler slot, kernel stack from the pool, first registers = the parent's with `ax` 0 and `sp` of a requested stack, the parent's live x87/SSE and segment state and the new CR3), `copy_files` (`dup_fd`: the same open descriptions, a table of its own, close-on-exec bits kept), `copy_fs` (root/cwd references and umask), the mapping pins and the executable reference, `copy_sighand`/`copy_signal` and `alloc_pid` + publication in `Tasklist::fork` (the child is visible to the rest of the system only here, `real_parent`, process group and session inherited, `PF_FORKNOEXEC`), `wake_up_new_task`. The parent's `rax` is the child's pid, the child's is 0; `comm`, credentials, rlimits, `umask`, the program break, the TLS bases and the FP state are inherited; pending signals, the interval timer, the restart block and the robust list are not (`init_sigpending`, `copy_signal`, `copy_process`' resets).
* **Address spaces** (`dup_mmap`): the model copies the VMAs (`Mm::dup`) and `Tables::fork_user` gives the child a new PML4 whose upper half is a copy of the parent's entries and a private frame for every present user page with the same protection; the shared zero page stays shared and a `PROT_NONE` page stays `PROT_NONE` (its frame is copied, the PTE keeps its inverted form). **Eager, not copy-on-write**, because COW needs per-frame reference counts, a write-protecting walk of the parent (with `invlpg`) and an exclusive-ownership test in the fault path (`mm/fault.rs` says "no fork, so the page is exclusive"); that is a separate mechanism with its own review (shortcut S2/T10), and the eager copy is observably the same.
* **Switching** (`switch_mm_irqs_off`): every task has its own CR3, loaded by `ktask::context_switch` when it differs; idle keeps whatever is loaded (`active_mm`). A task leaving the system loads the kernel-only tree (`services::TABLES`) before its own is freed, so no tree is freed while loaded.
* **`do_exit`** (`kernel/exit.c`): init's death keeps its panic path. Any other task, in Linux's order: `exit_mm` (kernel tree loaded, user pages and tables freed, the mapping pins and the executable reference dropped), `exit_files` (`close_files`, in descriptor order), `exit_fs`, the CPU times folded into the task (`cputime_adjust`, what `wait` reports), `exit_notify` (children handed to init in list order, `SIGCHLD` or the cloned exit signal to the parent with `CLD_*`, pid, uid, status and times, auto-reap for `SIG_IGN`/`SA_NOCLDWAIT`), then `do_task_dead`: the next task releases the slot and the stack. The task's state block is freed before that (a block left to the scope of a function that never returns would leak; found by the object check below).
* **Wake-ups**: `Tasklist` keeps each task's scheduling handle; `signal_wake` and `child_event` reach every task (`SchedWaker` is stateless). Fatal signals of a child are ordinary deaths (`PF_SIGNALED`, status = signal number, no core flag with `RLIMIT_CORE` 0): `show_signal_msg`/`show_signal` print under `unhandled_signal` (always for init, else default or ignore disposition and not already dying) with the faulting task's pid.
* **Accounting**: each task has a `CpuAccount` marked off the CPU at every switch, so a tick is charged only to the task it found running; `times`, `getrusage`-style readers and `wait4`'s `rusage` read the right account.

### Locking and contexts (the lock-order DAG changed)

`PROCESS` (the task list and the VFS: `TASKLIST` + `VFS`) is taken **on demand** by a system call and dropped by `Task::wait` before `sleep_current`, so no guard is alive across `schedule()`: the exception of the single-task kernel (sleeping with `PROCESS` and `TABLES` held) is gone. The tables and the memory model of a task are its own (`LOCALS`, no lock; a busy flag per slot turns a second simultaneous access into a loud stop). `services::TABLES` is only the kernel-only template tree. Order: `PROCESS -> heap -> FRAMES`, `TABLES -> FRAMES`, `any -> RQ`, `TIMERQ -> TIMER_HW`; the page-table copy holds `FRAMES` and allocates no heap. `docs/subsystems/process.md` and `kernel/src/services.rs` carry the new text and rows T10-T15.

### What the `proc` group does now

*Historical: this is the state at the end of the stage; the [pipes, `poll` and job control stage](#pipes-poll-and-job-control) made the whole group run and match (135 of 135).*

`-- proc` stops at its second check: `fork-returns-child-pid` matches the pinned Linux's line (a new pid > 1), then the parent reads the pipe its child writes and the kernel stops loudly (`FAIL syscall would block nr=0: cross-task wake-ups not implemented`), which is the plan's WP6 (pipes across tasks). The rows of `proc` whose logic does not need a pipe, a stop or a continue are covered by the `fork` group under other names: `wait4-*`, `exit-code-*`, `waitid-*` (the `pidfd` row included: every descriptor is `EBADF`), `killed-by-*` (including `sigpipe` as a plain `kill`), `sigchld-*` (except `stopped-info`), `orphan-*` (reported through exit statuses), and `execve` in a forked child (reported through the exit status; the pipe-based `execve-output` row is not). That is a statement about the checks' logic and about identical `fork` lines on the two kernels, **not** a claim that those `proc.*` rows match: they have not run.

### Shortcuts (each is a `shortcut:` comment or a row of the table in [process.md](subsystems/process.md#lock-order-and-non-parity-table-for-the-task-lane))

* Eager address-space copy (T10), a stack pool of 127 (T11), only fork-equivalent `clone`/`clone3` (T12), a descheduled task's `ITIMER_REAL` serviced when it next runs (T13), no cross-task pipe/console wake-ups (T14), only the two CPU times in `rusage` (T15).
* The `State`-level path of a signal sent to the caller itself (`kill(getpid())`) is kept next to the task-list path: both end in `State::send_signal`; the task-list path adds the permission check and the wake-up.
* An `ITIMER_REAL` armed by the previous task is cleared from the clockevent by the next task's return to user mode; the owner's expiry is re-read when it runs (T13).
* A group of the bare-metal suite ends as a whole, whichever task is current when it ends (init's exit, a kernel stop or the time bound hitting a child): `baremetal::group_ended` calls `ktask::abandon_group`, which marks every task of the group dead (`Rq::abandon`, host-tested), empties the run queue and switches to idle; the idle loop's condition is "init is gone or dead", so it returns to the suite at once and `finish_group` tears the process down. An orphan left behind by init's exit therefore does not run on (an earlier version let it run until it blocked, and a child current at a timeout left the suite hanging with no result for the group). Exercised on the machine by the `spinchild` and `orphan` probes of `baremetal-suite-stops` ([fix round 1](#fix-round-1-after-the-independent-review)).
* Not independently reviewed: the CR3 switch in `context_switch`, the slot table's `unsafe`, `Tables::fork_user`/`destroy_user`. Disassembly was not re-taken: no assembly changed.

### The five design questions

* *Can I use a preexisting crate?* No process, task or page-table crate fits a fallible, Linux-ordered `copy_process`; the one crate-shaped input is the vendored `x86_64` (CR3 load through the existing `activate_tables`) and `bitflags`-style flag types are not needed (plain constants, as the neighbouring ABI code). Nothing was added to `vendor/`.
* *Can I bundle functionality together?* One `Syscall::fork` for `fork`/`clone`/`clone3` (the decoders differ, the transaction does not); one `dup_fd`/`dup_fs`/`pin_clone` family next to the single-process versions in the VFS; the existing `ret_from_fork_asm` and `prepare` for the child's first run; one `current_local`/`install_local` slot table for init and every child.
* *Can I simplify things?* Yes, and named: eager copy instead of COW, a pool instead of a stack window, fork-equivalent flags only. Each has a trigger in its `shortcut:` comment or table row.
* *Is this secure?* Every allocation is fallible and unwound in reverse (`forkbomb` drives the pool-exhaustion failure path twice, after the address space, descriptors, pins and `fs_struct` were built, and checks the tables, files and RAM after both floods); the kernel half of a new tree is the published one (immutable), so a task can never see another's pages; a tree is freed only after CR3 left it; user pointers go through the one usercopy path (`clone3` struct and `set_tid` array included); the credential snapshot is published for the child at `fork`; an exit signal above `_NSIG` is never sent; the unsupported flags stop instead of returning a success.
* *How can I improve this?* COW fork with per-frame counts; a stack window; per-task `ITIMER_REAL` entries in the timer queue; the cross-task pipe wake-ups (WP6) so that the `proc` group can run; counted shared slots for `CLONE_VM/FILES/SIGHAND/THREAD`; an independent review of the new CR3 and slot-table `unsafe`.

### Evidence

Same workstation, user, toolchain installation and firmware as earlier records; QEMU only (TCG, plus the KVM cases on this machine); not independently attested.

* **Gate run `pf-g2`** (`target/gates.sh`, logs in `target/gates-pf-g2/`, branch `feat/process-fork` at `0ac577e`, on `main` 3fe187e), every line `exit=0`: `cargo fmt --all --check`; host 1.88 Clippy and tests (xtask 88, baremetal 10); `cargo +nightly clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`; `cargo +nightly test --workspace` (kernel library **495** tests, xtask 88, baremetal 10, two doc tests); `xtask kernel-test` run `pf-g2`: **40/40 debug-exit cases matched** (the 37 of `main` unchanged, `oracle-ids` 158, `oracle-time` 119, `oracle-mm` 122, `oracle-fs` 286 and `oracle-signal` 202 lines identical to the pinned Linux's, plus `oracle-fork` 122, `forkleak` and `forkbomb`) and **4/4 baremetal-suite cases matched** (`RESULT: ALL PASS (6 groups, 23 self-tests)`, `SUITE complete ok=true groups=6/6 proofs=23/23`; the `fork` group `matched=122 expected=122 observed=122 mismatches=0` under TCG and under KVM); target Clippy of all four kernel profiles (`qemu-self-test`, `freestanding` and both with `baremetal-suite`); `xtask check` and `status` (26 `in_progress`, 2,597 `not_started`). The harness-built `/init` of that run (sha256 `317b65cf...e94b35e`) is byte-identical to the one the reference Linux ran. Two earlier gate runs failed for reasons fixed before it: `pf-g1` (Clippy `match_same_arms` in a new `xtask` arm; its `kernel-test` step was also cut short because `xtask` was edited while it ran) and `pf-t3` (a plain `kernel-test`: `oracle-fork` rejected because the `execve` check's child printed init's banner to the console; the output now goes to a pipe).
* **`fork` group on the pinned Linux 6.18.57** (`target/linux-reference/init-reference-009/`, harness-built `/init` and initramfs of the `pf-t4` run, QEMU q35 TCG `-cpu qemu64 -m 512M`): 122 lines (121 checks and `init: tests fork passed=121 failed=0`), recorded as the `fork` rows of `xtask/oracle/expected.tsv` and equal one for one (`diff`) in a second run; the candidate prints the same 122 lines in `oracle-fork` (and in the bare-metal suite's `fork` group: `matched=122 expected=122 mismatches=0`). A first version of the group had `clone-exit-signal-is-sent` failing on this kernel (the cloned child's exit signal carried `si_signo` 17, so the queued entry never matched the signal): fixed in `do_notify_parent` (`info.si_signo = sig`) with a host test, and no other check differed on the first run (120/121, then 121/121).
* `forkleak` (`kernel-test` case; `target/kernel-tooling/pf-g2/cases/forkleak/serial.log`): `init: forkleak warm=true rounds=200 reaped=200 none_left=true` (the same line as on the reference), `free_ram before=453292032 after=453292032`, and the self-test kernel's `CHECK fork-objects forks=202 heap=484795/484795 files=2/2 file_refs=5/5 pins=1/1 root_refs=5/5 slots=1/1 threads=1/1`. The first version of this check found a real leak: `do_exit` moved the fields out of the task-state box and then never returned, so the box itself (767 bytes) leaked on every fork (`heap=486347/639771` with the free-RAM line unchanged, because the heap grows inside frames it already holds); the pid number table (`heap` minus its capacity is compared, since the table legitimately grows with the highest pid until the cursor wraps) was the second movement the check explained.
* `forkbomb` (`kernel-test` case): `first children=127 eagain=true reaped=127`, `second children=127 eagain=true reaped=127 again=true`, `free_ram after_first=453357568 after_second=453357568` (the stack pool holds 127 children beside init).
* Mutation evidence (changed code in scratch edits of the tree, same harness inputs and QEMU, manual runs with the case's kernel command line; serial logs archived in `target/evidence-archive/proc-fork/mutations/`): removing `put_fs` at exit fails the object check (`root_refs=9/409`), removing `destroy_tables` at exit fails the free-RAM comparison (`before=452481024 after=364826624`), removing `unpin_all` fails it (`pins=3/203`), removing the `fput` of the executable (`file_refs=7/207`) or `close_files` (`file_refs=11/611`) fails it, skipping the page copy of `fork_user` fails 53 of the 121 `fork` checks (a child with zero-filled pages dies of `SIGSEGV`: status 11), and not zeroing the child's `rax` fails 62 (every child continues as the parent). The cases therefore detect a leaked directory, executable, descriptor, pin, frame or heap block and a wrong child; they do not prove the copy correct for every address-space shape.
* `-- proc` on this kernel: line 1 `init: test proc.fork-returns-child-pid ok` (equal to the pinned Linux's row), then `FAIL syscall would block nr=0: cross-task wake-ups not implemented` (the parent reads the pipe its child writes). No other `proc` row has run.
* Not run / not done: no physical hardware; no independent review (the CR3 load in `context_switch`, the slot table `unsafe`, `fork_user`/`destroy_user`); no memory-exhaustion injection into `fork` (the `ENOMEM` unwinding paths are not exercised; only the `EAGAIN` of the stack pool is, by `forkbomb`); no SMP; no copy-on-write; no measurement of fork latency or memory (the eager copy of init's roughly 90 pages is not timed); no KVM case of `forkbomb`/`forkleak` (the `fork` group ran under KVM in `baremetal-suite-kvm`); the `Outcome::Unimplemented` policy (a whole-kernel stop even for a child) is unchanged and awaits the user's decision.

### Fix round 1 (after the independent review)

Ten findings (three should-fix: the missing exec-id semantics, the evidence gaps and the suite group end; seven nits, two of which are duplicates of others). Each was fixed at the root or refuted with the pinned source; the commits are `kernel: clone exit signal falls back to SIGCHLD after an exec, ...`, `user/init, xtask, kernel: fork oracle rows for the untested paths; a suite group ends as a whole` and `kernel: two more comments ...`.

| Finding | Disposition |
| --- | --- |
| `do_notify_parent` omitted the `parent_exec_id` check | **Implemented, not a non-parity row.** `TaskStruct::self_exec_id` (copied at fork, `wrapping_add(1)` in `Tasklist::exec_committed`, fs/exec.c:1274) and `parent_exec_id` (the parent's `self_exec_id` at fork, fork.c:2341); a clone exit signal other than `SIGCHLD` becomes `SIGCHLD` (and so does `si_signo`) once they differ (signal.c:2273-2279). Host test `a_parent_that_exec_d_gets_sigchld_instead_of_the_clone_exit_signal`; oracle row `fork.exec-resets-the-clone-exit-signal` (a parent clones with exit signal `SIGUSR1`, `execve`s `/init execsig <child>`, which releases the child and exits with the number of the signal its death raised: `SIGCHLD`; the same on the pinned Linux). |
| Newly wired paths had no differential row | **43 rows added to the `fork` group** (121 -> 164 checks, re-recorded on the pinned Linux, [`init-reference-010`](#evidence-fix-round-1)): `kill(-pgid)` (members killed, another group spared, a reaped and a missing group `ESRCH`), `kill(0)` (the sender's group, the sender included), `kill(-1)` (everyone but init and the sender), `tkill` (success, `tid` 0 `EINVAL`, missing `ESRCH`), `rt_sigqueueinfo`/`rt_tgsigqueueinfo` to another task (the `siginfo` arrives as sent: `si_code`, `si_pid`, `si_uid`, `si_int`; wrong group `ESRCH`, `tid` 0 `EINVAL`, `si_code` 0 and `SI_TKILL` `EPERM` before the lookup), `CLONE_CLEAR_SIGHAND`, `CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID` (accepted, pointer unchecked, nothing written for a private mm; `clone` and `clone3`, a faulting pointer too), the child's stack pointer for `clone` (new top; 0 keeps the parent's) and `clone3` (`stack + stack_size`), both through an `asm!` probe (`stack_probe`) that compares `rsp` in the child before it touches the stack, `clone3` sizes 64 and 96 (zero tail), and the inheritance of the alternate signal stack (finding 4), `RLIMIT_NOFILE`, the command name and the credentials, each with the child's change staying its own. **The new rows found a real bug**: `CLONE_CLEAR_SIGHAND` is `flush_signal_handlers(tsk, 0)` (kernel/fork.c:1628), which keeps `SIG_IGN`, where the first version reset every disposition (`force_default = 1`, the author's misreading, also in a comment of `fork.rs`); `SigHand::flush` is now the one routine `execve` and `CLONE_CLEAR_SIGHAND` share, with a host test. |
| `EAGAIN` before `ENOMEM` | **Reordered.** `Syscall::fork` allocates the child's state block (`dup_task_struct`, fork.c:2020) before `Tasklist::fork_allowed` (fork.c:2059, 2072); the comment and the "copy" bullet above say so. Not observable without allocation-failure injection (none runs). |
| Altstack inheritance unchecked | `fork.fork-sigaltstack-copied` and `fork-sigaltstack-is-private` (child sees the parent's `{sp, flags, size}`, disables its own, the parent keeps its). |
| `Mm::dup` copied `def_flags` | Now `VmFlags::NONE`: `VM_INIT_DEF_MASK` is `VM_NOHUGEPAGE` (mm.h:499), which is `VM_NONE` without THP, so `VM_LOCKED` from `mlockall(MCL_FUTURE)` is dropped like the per-VMA flag (fork.c:1071). The two review entries for it are one fix; host test extended. |
| A suite group that ends in a child, or leaves a spinning orphan | **Fixed.** `baremetal::group_ended` called `exit_current()` on whichever task was current. Now `ktask::abandon_group` (`Rq::abandon`: every task of the group dead, the queue empty, so the next `schedule()` is idle and no wake-up resurrects a task) and the idle loop's condition is "init gone or dead", checked after the queue is empty. Host test `abandon_kills_every_task_and_the_next_schedule_is_idle`. On the machine: the `spinchild` probe (init sleeps in `wait4`, its child spins; the time bound finds the child on the CPU) and the `orphan` probe (init exits, the child spins on) joined `baremetal-suite-stops`, which requires cause `timeout` for the first, cause `init exit 0` for the second, and the `ids` group after both to pass; `suite::validate` checks the scenario lines. The group's orphan no longer runs on after init's exit (the earlier documented "orphan runs until it blocks" is gone). |
| Stale comments (`TABLES` lock model, two entries; `Threads` Sync argument; `clear_child_tid`) | `paging.rs` (module contract, the fork copy's SAFETY, `UserSpace::parts`, the `map_user` entry write, the `UserPages` impl), `usercopy.rs` (TOCTOU argument now rests on the private per-task tree), `kalloc.rs` and `vfs/mod.rs` (order `PROCESS -> heap -> FRAMES`); `ktask::Threads` names `set_current_cr3` as the third accessor; `Attributes::clear_child_tid` explains why exit need not process it (`mm_release` writes only when `mm_users > 1`, unobservable until `CLONE_VM`). |
| `kernel-tooling.md` omitted `file_refs`, `threads` | Fixed (the field list now matches `leak::report` and `forkleak_objects_unchanged`). |

#### Evidence (fix round 1)

Same workstation, user, toolchain installation and firmware as above; QEMU only; not independently attested.

* **Gate run `pf-g4`** (`target/gates.sh`, logs in `target/gates-pf-g4/`, archived under `target/evidence-archive/proc-fork/fix1/pf-g4/`; branch `feat/process-fork` at the third fix-round commit, on `main` 3fe187e), every line `exit=0`: `cargo fmt --all --check`; host 1.88 Clippy and tests (xtask 89, baremetal 10); `cargo +nightly clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`; `cargo +nightly test --workspace` (kernel library **498** tests, xtask 89, baremetal 10, two doc tests); `xtask kernel-test` run `pf-g4`: **40/40 debug-exit cases matched** (`oracle-fork` now 165 lines identical to the pinned Linux's; every other case as in `pf-g2`) and **4/4 baremetal-suite cases matched** (`RESULT: ALL PASS (6 groups, 23 self-tests)`; the `fork` group `matched=165 expected=165 observed=165 mismatches=0` under TCG and under KVM; `baremetal-suite-stops` ran six groups: `spinchild` `cause="timeout"` (1,012 ms), `orphan` `cause="init exit 0"` (95 ms), `ids` `matched=158` after both); target Clippy of all four kernel profiles; `xtask check` (56 Markdown files, 1,835 local links) and `status` (26 `in_progress`, 2,597 `not_started`: unchanged). Earlier attempt `pf-g3` was stopped by the author before the kernel build finished because two more stale comments were found; nothing from it is kept.
* **Reference** (`target/linux-reference/init-reference-010/`, README and `SHA256SUMS` there; the harness-built `/init` and initramfs of `pf-g4`, QEMU q35 TCG `-cpu qemu64 -m 512M`): `-- fork` prints 165 lines equal one for one, in order, to the `fork` rows of `xtask/oracle/expected.tsv` (checked by script), twice; the 121 earlier rows are a subsequence with identical text. `forkleak` prints the same counts line as before.
* `forkleak` / `forkbomb` (`pf-g4`): `CHECK fork-objects forks=202 heap=517627/517627 files=2/2 file_refs=5/5 pins=1/1 root_refs=5/5 slots=1/1 threads=1/1`, `free_ram before=453296128 after=453296128`; `forkbomb` `first children=127 eagain=true reaped=127`, `second ... again=true`, free RAM equal after both floods.
* **Mutation run `mut3`** (scratch worktree at the fix-round HEAD with three edits at once, `kernel-test` with the same harness; logs in `target/evidence-archive/proc-fork/fix1/mut3/`): (1) the old group end (`exit_current()` and `state(init).is_none()`), (2) the exec-id check disabled, (3) `CLONE_CLEAR_SIGHAND` resetting `SIG_IGN`. Results, attributable per case/row: `oracle-fork` fails exactly two rows, `clone3-clear-sighand` (`got=(Ok(97), 256) want=(Ok(97), 768)`) and `exec-resets-the-clone-exit-signal` (`got=(Ok(106), 2560)`, i.e. `SIGUSR1`, `want` 17 << 8), `passed=162 failed=2`; `baremetal-suite-stops` records the `spinchild` result and then **hangs until the harness timeout** ("QEMU timed out; timeout is never a pass"), the defect the review predicted; the three other suite cases exit 35 because their `fork` group has the two failing rows. The new rows and probes therefore detect each defect; `mut3` is not part of any maintained gate.
* Not run / not done: nothing here is independently reviewed; no allocation-failure injection into `fork` (the `ENOMEM`-before-`EAGAIN` order is by reading the source); `CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID`'s exit-time write is unobservable for a private mm and therefore only its acceptance is compared; `kill(-1)` on the reference also reaches kernel threads (they ignore signals); the `Outcome::Unimplemented` policy and the physical/SMP/COW items of the first record are unchanged.

## Pipes, poll and job control

Lane `fork`, stage WP6 + WP7 of the process plan (`target/designs/process-lane.md`; branch `feat/process-fork`, on top of the [process creation and exit](#process-creation-and-exit) stage). Pipes now work between tasks, `poll` exists, and the stop signals stop a process group: the whole `proc` oracle group (135 checks) runs on this kernel and matches the lines the pinned Linux 6.18.57 printed, and two new groups compare behaviour the 135 rows do not reach: `jobctl` (70 checks) and `pipe` (38 checks); the `block` probe of the bare-metal suite covers the sleep that nothing can end. All of them are in `oracle_case!` entries of `kernel-test` and in the [bare-metal suite](bare-metal-suite.md), which now runs nine groups.

### What exists

| Piece | Where | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Pipe reads and writes that sleep and wake across tasks, `anon_pipe_read`/`anon_pipe_write` order, partial progress kept over sleeps, `PIPE_BUF` atomicity, `SIGPIPE`/`EPIPE`, EOF with the last writer, `-ERESTARTSYS` on a signal | [`kernel/src/syscall/fd.rs`](../kernel/src/syscall/fd.rs) (`pipe_read`, `pipe_write`, `pipe_readable`, `pipe_writable`) | the wait is `wait_event_interruptible` on `sched::PIPE_KEY`; the console read sleeps until a signal |
| Pipe change counter and the wake-up it triggers, `F_SETPIPE_SZ` (`round_pipe_size`, `pipe_resize_ring`), `pipe(2)` | [`kernel/src/vfs/ops.rs`](../kernel/src/vfs/ops.rs) (`pipe_mut`, `take_pipe_events`), [`pipe.rs`](../kernel/src/vfs/pipe.rs), [`userspace.rs`](../kernel/src/userspace.rs) (`wake_pipe_sleepers`) | the pure VFS counts, the owner of the scheduler wakes |
| `poll(2)`: `do_sys_poll`, `do_poll`, `do_pollfd`, `pipe_poll`, `n_tty_poll`, `DEFAULT_POLLMASK`, `poll_select_set_timeout`, the slack of `select_estimate_accuracy`, `do_restart_poll` | [`kernel/src/syscall/poll.rs`](../kernel/src/syscall/poll.rs), `RestartFn::Poll` in [`time.rs`](../kernel/src/syscall/time.rs) | `-ERESTARTNOHAND` becomes `-ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK` with `ufds`, `nfds` and the absolute end in the restart block |
| `TASK_STOPPED` and its wake-ups (`wake_stopped` for `SIGCONT`, `signal_wake_up` with `resume` for a fatal signal), `stop_current` | [`kernel/src/sched/mod.rs`](../kernel/src/sched/mod.rs), [`ktask/mod.rs`](../kernel/src/ktask/mod.rs) | no key, no timer and no `TASK_NORMAL` wake-up reaches a stopped task |
| Group stop and continue: `task_set_jobctl_pending`, `task_clear_jobctl_pending`, `task_participate_group_stop`, `do_signal_stop`, the job-control half of `prepare_signal`, `wants_signal`, the `JOBCTL_STOP_DEQUEUED` marker, the `SIGNAL_CLD_MASK` step of `get_signal` | [`kernel/src/signal/jobctl.rs`](../kernel/src/signal/jobctl.rs), [`state.rs`](../kernel/src/signal/state.rs) | `forbid(unsafe_code)`, host-tested; `ThreadSignal::jobctl` and `SignalStruct::group_stop_count` are new |
| `Tasklist::signal_stop` (the stop plus `do_notify_parent_cldstop`), `Tasklist::notify_job_control`, `deliver` waking the stopped task of a `SIGCONT` and resuming it for a fatal signal, `Waker::wake_stopped` | [`kernel/src/task/jobctl.rs`](../kernel/src/task/jobctl.rs), [`kill.rs`](../kernel/src/task/kill.rs), [`exit.rs`](../kernel/src/task/exit.rs) | `TaskSignals` views carry `exiting` and `curr` (`PF_EXITING`, `task_curr`) for `wants_signal` |
| The stop on the machine: the orphaned-group rule for `SIGTSTP`/`SIGTTIN`/`SIGTTOU`, `do_signal_stop`, the sleep, the restart rules after the stop | [`kernel/src/userspace.rs`](../kernel/src/userspace.rs) (`exit_to_user`) | no new `unsafe`, no new assembly |
| `jobctl` and `pipe` oracle groups, the `block` probe | [`user/init/src/tests/jobctl.rs`](../user/init/src/tests/jobctl.rs), [`pipe.rs`](../user/init/src/tests/pipe.rs), `xtask/oracle/expected.tsv`, `xtask/src/kernel.rs`, `xtask/src/kernel/suite.rs` | recorded on the pinned Linux, see Evidence |

### Behaviour, with Linux 6.18.57 citations

* **`anon_pipe_read`** (`fs/pipe.c:269`): copy buffer by buffer; a read that got something returns it (`if (ret) break`), one that found nothing and has no writer returns 0 (EOF), a non-blocking one `EAGAIN`, else it sleeps on `rd_wait` (`wait_event_interruptible_exclusive`, `-ERESTARTSYS` when interrupted: nothing was read, so the restart repeats nothing). **`anon_pipe_write`** (431): `EPIPE` plus `SIGPIPE` (`send_sig`) when no reader is left, a head merged into the newest buffer only if it fits (so a write of at most `PIPE_BUF` fits one slot whole or waits for one), one page per free slot, then `EAGAIN` (non-blocking), `-ERESTARTSYS` when a signal is pending and nothing was written (a partial write returns its count), else the sleep on `wr_wait`; a reader that leaves wakes the sleeper, which sends `SIGPIPE` and returns what it wrote or `EPIPE`. **`pipe_poll`** (660): the read end is `POLLIN|POLLRDNORM` with data and `POLLHUP` without writers (an anonymous pipe: `f_pipe` never equals `w_counter`), the write end `POLLOUT|POLLWRNORM` with room and `POLLERR` without readers. **`pipe_release`** wakes both queues; **`pipe_resize_ring`** (1291) fails with `EBUSY` below the occupancy and wakes `wr_wait`; `round_pipe_size` (1272) is one page minimum, a power of two, `EINVAL` above 2^31.
* **`poll`** (`fs/select.c`): `nfds` above `RLIMIT_NOFILE` is `EINVAL` before anything is read (`do_sys_poll` 967), the array is copied in page-sized chunks (a fault is `EFAULT` before anything is polled), `do_pollfd` (853) answers `POLLNVAL` for a descriptor that is not open (an `O_PATH` one included), skips a negative one, and keeps `mask & (events | POLLERR | POLLHUP)`; `do_poll` (876) loops: scan, and with nothing ready and a signal pending `-ERESTARTNOHAND` (even for a zero timeout), else sleep until the end (`poll_schedule_timeout`: only the timer running out counts as a timeout, any other wake-up scans again); a zero timeout (`poll_select_set_timeout` 271) never waits. The timer fires at the end plus the slack of `select_estimate_accuracy` (a thousandth of the time left, 50 us to 100 ms). `revents` is stored for every entry whatever the result (`unsafe_put_user`, so a fault part-way leaves the earlier ones and answers `EFAULT`). `sys_poll` (1062) turns `-ERESTARTNOHAND` into `-ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK` with the array, the count and the absolute end (-1 for none); `restart_syscall` (`do_restart_poll` 1041) polls on to the original end.
* **Group stop** (`kernel/signal.c`): dequeuing a stop signal sets `JOBCTL_STOP_DEQUEUED` (`dequeue_signal` 642-690). `get_signal` (2885) runs the default action: `SIGSTOP` always stops; `SIGTSTP`/`SIGTTIN`/`SIGTTOU` do nothing in an orphaned process group (`is_current_pgrp_orphaned`, `kernel/exit.c:386`), the dequeued signal is simply dropped. `do_signal_stop` (2636) refuses unless the marker is still set (a `SIGCONT` or `SIGKILL` since cleared it), starts the group stop (`group_exit_code` = the signal unless a stop is already in effect), `task_participate_group_stop` (361) sets `SIGNAL_STOP_STOPPED` (replacing the other stop bits: the unreported continue goes) and tells whether the parent hears of it, the task becomes `TASK_STOPPED`, the parent gets `CLD_STOPPED` (`do_notify_parent_cldstop` 2365: `SIGCHLD` unless ignored or `SA_NOCLDSTOP`, the `wait` sleepers always), and `schedule()`; a fatal signal already pending keeps the task running (`signal_pending_state`). **`prepare_signal`** (895): at generation, whatever happens to the signal, a stop signal discards a pending `SIGCONT`, and `SIGCONT` discards the stop signals, clears `JOBCTL_STOP_PENDING` with the dequeued marker, ends `JOBCTL_STOPPED` (the sender wakes the task: `wake_up_state(__TASK_STOPPED)`), and if a stop was in effect sets `SIGNAL_CLD_CONTINUED | SIGNAL_STOP_CONTINUED` and clears the stop code (so an unreported stop is not reported after the continue); the woken task tells its parent `CLD_CONTINUED` at the top of `get_signal` (2913). **`wants_signal`** (970): a signal makes a task take the group down only if it is not blocked, not exiting, not stopped (`SIGKILL` always) and either running or with nothing pending, so a terminating signal waits for a stopped task until `SIGCONT`; `SIGKILL` and a group exit wake it (`signal_wake_up` with `TASK_WAKEKILL`). `wait4`/`waitid` report a stop once while `SIGNAL_STOP_STOPPED` is set (`task_stopped_code`, `kernel/exit.c:1306`: the task's own state is not consulted) and a continue once while `SIGNAL_STOP_CONTINUED` is.
* **Orphaned groups** (`kernel/exit.c`): the existing port of `will_become_orphaned_pgrp`/`kill_orphaned_pgrp` (361-431) now acts on a real stopped member: `SIGHUP` is queued (the stopped task does not take it), `SIGCONT` wakes the task, which then dequeues the `SIGHUP`. Global init is never a connection to the outside, so a process group whose members are all children of init, or whose parents are inside it, is orphaned from the start: the terminal stop signals do nothing for such a group (the tests of the `jobctl` group run the stop checks in a middle process whose children lead groups of their own for that reason).
* **After the stop** the task is back in `get_signal`'s loop in Linux; here `exit_to_user` loops again with `after_stop` set, so `arch_do_signal_or_restart`'s restart rules still run for a system call that was interrupted before the stop (an `ERESTARTSYS` read, a `poll` with a restart block) even though no signal is pending any more. A first version without it returned `-512` to the program (found by the `jobctl` group: `kill-group-continues-every-member`, 2 of 70 failing).

### Locking and contexts

No lock-order edge is added. A pipe change is noted by `Vfs::pipe_mut`/`pipe_release` (`PROCESS` held) and turned into `ktask::wake_up_key(PIPE_KEY, Interruptible, 0)` by `wake_pipe_sleepers` (`PROCESS -> RQ`, legal): before a task sleeps (`Syscall::wait`, so what a half-finished write put in the pipe reaches the reader that waits for it), when a system call ends, and when a task exits (its descriptors close). `Rq::stop_current` and `ktask::stop_current` run in task context with IF=0 and no guard held; `Tasklist::signal_stop` and `deliver` run under `PROCESS` and call `RQ` only for the wake-ups. Nothing sleeps with a guard.

### Shortcuts (each is a `shortcut:` comment or a row of the [table](subsystems/process.md#lock-order-and-non-parity-table-for-the-task-lane))

* One wait key for all pipes and a wake-all (T14); `poll` only, the console never readable (T16); `F_SETPIPE_SZ` for root only (T17); a sleep nothing can end sleeps forever, ended in the suite by its time bound (T18).
* One thread per group: `group_stop_count` is 0 or 1, the pending-stop step at the top of `get_signal` (`JOBCTL_STOP_PENDING` with several threads) and `task_join_group_stop` do not exist; no ptrace stop, no cgroup freezer. `TASK_STOPPED` and `JOBCTL_STOPPED` change together (T8).
* No deadlock report in the idle loop: all tasks asleep with no timer armed halts the CPU, as Linux would.

### The five design questions

* *Can I use a preexisting crate?* No: a pipe wait loop, `poll` and job control are Linux-ordered kernel state machines over this kernel's task list and scheduler; no crate models them, and none was added to `vendor/`.
* *Can I bundle functionality together?* The sleeps of pipe, `poll` and the console are the one `wait_event_interruptible` of the signal lane over one key; the stop and continue effects are one `signal::jobctl` shared by the send side (`prepare_signal`), the receive side (`get_signal`) and `wait`; `poll`'s timeout and restart use the nanosleep lane's restart block.
* *Can I simplify things?* Yes, and named above: one key and wake-all, `poll` alone, root-only pipe resizing, one thread per group. Each has an upgrade path in its row.
* *Is this secure?* A user pointer is read and written only through the one usercopy path (`copy_from_user`, `put_user`, `access_ok`); the `pollfd` array is allocated fallibly in page-sized chunks after the `RLIMIT_NOFILE` check, so a hostile `nfds` costs at most that many entries and a failure is `ENOMEM`; no `unsafe`; no lock is held while a task sleeps; a stop cannot be forced on init (`SIGNAL_UNKILLABLE`) and a terminating signal is never lost by a stopped task; the credential rules of `kill` are unchanged. A sleeping task can still be woken only by a key, a signal or its timer, never by a stale id (`SchedId` generations). The nine suite groups and the oracle compare behaviour, not security.
* *How can I improve this?* A key per pipe end with exclusive waiters; `ppoll`/`select`/`epoll` in front of `do_sys_poll`; a deadlock report; the multi-thread group stop; `ptrace` stops; a real tty (`SIGTTIN`/`SIGTTOU` for background I/O, `TIOCSCTTY`, session-leader hangup); the per-user pipe accounting.

### Evidence

Same workstation, user, toolchain installation and firmware as earlier records; QEMU only (TCG, plus the KVM cases on this machine); not independently attested.

* **Gate run `pj-g1`** (`target/gates.sh`, logs in `target/gates-pj-g1/`, archived under `target/evidence-archive/proc-fork/pipes-jobctl/`, branch `feat/process-fork` at `e64326d` (the commits after it change documentation only), on `main` 3fe187e), every line `exit=0`: `cargo fmt --all --check`; host 1.88 Clippy and tests (xtask 89, baremetal 10); `cargo +nightly clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`; `cargo +nightly test --workspace` (kernel library **524** tests, 26 more than before the stage; xtask 89, baremetal 10, two doc tests); `xtask kernel-test` run `pj-g1`: **43/43 debug-exit cases matched** (the 40 of the previous record unchanged, plus `oracle-proc` 136 lines, `oracle-jobctl` 71 and `oracle-pipe` 39, each identical to the rows of `xtask/oracle/expected.tsv`) and **4/4 baremetal-suite cases matched** (`RESULT: ALL PASS (9 groups, 23 self-tests)` under TCG, under KVM and with two displays; `proc` `136/136`, `jobctl` `71/71`, `pipe` `39/39`, `fork` `165/165`; `baremetal-suite-stops` ran `fault`, `io_uring`, `spin`, `spinchild`, `orphan`, the new `block` (`cause="timeout"`, 1,012 ms: init asleep on an empty pipe, nothing to wake it) and `ids` `158/158` after them); target Clippy of all four kernel profiles; `xtask check` and `status` (26 `in_progress`, 2,597 `not_started`: unchanged, no new routed owner). `forkleak` and `forkbomb` are unchanged (`free_ram before=452608000 after=452608000`).
* **First run of `-- proc` on this kernel** (dev build, before any of the stage's tests existed): 135 of 135 checks passed with the pipe wait loops, `poll` and the job-control stop in place, after one missing syscall (`pipe(2)`, row `pipe-legacy`) was added. Earlier in the stage the group stopped at `FAIL signal stop unimplemented sig=19` (the `wait4-stopped` row) and, before that, at the first cross-task pipe read.
* **Reference** (`target/linux-reference/init-reference-011/`, README and `SHA256SUMS` there; the harness-built `/init` and initramfs of `pj-g1`, QEMU q35 TCG `-cpu qemu64 -m 512M`): `-- proc` prints 136 lines, `-- jobctl` 71 and `-- pipe` 39, each equal one for one, in order, to the group's rows of `xtask/oracle/expected.tsv` (checked by script), each run twice with the same result. The `jobctl` and `pipe` rows exist because the 135 `proc` rows do not reach: terminal stop signals in orphaned groups, `SIGHUP` + `SIGCONT` for a group orphaned with a stopped member, `SA_NOCLDSTOP`, `WNOWAIT` on stops, a continue that cancels an unreported stop, a stopped child that terminating signals wait for, `kill` to a stopped group, `setpgid` across sessions and after `execve`; and blocking reads and writes with partial progress, `SIGPIPE`/`EPIPE` for sleeping writers, `PIPE_BUF` atomicity under contention, interrupted and restarted calls, `poll` events, timeouts, wake-ups and its restart after a stop. A first version of the pipe group passed on both kernels at once; the checks that were added later were each added because a mutation of the kernel (below) survived the group, never because the reference disagreed.
* **Mutation runs** (a one-edit change of the kernel source, the dev build, one group per run, logs in `target/evidence-archive/proc-fork/pipes-jobctl/evidence-mut2/`; none is part of any gate): (1) no restart rules after a stop: `jobctl` 68 of 70 (`kill-group-continues-every-member`, `group-members-exit-after-continue`; the program saw `-512`); (2) `SIGCONT` does not wake the stopped task: `proc` hangs at `waitid-continued-then-exited` until the harness timeout (the rows that need no wake-up still pass); (3) a stopped task takes a fatal signal at once: `jobctl` `sigterm-waits-for-the-continue` and `sigcont-delivers-the-pending-sigterm` fail (the first version of that check let this mutation pass, so it now waits 40 ms); (4) no orphaned-group rule for `SIGTSTP`/`SIGTTIN`/`SIGTTOU`: three `orphaned-*-is-dropped` rows fail; (5) no `SIGHUP`/`SIGCONT` for a newly orphaned group: `jobctl` hangs in `orphaned-handled-*`; (6) no wake-up of the pipe sleepers before a task sleeps, (11) none at the end of a system call, (12) none at exit: the `pipe` group hangs at `sleeping-reader-and-sleeping-writer-hand-over-pages` (6), fails `write-wakes-the-reader-while-the-writer-keeps-running` (11) and `exit-of-the-last-writer-wakes-a-reader-nobody-else-wakes` (12); (7) `poll` restarts with a later end: `poll-resumes-until-its-original-end` fails; (8) `F_SETPIPE_SZ` ignores buffered data: `proc` `pipe-set-size-shrinks-not-below-data` fails; (10) a sleeping writer ignores that the reader left: the `pipe` group hangs at the first check where a reader leaves a sleeping writer (`blocked-write-returns-the-partial-count-when-the-reader-leaves`, in the earlier build of the group this run used). One mutation survived: (9) `SIGCONT` leaving the stop code non-zero changes nothing, because `wait4` also requires `SIGNAL_STOP_STOPPED`, which the same call clears (an equivalent mutant, as in the pinned kernel). Two of the first versions of these runs showed that the wake-up at exit and at the end of a system call are partly redundant with the parent's `SIGCHLD` wake-up and with each other; the checks now isolate each (the parent ignores `SIGCHLD` and sleeps; the writer keeps running).
* Found by the new groups or by their first run, and fixed: `-ERESTARTSYS` returned to the program after a stop (the restart rules did not run once the task was woken in the same pass; `jobctl` 2 of 70); `Syscall::wait` released the pipe change without waking the sleepers; the heap boot proof failed in every QEMU case once `/init` grew by a few kilobytes (it assumed eight 128 KiB buffers outgrow the free heap, which depends on the initramfs size; it now takes buffers until a chunk has been added, so a physical machine with another initramfs is no longer at risk).
* Not run / not done: no physical hardware; no independent review of any of this (the `exit_to_user` stop and restart path, the wake-up counter, `signal::jobctl`, `poll`); the console `poll` is compared with the source only (the serial transmit queue makes `POLLOUT` timing-dependent on the reference); no SMP; `ppoll`, `select`, `pselect6` and `epoll` are not implemented and stop loudly; no real tty (`SIGTTIN`/`SIGTTOU` for background I/O, controlling terminals); no deadlock report when every task sleeps with no timer; the timing checks of `pipe` and `jobctl` use bounds (a lower one equal to the timeout given, an upper one seconds above it) that hold on this host and would fail on one that is badly overloaded; the `Outcome::Unimplemented` policy (a whole-kernel stop even for a child) is unchanged and awaits the user's decision.


## Process lane closure

Lane `fork` of the process plan (`target/designs/process-lane.md`, WP8; branch `feat/process-fork`, on top of [process creation and exit](#process-creation-and-exit) and [pipes, poll and job control](#pipes-poll-and-job-control)). The lane's behaviour is in those two records and in [scheduler core](#scheduler-core) and [task model](#task-model-and-process-syscalls); this record closes the lane: every oracle group runs on the kernel and matches, the kernel-run suite runs all of them, and the kernel stacks are measured.

### What closes

| Item | State | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `oracle-proc` | the whole `proc` group, 135 checks plus the summary, equal one for one to the lines the pinned Linux printed | `oracle_case!(proc)` in `xtask/src/kernel.rs` (added with the pipe/job-control stage, first matched at `pj-g1`; again below) |
| every other group | `ids` 158, `time` 119, `mm` 122, `fs` 286, `signal` 202, `fork` 165, `jobctl` 71, `pipe` 39 expected lines (checks, summary and info rows), each equal | `oracle-<group>` cases |
| Bare-metal suite | nine groups (`ids time mm fs signal fork proc jobctl pipe`), `UNSUPPORTED` empty, `SUITE complete ok=true groups=9/9 proofs=23/23` in the plain, KVM and two-display cases | `kernel/src/suite.rs` (`GROUPS`), `kernel/src/baremetal.rs`, `xtask/src/kernel/suite.rs` |
| Group teardown | a group's children never outlive it: `reset_group` forgets every scheduler slot, wake-up and pending switch, `abandon_group` kills the whole group from inside whichever task is current at a stop or the time bound, and `userspace::teardown` frees what the group's tasks held (exercised by `baremetal-suite-stops`, `fork`/`proc`/`jobctl`/`pipe` groups that leave zombies, stopped and sleeping children) | [scheduler core](#scheduler-core) "Group lifecycle in the bare-metal suite", commit `6f3d32a` |
| Kernel-stack depth | measured under exec, signal, page-fault, fork and plain system-call paths, below | `ktask::selftest`, `CHECK kstack-usage` |

### Kernel-stack watermarks

Linux's `CONFIG_DEBUG_STACK_USAGE` (`kernel/exit.c` `check_stack_usage`, `stack_not_used`) zeroes a fresh stack and counts the untouched words at exit. The self-test kernel (`qemu-self-test`; nothing of it is in the `freestanding` or `baremetal-suite` profiles) does the same per entry from ring 3. A new task's stack is painted with a non-zero pattern (a zero can be live data, which would make the zero count an under-estimate); every system call and ring-3 exception starts on an empty stack, so the lowest overwritten word when it ends is that path's depth from the top of the stack (the `pt_regs` frame, 168 bytes, included), after which the unused part is painted again below the frame of `measure` with a 512-byte margin. The dead frames of the probe and that margin stay dirty, so every figure is an upper bound with a floor of about 896 bytes (the smallest `between` recorded in `wp8-g2`): the shallow rows overstate by up to that amount, the deep ones (`fork` 15,408) by the same bounded amount. A scan costs (65,536 - depth) / 8 volatile loads, an entry scans twice. `ktask::selftest::{entry_begin, entry_end}` wrap `userspace::syscall` and `userspace::exception`; an entry during which a handler frame was built for its own task (`SignalOutcome::Handled`; a per-stack counter, so another task's frame while this one sleeps does not count) also counts as a signal path; a task's exit and a kernel thread's whole run count as `exit`; what happened between entries (interrupts that hit ring 3, a child's first return) counts as `between`. When `/init` dies the kernel prints one record, which `kstack_usage_ok` in the harness requires in every successful userspace case (some use recorded, at most half of the 64 KiB usable):

`CHECK kstack-usage max_used=<bytes> usable=65536 slot=<pool slot> syscall=<nr or -1> measured=<stretches> exec=<> signal=<> fault=<> fork=<> syscall=<> exception=<> between=<> exit=<>`

Deepest value per path over the `kernel-test` run `wp8-g2` (fix round 1; the figures of the first version, `wp8-t2`, are each 16 bytes lower: the probe now hands a pool-and-counter value to `entry_end`) (bytes; 0 = the path did not run in that case; one run, QEMU TCG; the table is read from `target/kernel-tooling/wp8-g2/cases/*/serial.log`):

| Path | Bytes | Of 65,536 | Case that reached it | What it was doing |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `fork`/`clone`/`clone3` | 15,424 | 23.5 % | `forkbomb`, `oracle-fork` | `copy_process`: the task state block, `Mm::dup` of the address space, descriptor and `fs_struct` copies (the deepest path overall) |
| other system call | 13,544 | 20.7 % | `oracle-fs` (`open`) | pathname walk and `open` on the tmpfs |
| `execve`/`execveat` | 10,736 | 16.4 % | `oracle-fork`, `oracle-proc`, `oracle-jobctl` | the `execve` path (open, ELF plan, new `Mm`, the stack block, the point of no return), in a child |
| signal handler frame built | 7,264 | 11.1 % | `oracle-signal`, `signals` | `get_signal` -> `handle_signal` -> `setup_rt_frame` with the FX image, from a system call or an exception; `rt_sigreturn` entries count here too |
| page fault from ring 3 | 6,144 | 9.4 % | `oracle-mm` (`demand`: 6,000) | the fault handler and the address-space model |
| other exception | 2,872 | 4.4 % | `signals`, `sched-preempt` | faults other than page faults turned into signals |
| exit | 5,656 | 8.6 % | `oracle-pipe` | `do_exit`, `exit_notify`, `release_task`, reparenting, pipe release |
| between entries | 2,040 | 3.1 % | `oracle-fork`, `oracle-proc`, `oracle-jobctl`, `signals` | timer-interrupt preemption on the way back to ring 3 |

The deepest single stretch anywhere in the 43 cases (30 of which print the record; the kernel-thread, non-userspace and suite cases do not) is 15,424 bytes, so the 64 KiB per stack has 4.25 times the depth the exercised paths need. What this does and does not show: it is a measured maximum over the paths these cases take, not a bound (no static stack-size analysis was run, a deeper path may exist in code no case reaches, for instance a `-ENOMEM` unwinding, `E2BIG` argument handling at its limits, or an interrupt arriving at the deepest point of a system call, which the `between` figure does not add to a system call's); the boot-time load of `/init` and the `NMI`/`#DF`/`#MC`/`#DB` IST stacks are not measured; the idle task's stack is the boot stack, also not measured. Without a bound the number is evidence for the pool size in `cpu.rs`, not a proof, and the margin should be re-read when a path is added (`fork` is the one to watch).

### The five design questions

* *Can I use a preexisting crate?* No for the measurement: it is Linux's `check_stack_usage` idea over this kernel's static stack pool, twenty lines of volatile loads and stores, and no crate knows the pool. The lane as a whole used `bitflags` (already vendored) and added no crate after the task-model stage.
* *Can I bundle functionality together?* One measurement for every path (`entry_begin`/`entry_end` around the two ring-3 entry points, one record), not a probe per call site; the existing oracle and fork cases are the workloads, so there is no stack-specific test program, and one `kstack_usage_ok` check covers every successful userspace case.
* *Can I simplify things?* Yes: no per-syscall histogram (the deepest syscall number is recorded, the rest is not), no static analysis, no hook in the production kernels. The classification is by entry kind, so a figure says which kind of entry was deepest, not which function.
* *Is this secure?* The measurement exists only in `qemu-self-test` builds: the `freestanding` kernel of `wp8-g1` has no symbol of it (`nm`), the `freestanding,baremetal-suite` image the user runs is only Clippy-built here and takes the same `cfg`, while the `qemu-self-test,baremetal-suite` kernel of the four suite cases carries the paint and the scans (it prints no record) and so runs a little slower than the user's image would. It writes only a pattern into the dead part of the running task's own pool stack, below the current frame with a 512-byte margin; the kernel has no red zone and an interrupt completes before control returns to the repaint, so no live word is overwritten; the `unsafe` blocks carry their argument. No user pointer is involved. It cannot hide an overflow: the guard page and the end marker are untouched (the paint starts above the marker word).
* *How can I improve this?* Add `-Z emit-stack-sizes` call-graph analysis for a bound; measure the interrupt-on-deepest-syscall case with a forced timer interrupt; add an `ENOMEM` injection run so the unwinding paths are measured; find out which frames make `fork` the deepest path (not investigated here) and shrink them if they are avoidable; measure the IST stacks.

### Evidence

Same workstation, user, toolchain installation and firmware as earlier records; QEMU only (TCG, plus the KVM cases on this machine); not independently attested.

* **`kernel-test` run `wp8-t2`** (output `target/kernel-tooling/wp8-t2/` in the worktree, branch `feat/process-fork` with the stack measurement): 43/43 debug-exit cases matched and 4/4 baremetal-suite cases matched. The oracle summaries printed by the kernel: `ids passed=157`, `time 118`, `mm 121`, `fs 284`, `signal 201`, `fork 164`, `proc 135`, `jobctl 70`, `pipe 38`, each `failed=0` (the expected-line counts above are one more: the summary row).
* **Suite** (`baremetal-suite`, `-kvm`, `-two-displays` serial logs in the same directory): each of the nine groups `result=pass` with `matched == expected == observed` and `mismatches=0`; `SUITE complete ok=true groups=9/9 proofs=23/23` in the plain case.
* **Stack figures**: the table above, from the `CHECK kstack-usage` line of every successful userspace case. A first version (a cumulative per-task paint, `wp8-t1`) gave 15,392 for `fork` and 7,416 for the plain write path, the same within 16 bytes of the per-entry figures (`15,408`, `7,432`).
* **Gate run `wp8-g1`** (`target/gates.sh`, logs in `target/gates-wp8-g1/`, archived under `target/evidence-archive/proc-fork/closure/`, branch `feat/process-fork` at the stack-measurement commit, on `main` 3fe187e), every line `exit=0`: `cargo fmt --all --check`; host 1.88 Clippy and tests (xtask 89, baremetal 10); `cargo +nightly clippy --workspace --all-targets`; nightly tests (kernel 524, xtask 89, baremetal 10, the rest 2); `kernel-test` (43/43 debug-exit cases, 4/4 baremetal-suite cases; its 30 `CHECK kstack-usage` lines equal those of `wp8-t2` in every depth value, only the `measured=` counts differ with timing; both sets are archived as `stack-records/*.tsv`); target Clippy of all four kernel profiles; `xtask check` (97,294 entries, 2,623 workstreams, 56 Markdown files, 23 vendored packages); `xtask status` (26 `in_progress`, 2,597 `not_started`, unchanged by this stage: no routing row is added because no new upstream-prefix code is reachable that an earlier record has not routed).
* **Fix round 1 (review of the stack measurement) and gate run `wp8-g2`** (logs in `target/gates-wp8-g2/`, archived with the `stack-records/wp8-g2.tsv` under `target/evidence-archive/proc-fork/closure/`, branch `feat/process-fork` at the fix commit): `measure`, `entry_begin` and `entry_end` are `#[inline(never)]` (the repaint's argument needs `here` in `measure`'s own frame; the `qemu-self-test` ELF has `measure` at one `push %rax`); `entry_end` hands its scan to `measure` (two scans per entry, was three); the handler-frame counter is per pool stack, so another task's frame during a sleep no longer marks an entry as a signal path; the dirty floor (about 896 bytes) and the scan cost are stated instead of the wrong cost note; `kstack_usage_ok` pins `usable` to 65536 and compares `used <= 65536 / 2` (no multiplication; two new negative cases: `used = 2^63`, `usable=999999`). The refuted part of the review: `Finder::at` already rejects a record that appears twice, so "exactly one" holds. `wp8-g2`: every gate line `exit=0`; `kernel-test` 43/43 debug-exit and 4/4 suite cases; 30 `CHECK kstack-usage` lines; host and nightly test counts as in `wp8-g1` (xtask 89, baremetal 10, kernel 524); deepest `fork` 15,424 (16 bytes above `wp8-t2`: the `Entry` value kept across the call), `syscall` 13,544, `exec` 10,736, `signal` 7,264, `fault` 6,144, `exit` 5,656, `between` 2,040.
* Not run / not done: no physical hardware; no independent review of anything in this lane (the `exit_to_user` stop and restart path, `copy_process` unwinding, the wake-up counter, `signal::jobctl`, `poll`, the three scheduler asm sites); no SMP, no performance measurement; `ppoll`, `select`, `pselect6`, `epoll`, threads (`CLONE_VM`/`CLONE_THREAD`), `vfork`, ptrace, a real tty and pidfd-based waits stop loudly or are absent; **a decision is still open for the user:** any `Outcome::Unimplemented` stops the whole kernel, so one child that reaches an unimplemented system call halts every process (kept because a success-shaped stub is forbidden; killing only the offending task with a loud console line is a behaviour change that needs a decision before any untrusted binary runs); the stack figures are maxima over the exercised paths, not a bound.
